### Switch List

List with switches as secondary actions.
This story demonstrates using switches for toggling options in a settings-like interface.

```tsx
{
  render: function SwitchListRender(args) {
    const [settings, setSettings] = useState({
      wifi: true,
      bluetooth: false,
      notifications: true,
      darkMode: false
    });
    const handleToggle = (setting: keyof typeof settings) => () => {
      setSettings({
        ...settings,
        [setting]: !settings[setting]
      });
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%',
      maxWidth: 360,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper'
    }}>
        <List {...args} subheader={<ListSubheader>Settings</ListSubheader>}>
          <ListItem>
            <ListItemIcon>
              <WifiIcon color={settings.wifi ? 'primary' : 'disabled'} />
            </ListItemIcon>
            <ListItemText primary="Wi-Fi" secondary={settings.wifi ? 'Connected' : 'Disconnected'} />
            <Switch edge="end" checked={settings.wifi} onChange={handleToggle('wifi')} slotProps={{
            input: {
              'aria-labelledby': 'switch-wifi'
            }
          }} />
          </ListItem>
          <ListItem>
            <ListItemIcon>
              <BluetoothIcon color={settings.bluetooth ? 'primary' : 'disabled'} />
            </ListItemIcon>
            <ListItemText primary="Bluetooth" secondary={settings.bluetooth ? 'Enabled' : 'Disabled'} />
            <Switch edge="end" checked={settings.bluetooth} onChange={handleToggle('bluetooth')} slotProps={{
            input: {
              'aria-labelledby': 'switch-bluetooth'
            }
          }} />
          </ListItem>
          <ListItem>
            <ListItemIcon>
              <NotificationsIcon color={settings.notifications ? 'primary' : 'disabled'} />
            </ListItemIcon>
            <ListItemText primary="Notifications" secondary={settings.notifications ? 'Enabled' : 'Disabled'} />
            <Switch edge="end" checked={settings.notifications} onChange={handleToggle('notifications')} slotProps={{
            input: {
              'aria-labelledby': 'switch-notifications'
            }
          }} />
          </ListItem>
          <ListItem>
            <ListItemIcon>{settings.darkMode ? <DarkModeIcon /> : <LightModeIcon />}</ListItemIcon>
            <ListItemText primary="Dark Mode" secondary={settings.darkMode ? 'Enabled' : 'Disabled'} />
            <Switch edge="end" checked={settings.darkMode} onChange={handleToggle('darkMode')} slotProps={{
            input: {
              'aria-labelledby': 'switch-dark-mode'
            }
          }} />
          </ListItem>
        </List>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Contact List

Contact list with different interactions.
This story demonstrates a contact list with availability indicators
and different interaction options.

```tsx
{
  render: function ContactListRender(args) {
    const [selectedContact, setSelectedContact] = useState<number | null>(null);
    const contacts = [{
      id: 1,
      name: 'John Smith',
      status: 'online',
      lastSeen: 'now',
      avatar: 'J'
    }, {
      id: 2,
      name: 'Sarah Johnson',
      status: 'away',
      lastSeen: '5m ago',
      avatar: 'S'
    }, {
      id: 3,
      name: 'Michael Brown',
      status: 'offline',
      lastSeen: '2h ago',
      avatar: 'M'
    }, {
      id: 4,
      name: 'Emma Wilson',
      status: 'online',
      lastSeen: 'now',
      avatar: 'E'
    }, {
      id: 5,
      name: 'David Lee',
      status: 'busy',
      lastSeen: '1h ago',
      avatar: 'D'
    }];
    const getStatusColor = (status: string) => {
      switch (status) {
        case 'online':
          return 'success.main';
        case 'away':
          return 'warning.main';
        case 'busy':
          return 'error.main';
        default:
          return 'grey.500';
      }
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%',
      maxWidth: 360,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper'
    }}>
        <List {...args} subheader={<ListSubheader>Contacts</ListSubheader>}>
          {contacts.map(contact => <ListItem key={contact.id} secondaryAction={<IconButton edge="end" aria-label="call">
                  <PhoneIcon fontSize="small" />
                </IconButton>} disablePadding>
              <ListItemButton selected={selectedContact === contact.id} onClick={() => setSelectedContact(contact.id)}>
                <ListItemAvatar>
                  <Badge overlap="circular" anchorOrigin={{
                vertical: 'bottom',
                horizontal: 'right'
              }} variant="dot" sx={{
                '& .MuiBadge-badge': {
                  backgroundColor: getStatusColor(contact.status),
                  border: '2px solid white',
                  width: 10,
                  height: 10,
                  borderRadius: '50%'
                }
              }}>
                    <Avatar>{contact.avatar}</Avatar>
                  </Badge>
                </ListItemAvatar>
                <ListItemText primary={contact.name} secondary={<Typography variant="caption" component="span">
                      {contact.status === 'online' ? 'Online' : `Last seen ${contact.lastSeen}`}
                    </Typography>} />
              </ListItemButton>
            </ListItem>)}
        </List>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### File Explorer

File explorer using list components.
This story demonstrates how to build a hierarchical file browser interface.

```tsx
{
  render: function FileExplorerRender(args) {
    const [expanded, setExpanded] = useState<string[]>(['documents']);
    const handleToggle = (folder: string) => {
      setExpanded(prev => prev.includes(folder) ? prev.filter(item => item !== folder) : [...prev, folder]);
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%',
      maxWidth: 360,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper'
    }}>
        <List {...args} component="nav" aria-label="file explorer">
          <ListItemButton onClick={() => handleToggle('documents')}>
            <ListItemIcon>
              {expanded.includes('documents') ? <FolderOpenIcon color="primary" /> : <FolderIcon />}
            </ListItemIcon>
            <ListItemText primary="Documents" />
            {expanded.includes('documents') ? <ExpandLess /> : <ExpandMore />}
          </ListItemButton>

          {expanded.includes('documents') && <List component="div" disablePadding>
              <ListItemButton sx={{
            pl: 4
          }} onClick={() => handleToggle('projects')}>
                <ListItemIcon>
                  {expanded.includes('projects') ? <FolderOpenIcon color="primary" /> : <FolderIcon />}
                </ListItemIcon>
                <ListItemText primary="Projects" />
                {expanded.includes('projects') ? <ExpandLess /> : <ExpandMore />}
              </ListItemButton>

              {expanded.includes('projects') && <List component="div" disablePadding>
                  <ListItemButton sx={{
              pl: 8
            }}>
                    <ListItemIcon>
                      <InsertDriveFileIcon />
                    </ListItemIcon>
                    <ListItemText primary="Project_proposal.docx" />
                  </ListItemButton>
                  <ListItemButton sx={{
              pl: 8
            }}>
                    <ListItemIcon>
                      <InsertDriveFileIcon />
                    </ListItemIcon>
                    <ListItemText primary="Budget.xlsx" />
                  </ListItemButton>
                </List>}

              <ListItemButton sx={{
            pl: 4
          }}>
                <ListItemIcon>
                  <InsertDriveFileIcon />
                </ListItemIcon>
                <ListItemText primary="Resume.pdf" />
              </ListItemButton>
            </List>}

          <ListItemButton onClick={() => handleToggle('images')}>
            <ListItemIcon>
              {expanded.includes('images') ? <FolderOpenIcon color="primary" /> : <FolderIcon />}
            </ListItemIcon>
            <ListItemText primary="Images" />
            {expanded.includes('images') ? <ExpandLess /> : <ExpandMore />}
          </ListItemButton>

          {expanded.includes('images') && <List component="div" disablePadding>
              <ListItemButton sx={{
            pl: 4
          }}>
                <ListItemIcon>
                  <InsertDriveFileIcon />
                </ListItemIcon>
                <ListItemText primary="photo1.jpg" />
              </ListItemButton>
              <ListItemButton sx={{
            pl: 4
          }}>
                <ListItemIcon>
                  <InsertDriveFileIcon />
                </ListItemIcon>
                <ListItemText primary="screenshot.png" />
              </ListItemButton>
            </List>}
        </List>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-data-display-pdfviewer.txt">
# Design System/data-display/PdfViewer

A PDF viewer component with navigation, zoom, print, and download capabilities. Built with react-pdf and Material-UI.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `pdfData` | `PdfSource` | Yes |  | PDF source: a URL string, raw `ArrayBuffer`, `Blob`, or an object with a `url` and optional HTTP headers. |
| `currentPage` | `number` | No | 1 | Page number to display on initial render and whenever the value changes externally. |
| `onReachLastPage` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the user scrolls to or navigates past the last page. |


## Examples


### Starting On Page Two

PDF Viewer starting on page 2 of the document.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    pdfData: './sample.pdf',
    currentPage: 2
  }
}
```

### Mobile View

PDF Viewer simulating mobile viewport with responsive controls.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    pdfData: './sample.pdf',
    currentPage: 1
  },
  parameters: {
    viewport: {
      defaultViewport: 'mobile1'
    }
  },
  decorators: [Story => <Box sx={{
    height: '500px',
    width: '375px',
    border: 1,
    borderColor: 'divider',
    margin: 'auto'
  }}>
        <Story />
      </Box>]
}
```

### Playground

Playground — explore all key PdfViewer props.
Features navigation controls, zoom functionality, and pagination.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    pdfData: './sample.pdf',
    currentPage: 1
  }
}
```

### Small Container

PDF Viewer in a smaller container to demonstrate responsive behavior.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    pdfData: './sample.pdf',
    currentPage: 1
  },
  decorators: [Story => <Box sx={{
    height: '400px',
    width: '500px',
    border: 1,
    borderColor: 'divider',
    margin: 'auto'
  }}>
        <Story />
      </Box>]
}
```

### With Last Page Callback

PDF Viewer with callback demonstration when reaching the last page.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    pdfData: './sample.pdf',
    currentPage: 1,
    onReachLastPage: () => {
      onReachLastPageAction('Reached the last page!');
    }
  }
}
```

# Design System/data-display/SvgIcon

| `ref` | `((instance: SVGSVGElement | null) => void) | RefObject<SVGSVGElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |

<file path="design-system-data-display-tooltip.txt">
# Design System/data-display/Tooltip

Tooltips display informative text when users hover over, focus on, or tap an element.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<TooltipSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ popper?: SlotProps<ElementType<PopperProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, TooltipPopperSlotPropsOverrides, TooltipOwnerState>; transition?: SlotProps<...>; tooltip?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; arrow?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |


## Examples


### Custom Delays

Tooltips can have custom delays for showing and hiding.
This is useful for tooltips that should appear more or less quickly.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    justifyContent: 'center',
    gap: 2,
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Tooltip title="No delay (default)">
        <Button variant="outlined">Default</Button>
      </Tooltip>

      <Tooltip title="Quick to show (200ms)" enterDelay={200}>
        <Button variant="outlined">Fast</Button>
      </Tooltip>

      <Tooltip title="Slow to show (1000ms)" enterDelay={1000}>
        <Button variant="outlined">Slow</Button>
      </Tooltip>

      <Tooltip title="Lingers after mouse leaves (1000ms)" leaveDelay={1000}>
        <Button variant="outlined">Lingering</Button>
      </Tooltip>
    </Box>
}
```

### Placements

Tooltips can be placed in different positions around the element.
This example shows all 12 possible placement options.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    width: '100%'
  }}>
      <Grid container justifyContent="center" spacing={1} sx={{
      my: 2
    }}>
        <Grid>
          <Tooltip title="Top Start" placement="top-start">
            <Button sx={{
            width: 120
          }}>top-start</Button>
          </Tooltip>
        </Grid>
        <Grid>
          <Tooltip title="Top" placement="top">
            <Button sx={{
            width: 120
          }}>top</Button>
          </Tooltip>
        </Grid>
        <Grid>
          <Tooltip title="Top End" placement="top-end">
            <Button sx={{
            width: 120
          }}>top-end</Button>
          </Tooltip>
        </Grid>
      </Grid>

      <Grid container justifyContent="center" spacing={8} sx={{
      my: 2
    }}>
        <Grid size={{
        xs: 2
      }} container direction="column" spacing={1} alignItems="flex-end">
          <Grid>
            <Tooltip title="Left Start" placement="left-start">
              <Button sx={{
              width: 120
            }}>left-start</Button>
            </Tooltip>
          </Grid>
          <Grid>
            <Tooltip title="Left" placement="left">
              <Button sx={{
              width: 120
            }}>left</Button>
            </Tooltip>
          </Grid>
          <Grid>
            <Tooltip title="Left End" placement="left-end">
              <Button sx={{
              width: 120
            }}>left-end</Button>
            </Tooltip>
          </Grid>
        </Grid>

        <Grid size={{
        xs: 2
      }} container direction="column" spacing={1} alignItems="flex-start">
          <Grid>
            <Tooltip title="Right Start" placement="right-start">
              <Button sx={{
              width: 120
            }}>right-start</Button>
            </Tooltip>
          </Grid>
          <Grid>
            <Tooltip title="Right" placement="right">
              <Button sx={{
              width: 120
            }}>right</Button>
            </Tooltip>
          </Grid>
          <Grid>
            <Tooltip title="Right End" placement="right-end">
              <Button sx={{
              width: 120
            }}>right-end</Button>
            </Tooltip>
          </Grid>
        </Grid>
      </Grid>

      <Grid container justifyContent="center" spacing={1} sx={{
      my: 2
    }}>
        <Grid>
          <Tooltip title="Bottom Start" placement="bottom-start">
            <Button sx={{
            width: 120
          }}>bottom-start</Button>
          </Tooltip>
        </Grid>
        <Grid>
          <Tooltip title="Bottom" placement="bottom">
            <Button sx={{
            width: 120
          }}>bottom</Button>
          </Tooltip>
        </Grid>
        <Grid>
          <Tooltip title="Bottom End" placement="bottom-end">
            <Button sx={{
            width: 120
          }}>bottom-end</Button>
          </Tooltip>
        </Grid>
      </Grid>
    </Box>
}
```

### Without Arrow

### Feature Highlight

Tooltips can be used in more complex UI patterns like feature tours.
This example shows a tooltip highlighting a new feature.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    justifyContent: 'center',
    p: 4
  }}>
      <Box sx={{
      width: 350,
      height: 200,
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: 'divider',
      borderRadius: 1,
      p: 4,
      position: 'relative',
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column'
    }}>
        <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
          Dashboard
        </Typography>
        <Box sx={{
        display: 'flex',
        justifyContent: 'space-between',
        mb: 2
      }}>
          <Typography variant="body2">My Projects</Typography>
          {(() => {
          const [open, setOpen] = useState(true);
          return <Tooltip open={open} onClose={() => setOpen(false)} title={<Box sx={{
            p: 1
          }}>
                    <Typography variant="subtitle2" sx={{
              fontWeight: 'bold'
            }}>
                      New Feature!
                    </Typography>
                    <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
              mb: 1
            }}>
                      Try our new quick-add feature to create projects faster.
                    </Typography>
                    <Button size="small" variant="contained" color="primary" onClick={() => setOpen(false)} sx={{
              mt: 1
            }}>
                      Got it
                    </Button>
                  </Box>} placement="bottom-end" sx={{
            '& .MuiTooltip-tooltip': {
              backgroundColor: 'success.light',
              color: 'success.contrastText'
            },
            '& .MuiTooltip-arrow': {
              color: 'success.light'
            }
          }}>
                <IconButton color="primary" aria-label="add project" sx={{
              animation: 'pulse 1.5s infinite',
              '@keyframes pulse': {
                '0%': {
                  boxShadow: '0 0 0 0 rgba(25, 118, 210, 0.4)'
                },
                '70%': {
                  boxShadow: '0 0 0 10px rgba(25, 118, 210, 0)'
                },
                '100%': {
                  boxShadow: '0 0 0 0 rgba(25, 118, 210, 0)'
                }
              }
            }}>
                  <AddIcon />
                </IconButton>
              </Tooltip>;
        })()}
        </Box>
        <Box sx={{
        bgcolor: 'action.hover',
        borderRadius: 1,
        p: 4,
        flex: 1
      }}>
          <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary">
            No projects yet
          </Typography>
        </Box>
      </Box>
    </Box>
}
```

### Rich Content

Tooltips can include rich content, not just plain text.
This example shows how to use styled content within tooltips.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    justifyContent: 'center',
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Tooltip title={<Box sx={{
      p: 1,
      maxWidth: 220
    }}>
            <Typography variant="subtitle2" sx={{
        fontWeight: 'bold',
        mb: 1
      }}>
              Rich Tooltip Content
            </Typography>
            <Typography variant="body2">
              Tooltips can contain{' '}
              <Typography component="span" sx={{
          fontWeight: 'bold'
        }}>
                rich text
              </Typography>{' '}
              and multiple paragraphs for more detailed explanations.
            </Typography>
          </Box>}>
        <Button variant="contained" startIcon={<InfoOutlinedIcon />}>
          Hover for details
        </Button>
      </Tooltip>
    </Box>
}
```

### Interactive Tooltip

Tooltips can be interactive, allowing users to hover over the tooltip itself.
This is useful for tooltips that contain links or other interactive elements.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    justifyContent: 'center',
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Tooltip title={<Box sx={{
      p: 1
    }}>
            <Typography variant="body2">This tooltip is interactive. Try hovering over this text!</Typography>
            <Button size="small" sx={{
        mt: 1
      }} variant="outlined" onClick={fn()}>
              Click Me
            </Button>
          </Box>} disableInteractive={false} placement="top">
        <Button variant="contained">Interactive Tooltip</Button>
      </Tooltip>
    </Box>
}
```

### Form Context

In forms, tooltips can provide context for input fields.
This example shows how to use tooltips with form elements.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    maxWidth: 400,
    mx: 'auto',
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Stack spacing={3}>

          mr: 1
        }}>
            Username
          </Typography>

        display: 'flex',
        alignItems: 'center'

          mr: 1
        }}>
```

# Design System/Feedback/confirm-dialog/ConfirmDialog

ConfirmDialog is a specialized dialog used to confirm critical user actions such as deletions.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `open` | `boolean` | Yes |  | Controls whether the dialog is open. |
| `title` | `string` | Yes |  | Dialog heading text. |
| `message` | `string` | Yes |  | Confirmation message shown in the dialog body. |
| `confirmLabel` | `string` | No | Confirm | Label for the primary confirm button. |
| `cancelLabel` | `string` | No | Cancel | Label for the secondary cancel button. |
| `confirmColor` | `"error" "success" "warning" "info" "inherit" "primary" "secondary"` | No | error | MUI color variant applied to the confirm button. |
| `confirmStartIcon` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Optional icon rendered before the confirm button label. |
| `onConfirm` | `() => void` | Yes |  | Callback fired when the user clicks the confirm button. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | Yes |  | Callback fired when the dialog should close (cancel or backdrop click). |
| `loading` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, both action buttons are disabled and the confirm button shows a spinner. |


## Examples


### Controlled

Controlled Confirm Dialog

```tsx
(args: Readonly<ConfirmDialogProps>) => {
  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
  return <>
      <Button color="error" onClick={() => setOpen(true)}>
        Open Confirm Dialog
      </Button>

      <ConfirmDialog {...args} open={open} onClose={() => setOpen(false)} onConfirm={() => setOpen(false)} />
    </>;
}
```

### Loading State

Loading State

```tsx
{
  args: {
    open: true,
    title: 'Deleting...',
    message: 'Please wait while the item is being deleted.',
    confirmLabel: 'Delete',
    cancelLabel: 'Cancel',
    loading: true
  }
}
```

### Playground

### Playground
Interactive playground — tweak title, message, labels, and color via the controls panel.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    open: true,
    title: 'Delete item',
    message: 'Do you really want to delete this item?',
    confirmLabel: 'Delete',
    cancelLabel: 'Cancel',
    confirmColor: 'error',
    onConfirm: fn(),
    onClose: fn()
  }
}
```

### With Icon

With Custom Icon

```tsx
{
  args: {
    open: true,
    title: 'Delete item',
    message: 'Do you really want to delete this item?',
    confirmLabel: 'Delete',
    cancelLabel: 'Cancel',
    confirmStartIcon: <DeleteOutlined />
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/Feedback/confirm-dialog/ConfirmDialogProvider

Renders a singleton ConfirmDialog and exposes a `confirm()` function via the `useConfirm` hook. Already mounted by `MetrisUIProvider` — these stories show it in isolation.

## Examples


### Multiple

Multiple sequential confirmations share the same singleton dialog instance.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ConfirmDialogProvider>
      <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
        <DangerCaller />
        <AsyncCaller />
      </Stack>
    </ConfirmDialogProvider>
}
```

### Async Accept

Async accept callback — the dialog stays open and shows a spinner until the promise resolves.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ConfirmDialogProvider>
      <AsyncCaller />
    </ConfirmDialogProvider>
}
```

### Playground

### Playground

```tsx
      <DangerCaller />
    </ConfirmDialogProvider>
}
```

<file path="design-system-feedback-dialog-dialog.txt">
# Design System/Feedback/dialog/Dialog

Dialogs inform users about a task and can contain critical information, require decisions, or involve multiple tasks.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `draggable` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, the dialog can be dragged around the screen via its title bar. |
| `allowBackdropClick` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, clicking outside the dialog does not close it, enabling multiple dialogs to coexist on screen simultaneously. |
| `size` | `"xs" "sm" "md" "lg"` | No | sm | Predefined width + height preset applied when MUI's `maxWidth` is not set and `fullWidth` is false. Each size pins the paper to a width range and applies a default height range of `40vh`–`60vh`.  | Size | min-width | max-width | | ---- | --------- | --------- | | `xs` | —         | 400 px    | | `sm` | 600 px    | 600 px    | | `md` | 600 px    | 960 px    | | `lg` | 960 px    | 1280 px   |  Consumers can override individual bounds by passing `slotProps={{ paper: { sx: { ... } } }}` — overrides merge per-property because the size defaults are emitted as an `sx` array.  If MUI's `maxWidth` is provided (any value other than `undefined`/`false`) it takes precedence and the size preset is skipped entirely. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<DialogSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<ModalProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, DialogRootSlotPropsOverrides, DialogOwnerState>; backdrop?: SlotProps<...>; container?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; transition?: SlotComponentProps<...> | undefined; paper?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Confirm Destructive

Type-to-confirm destructive action pattern: the user must type "DELETE" exactly
before the Confirm button becomes enabled. On confirmation the dialog closes and
the onConfirmDelete action fires (visible in the Actions panel).

```tsx
_args => {
  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
  const [confirmation, setConfirmation] = useState('');
  const isConfirmed = confirmation === 'DELETE';
  const handleConfirm = () => {
    onConfirmDelete();
    setOpen(false);
    setConfirmation('');
  };
  const handleClose = () => {
    setOpen(false);
    setConfirmation('');
  };
  return <>
      <Button color="error" onClick={() => setOpen(true)}>
        Delete workspace
      </Button>

      <Dialog open={open} onClose={handleClose} maxWidth="xs" fullWidth>
        <DialogTitle title="Delete workspace" subTitle="This action cannot be undone." closable onClose={handleClose} />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
          mb: 2
        }}>
            Type <strong>DELETE</strong> to confirm you want to permanently remove this workspace and all its data.
          </Typography>
          <TextField label="Confirmation" placeholder="Type DELETE" value={confirmation} onChange={e => setConfirmation(e.target.value)} fullWidth size="small" />
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Confirm',
        color: 'error',
        disabled: !isConfirmed,
        onClick: handleConfirm
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Cancel',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Controlled Dialog

Dialog Controlled with Button

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    open,
    handleClose,
    handleOpen
  } = useDialogState(args);
  return <>
      <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open Dialog</Button>
      <Dialog {...args} open={open} onClose={handleClose}>
        <DialogTitle title="Controlled Dialog" />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>This dialog is controlled with a button.</Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Close',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Dialog With Closable Header

Dialog with Closable Header

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    open,
    handleClose,
    handleOpen
  } = useDialogState(args);
  return <>
      <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open Closable Dialog</Button>
      <Dialog {...args} open={open} onClose={handleClose}>
        <DialogTitle title="Closable Dialog" subTitle="This dialog can be closed from the header." closable onClose={handleClose} />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>This dialog has a close button in the header.</Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Confirm',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Draggable Dialog

Draggable Dialog

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    open,
    handleClose,
    handleOpen
  } = useDialogState(args);
  return <>
      <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open Draggable Dialog</Button>
      <Dialog {...args} open={open} onClose={handleClose} draggable={true}>
        <DialogTitle title="Draggable Dialog" draggable />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>
            This dialog can be dragged around! Click and drag the title bar to move it around the screen.
          </Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Confirm',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Cancel',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Extra Small

Extra Small Dialog

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    open,
    handleClose,
    handleOpen
  } = useDialogState(args);
  return <>
      <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open Extra Small Dialog</Button>
      <Dialog {...args} open={open} onClose={handleClose} size="xs">
        <DialogTitle title="Extra Small Dialog" subTitle="This is the extra small dialog." />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>This is the default dialog content.</Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Confirm',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Cancel',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Multiple Dialogs

Multiple Dialogs Support

```tsx
_args => {
  const [dialog1Open, setDialog1Open] = useState(false);
  const [dialog2Open, setDialog2Open] = useState(false);
  const [dialog3Open, setDialog3Open] = useState(false);
  return <>
      <div style={{
      display: 'flex',
      gap: '16px',
      flexWrap: 'wrap'
    }}>
        <Button onClick={() => setDialog1Open(true)}>Open Dialog 1</Button>
        <Button onClick={() => setDialog2Open(true)}>Open Dialog 2</Button>
        <Button onClick={() => setDialog3Open(true)}>Open Dialog 3</Button>
      </div>

      {/* Dialog 1 - Draggable with backdrop click allowed */}
      <Dialog open={dialog1Open} onClose={() => setDialog1Open(false)} draggable={true} allowBackdropClick={true} maxWidth="sm">
        <DialogTitle title="Dialog 1 - Draggable" draggable closable onClose={() => setDialog1Open(false)} />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>
            This is a draggable dialog that allows backdrop clicks. You can open multiple dialogs and drag them around.
          </Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Open Dialog 2',
        onClick: () => setDialog2Open(true)
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Close',
        onClick: () => setDialog1Open(false)
      }} />
      </Dialog>

      {/* Dialog 2 - Draggable with backdrop click allowed */}
      <Dialog open={dialog2Open} onClose={() => setDialog2Open(false)} draggable={true} allowBackdropClick={true} maxWidth="xs">
        <DialogTitle title="Dialog 2 - Smaller" draggable closable onClose={() => setDialog2Open(false)} />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>
            This is a smaller draggable dialog. Notice how you can have multiple dialogs open at once.
          </Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Open Dialog 3',
        onClick: () => setDialog3Open(true)
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Close',
        onClick: () => setDialog2Open(false)
      }} />
      </Dialog>

      {/* Dialog 3 - Regular dialog for comparison */}
      <Dialog open={dialog3Open} onClose={() => setDialog3Open(false)} maxWidth="md">
        <DialogTitle title="Dialog 3 - Regular" closable onClose={() => setDialog3Open(false)} />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>
            This is a regular dialog (non-draggable). It will close when you click the backdrop, unlike the other
            dialogs.
          </Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Close',
        onClick: () => setDialog3Open(false)
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Opened Automatically

Demonstrates the dialog open/close interaction cycle.
Clicking the trigger button opens the dialog; clicking the close action button dismisses it.

```tsx
{
  render: function OpenedAutomaticallyRender() {
    const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
    return <>
        <Button onClick={() => setOpen(true)}>Open Dialog</Button>
        <Dialog open={open} onClose={() => setOpen(false)}>
          <DialogTitle title="Auto-opened Dialog" closable onClose={() => setOpen(false)} />
          <DialogContent>
            <Typography>This dialog was opened automatically by the play function.</Typography>
          </DialogContent>
          <DialogActions primaryAction={{
          label: 'Close',
          onClick: () => setOpen(false)
        }} />
        </Dialog>
      </>;
  },
  play: async ({
    canvasElement
  }) => {
    const canvas = within(canvasElement);
    const trigger = await canvas.findByRole('button', {
      name: /open dialog/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(trigger);
    const dialog = await within(document.body).findByRole('dialog');
    await expect(dialog).toBeInTheDocument();
    const closeBtn = await within(document.body).findByRole('button', {
      name: /close/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(closeBtn);
    await expect(within(document.body).queryByRole('dialog')).toBeNull();
  }
}
```

### Playground

### Playground
Interactive playground — tweak open, fullWidth, maxWidth, and scroll via the controls panel.

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    open,
    handleClose,
    handleOpen
  } = useDialogState(args);
  return <>
      <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open Dialog</Button>
      <Dialog {...args} open={open} onClose={handleClose} fullWidth maxWidth="sm" scroll="paper">
        <DialogTitle title="Title" subTitle="Subtitle" />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>This is the default dialog content.</Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Confirm',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Cancel',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

### Three Actions

Dialog with All Three Action Buttons

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    open,
    handleClose,
    handleOpen
  } = useDialogState(args);
  return <>
      <Button onClick={handleOpen}>Open Dialog</Button>
      <Dialog {...args} open={open} onClose={handleClose} fullWidth>
        <DialogTitle title="More Actions" subTitle="Dialog with Three Actions" />
        <DialogContent>
          <Typography>This dialog shows all three possible action buttons with their default variants.</Typography>
        </DialogContent>
        <DialogActions primaryAction={{
        label: 'Save',
        color: 'primary',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} secondaryAction={{
        label: 'Cancel',
        onClick: handleClose
      }} tertiaryAction={{
        label: 'Delete',
        color: 'error'
      }} />
      </Dialog>
    </>;
}
```

<file path="design-system-feedback-dialog-dialogactions.txt">
# Design System/feedback/Dialog/DialogActions

A container for a Dialog's action buttons with enforced variant patterns.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `primaryAction` | `ActionButtonProps` | Yes |  | Primary action button — rendered rightmost with `variant="contained"`. Required. |
| `secondaryAction` | `ActionButtonProps` | No |  | Secondary action button — rendered in the middle with `variant="outlined"`. |
| `tertiaryAction` | `ActionButtonProps` | No |  | Tertiary action button — rendered leftmost with `variant="text"`. |
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### One Action

DialogActions with one action

```tsx
{
  args: {
    primaryAction: {
      label: 'Primary Action'
    }
  }
}
```

### Playground

### Playground
Interactive playground — configure all three action slots via the controls panel.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    primaryAction: {
      label: 'Confirm'
    },
    secondaryAction: {
      label: 'Cancel'
    },
    tertiaryAction: {
      label: 'Delete',
      color: 'error'
    }
  }
}
```

### Three Actions

DialogActions with three action

```tsx
{
  args: {
    primaryAction: {
      label: 'Primary Action'
    },
    secondaryAction: {
      label: 'Secondary Action'
    },
    tertiaryAction: {
      label: 'Tertiary Action'
    }
  }
}
```

### Two Actions

DialogActions with two actions

```tsx
{
  args: {
    primaryAction: {
      label: 'Primary Action'
    },
    secondaryAction: {
      label: 'Secondary Action'
    }
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/feedback/Dialog/DialogContent

an optional container for displaying the Dialog's content.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `dividers` | `boolean` | No | true | Display top and bottom dividers around the content area. |
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Playground

### Playground
Interactive playground — toggle dividers and edit children via the controls panel.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    children: 'This is a Dialog Content component.',
    dividers: false
  },
  render: args => <DialogContent {...args} sx={{
    alignItems: 'center'
  }}>
      {args.children}
    </DialogContent>
}
```

| `draggable` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, applies a move cursor and disables text selection to indicate the dialog can be dragged via this title bar. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |
| `subTitle` | `string` | No |  | The subtitle text |
| `closable` | `boolean` | No |  | When `true`, renders a close icon button in the top-right corner. Defaults to `true` when `onClose` is provided, unless explicitly set to `false`. |
| `endAdornment` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Optional end adornment on the right of the title bar |


## Examples

```tsx
    title: 'Dialog Title',
    subTitle: 'Dialog subtitle',
    closable: false
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-feedback-modal.txt">
# Design System/Feedback/Modal

The modal component provides a solid foundation for creating dialogs, popovers, lightboxes, or whatever else.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Disable Restore Focus

Modal with scroll lock disabled.
This story demonstrates how to disable the scroll lock behavior of the Modal component.
When scroll lock is disabled, the background content can be scrolled while the modal is open.
This is useful for modals that contain scrollable content.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    disableRestoreFocus: true
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

### Disable Escape Key

Modal with escape key disabled.
This story demonstrates how to disable the escape key behavior of the Modal component.
When escape key is disabled, hitting the escape key will not automatically fire the `onClose` callback.
This is useful for modals that require a confirmation before closing.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    disableEscapeKeyDown: true
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

### Disable Portal

Modal with portal disabled.
This story demonstrates how to disable the portal behavior of the Modal component.
When portal is disabled, the children will be under the DOM hierarchy of the parent component.
This is useful for modals that need to be rendered in the same DOM hierarchy as the parent component.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    disablePortal: true
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

### Disable Auto Focus

Modal with auto focus disabled.
This story demonstrates how to disable the auto focus behavior of the Modal component.
When auto focus is disabled, the modal will not automatically shift focus to itself when it opens.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    disableAutoFocus: true
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

### Nested Modal

Nested Modal
This story demonstrates how to create a nested modal using the Modal component.
When the first modal is open, a button inside it can be used to open a second modal.
This is useful for creating multi-step modals or dialogs.
The second modal can be closed independently of the first one.

```tsx
{
  args: {},
  decorators: [],
  render: args => {
    const NestedModalStory = () => {
      const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
      const [nestedOpen, setNestedOpen] = useState(false);
      const handleOpen = () => setOpen(true);
      const handleOuterClose = () => {
        setNestedOpen(false); // Close nested modal first
        setOpen(false); // Then close outer modal
      };
      const handleNestedOpen = () => setNestedOpen(true);
      const handleNestedClose = () => setNestedOpen(false);
      return <Box>
          <Button variant="text" onClick={handleOpen}>
            Open Modal
          </Button>
          <Modal onClose={handleOuterClose} {...args} open={open}>
            <Box sx={{
            display: 'flex',
            flexDirection: 'column',
            position: 'absolute',
            top: '50%',
            left: '50%',
            transform: 'translate(-50%, -50%)',
            width: 400,
            bgcolor: 'background.paper',
            border: '2px solid #000',
            boxShadow: 24,
            p: 4,
            gap: 2
          }}>
              <Typography variant="h6">First Modal</Typography>
              <Button variant="text" onClick={handleNestedOpen}>
                Open Nested Modal
              </Button>
              <Modal open={nestedOpen} onClose={handleNestedClose}>
                <Box sx={{
                display: 'flex',
                flexDirection: 'column',
                position: 'absolute',
                top: '25%',
                left: '50%',
                transform: 'translate(-50%, -50%)',
                width: 400,
                bgcolor: 'background.paper',
                border: '2px solid #000',
                boxShadow: 24,
                p: 4,
                gap: 2
              }}>
                  <Typography variant="h6">Nested Modal</Typography>
                  <Button variant="text" onClick={handleNestedClose}>
                    Close Nested Modal
                  </Button>
                </Box>
              </Modal>
            </Box>
          </Modal>
        </Box>;
    };
    return <NestedModalStory />;
  }
}
```

### Disable Backdrop

Modal with backdrop disabled.
This story demonstrates how to disable the backdrop of the Modal component.
When the backdrop is disabled, the modal will not have a backdrop overlay.
This is useful for modals that do not require a backdrop.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    hideBackdrop: true
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

### Disable Scroll Lock

Modal with scroll lock disabled.
This story demonstrates how to disable the scroll lock behavior of the Modal component.
When scroll lock is disabled, the background content can be scrolled while the modal is open.
This is useful for modals that contain scrollable content.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    disableScrollLock: true
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

### Opened Automatically

Demonstrates the modal open interaction.
Clicking the trigger button opens the modal and asserts the modal title is visible.

```tsx
{
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />,
  play: async ({
    canvasElement
  }) => {
    const canvas = within(canvasElement);
    const trigger = await canvas.findByRole('button', {
      name: /open modal/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(trigger);
    const modalTitle = await within(document.body).findByText(/modal title/i);
    await expect(modalTitle).toBeInTheDocument();
    const closeBtn = await within(document.body).findByRole('button', {
      name: /close/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(closeBtn);
  }
}
```

### Playground

### Playground
Interactive playground — tweak hideBackdrop, disableScrollLock, and disableEscapeKeyDown via the controls panel.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    hideBackdrop: false,
    disableScrollLock: false,
    disableEscapeKeyDown: false
  },
  render: args => <SimpleModal {...args} />
}
```

<file path="design-system-feedback-snackbar.txt">
# Design System/feedback/Snackbar

A customizable snackbar component built on top of Material-UI.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `severity` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary" "standard"` | No | standard | Visual severity — controls the background color of the snackbar body. |
| `actionButtonProps` | `SnackbarActionButtonProps` | No |  | When provided, renders a labelled action button inside the snackbar body. |
| `onClose` | `((event: Event | SyntheticEvent<any, Event>, reason: SnackbarCloseReason) => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the snackbar closes. Clickaway events are silently ignored — only `'timeout'` and `'escapeKeyDown'` reasons are forwarded. |
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<SnackbarSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<"div", SnackbarRootSlotPropsOverrides, SnackbarOwnerState>; content?: SlotProps<...>; clickAwayListener?: SlotComponentProps<...> | undefined; transition?: SlotComponentProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |


## Examples


### Error

Error example of the Snackbar component
This example demonstrates the Snackbar component with an error severity.

```tsx
{

    severity: 'error',
    message: 'Error snackbar message',

      text: 'Retry',
      onClick: fn()
    }
  },
  render: args => <SnackbarTemplate {...args} />
}
```

### Auto Shown

Demonstrates the snackbar open/dismiss interaction cycle.
Clicking the trigger button opens the snackbar; the message is asserted visible,
then the action button dismisses it.

```tsx
{
  render: function AutoShownRender() {
    const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
    return <>
        <Button onClick={() => setOpen(true)}>Show Snackbar</Button>
        <Snackbar open={open} message="Snackbar triggered by interaction" autoHideDuration={null} actionButtonProps={{
        text: 'Dismiss',
        onClick: () => setOpen(false)
      }} onClose={() => setOpen(false)} />
      </>;
  },
  play: async ({
    canvasElement
  }) => {
    const canvas = within(canvasElement);
    const trigger = await canvas.findByRole('button', {
      name: /show snackbar/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(trigger);
    const message = await within(document.body).findByText(/snackbar triggered by interaction/i);
    await expect(message).toBeInTheDocument();
    const dismissBtn = await within(document.body).findByRole('button', {
      name: /dismiss/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(dismissBtn);
  }
}
```

### Warning

Warning example of the Snackbar component
This example demonstrates the Snackbar component with a warning severity.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    severity: 'warning',
    message: 'Warning snackbar message',
    actionButtonProps: {
      text: 'Dismiss',
      onClick: fn()
    }
  },
  render: args => <SnackbarTemplate {...args} />
}
```

Custom Duration example of the Snackbar component
This example demonstrates the Snackbar component with a custom auto-hide duration.

```tsx
{

    autoHideDuration: 5000,
    onClose: fn()
  },
  render: args => <SnackbarTemplate {...args} />
}
```

### Notification Queue

Shows how to implement a notification queue that displays one snackbar at a time.
Clicking Success, Warning, or Error adds a message to the queue.
Each snackbar auto-dismisses after 2 s and the next one in the queue appears.

```tsx
{
  render: function NotificationQueueRender() {
    const [queue, setQueue] = useState<QueuedNotification[]>([]);
    const [current, setCurrent] = useState<QueuedNotification | null>(null);
    const counterRef = useRef(0);
    const enqueue = useCallback((message: string, severity: NotificationSeverity) => {
      counterRef.current += 1;
      setQueue(prev => [...prev, {
        id: counterRef.current,
        message,
        severity
      }]);
    }, []);
    useEffect(() => {
      if (!current && queue.length > 0) {
        const [next, ...rest] = queue;
        setCurrent(next);
        setQueue(rest);
      }
    }, [current, queue]);
    const handleClose = useCallback(() => setCurrent(null), []);
    return <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      gap: 1,
      flexWrap: 'wrap'
    }}>
        <Button variant="contained" color="success" onClick={() => enqueue('File saved successfully.', 'success')}>
          Success
        </Button>
        <Button variant="contained" color="warning" onClick={() => enqueue('Disk space running low.', 'warning')}>
          Warning
        </Button>
        <Button variant="contained" color="error" onClick={() => enqueue('Export failed — retry?', 'error')}>
          Error
        </Button>

        <Snackbar key={current?.id} open={Boolean(current)} message={current?.message ?? ''} severity={current?.severity ?? 'standard'} autoHideDuration={2000} onClose={handleClose} anchorOrigin={{
        vertical: 'bottom',
        horizontal: 'center'
      }} />
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Bottom Right

```tsx
    message: 'The Snackbar Message',
    anchorOrigin: {
      vertical: 'bottom',
```
```tsx
      horizontal: 'right'
    }
  },
  render: args => <SnackbarTemplate {...args} />
}
```

### Playground

### Playground
Interactive playground — tweak message, severity, anchorOrigin, actionButtonProps, and autoHideDuration via the controls panel.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    message: 'This is a snackbar message',
    severity: 'standard',
    anchorOrigin: {
      vertical: 'bottom',
      horizontal: 'left'
    },
    actionButtonProps: {
      text: 'Action',
      onClick: fn()
    },
    autoHideDuration: null,
    onClose: fn()
  },
  render: (args: SnackbarProps) => <SnackbarTemplate {...args} />
}
```

### Top Left

```tsx
{
  args: {
    message: 'The Snackbar Message',
    anchorOrigin: {
      vertical: 'top',
      horizontal: 'left'
    }
  },
  render: args => <SnackbarTemplate {...args} />
}
```

<file path="design-system-form-form.txt">
# Design System/form/Form


Type-safe form components built on react-hook-form with automatic error handling and Material-UI integration.

Traditional react-hook-form with Material-UI requires repetitive boilerplate:

```tsx
// ❌ Traditional - repetitive
<Controller
  control={control}
  name="email"
  render={({ field, fieldState: { error } }) => (
    <TextField
      {...field}
      label="Email"
      error={!!error}
      helperText={error?.message}
    />
  )}
/>
```

This form system eliminates boilerplate while maintaining type safety:

```tsx
// ✅ Simplified - one line
<Form.TextField control={control} name="email" label="Email" />
```

## Two APIs

### Simplified API 
Single-line components with automatic error handling, labels, and helper text.

**When to use:** Standard forms with typical layouts.

```tsx
<Form.TextField control={control} name="email" label="Email" required />
<Form.Select control={control} name="country" label="Country" options={countries} />
```

### Compositional API (complex layouts)
Full control over structure using Base components with Form.Field.

**When to use:** Custom layouts, inline buttons, complex field arrangements, or pixel-perfect control.

```tsx
<Form.Field
  control={control}
  name="email"
  render={({ field }) => (
    <Box sx={{ display: 'flex', gap: 2 }}>
      <Form.Item sx={{ flex: 1 }}>
        <Form.Label>Email</Form.Label>
        <Form.Control>
          <Form.TextFieldBase {...field} />
        </Form.Control>
        <Form.Message />
      </Form.Item>
      <IconButton><InfoIcon /></IconButton>
    </Box>
  )}
/>
```

## Available Components

### Simplified (Recommended)
- `Form.TextField` - Text input
- `Form.Select` - Dropdown
- `Form.Autocomplete` - Searchable dropdown
- `Form.Checkbox` - Checkbox
- `Form.Switch` - Toggle
- `Form.DatePicker` - Date selection
- `Form.DateTimePicker` - Date and time

### Base (Compositional API)
- `Form.TextFieldBase`
- `Form.SelectBase`
- `Form.AutocompleteBase`
- `Form.CheckboxBase`
- `Form.SwitchBase`
- `Form.DatePickerBase`
- `Form.DateTimePickerBase`

### Layout
- `Form.Provider` - Wraps form with context
- `Form.Root` - Form element
- `Form.Field` - Controller wrapper
- `Form.Item` - Field container
- `Form.Label` - Label with required indicator
- `Form.Control` - Input wrapper
- `Form.Message` - Error message
- `Form.HelperText` - Helper text

## Type Safety

All components are fully typed:

```tsx
type FormData = {
  email: string;
  age: number;
};

const form = useForm<FormData>();

// ✅ Type-safe
<Form.TextField control={form.control} name="email" />

// ❌ TypeScript error
<Form.TextField control={form.control} name="invalid" />
```
        

## Examples


### Required Fields

Required fields with validation.

The `required` prop adds an asterisk (*) to the label.
Combine with Zod schema for automatic validation.

```tsx
() => {
  const schema = z.object({
    firstName: z.string().min(1, 'First name is required'),
    lastName: z.string().min(1, 'Last name is required'),
    email: z.string().email('Invalid email'),
    phone: z.string().optional()
  });
  const form = useForm({
    resolver: zodResolver(schema),
    defaultValues: {
      firstName: '',
      lastName: '',
      email: '',
      phone: ''
    }
  });
  const onSubmit = (data: z.infer<typeof schema>) => {
    onFormSubmit(data);
  };
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        maxWidth: 400
      }}>
          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="firstName" label="First Name" required />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="lastName" label="Last Name" required />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="email" label="Email" type="email" required />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="phone" label="Phone" helperText="Optional field" />

          <Button type="submit" variant="contained" fullWidth>
            Submit
          </Button>
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```

### Simplified Vs Compositional

Simplified vs Compositional API side-by-side.

Left: Simplified API - one line, automatic wrappers
Right: Compositional API - manual structure, full control

Both produce the same visual result, but Compositional gives you
control over every aspect of the layout.

```tsx
() => {
  const simplifiedForm = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      email: ''
    }
  });
  const compositionalForm = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      email: ''
    }
  });
  return <Stack direction="row" spacing={4}>
      <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
      flex: 1
    }}>
        <h3>Simplified API</h3>
        <Form.Provider {...simplifiedForm}>
          <Form.Root>
            <Form.TextField control={simplifiedForm.control} name="email" label="Email" helperText="We'll never share your email" required />
          </Form.Root>
        </Form.Provider>
      </Stack>

      <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
      flex: 1
    }}>
        <h3>Compositional API</h3>
        <Form.Provider {...compositionalForm}>
          <Form.Root>
            <Form.Field control={compositionalForm.control} name="email" render={({
            field
          }) => <Form.Item>
                  <Form.Label required>Email</Form.Label>
                  <Form.Control>
                    <Form.TextFieldBase {...field} />
                  </Form.Control>
                  <Form.HelperText>We&apos;ll never share your email</Form.HelperText>
                  <Form.Message />
                </Form.Item>} />
          </Form.Root>
        </Form.Provider>
      </Stack>
    </Stack>;
}
```

### Basic Form

Basic login form demonstrating the Simplified API.

This is the recommended approach for most forms - single-line components
with automatic error handling, labels, and validation messages.

```tsx
() => {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      email: '',
      password: ''
    }
  });
  const onSubmit = (data: {
    email: string;
    password: string;
  }) => {
    console.log('Form submitted:', data);
    onFormSubmit(data);
  };
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        maxWidth: 400
      }}>
          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="email" label="Email" type="email" required />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="password" label="Password" type="password" required />

          <Button type="submit" variant="contained" fullWidth>
            Login
          </Button>
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```

### With Zod Validation

Form with Zod schema validation.

Shows how to integrate Zod for schema validation with automatic
error messages displayed inline.

```tsx
() => {
  const schema = z.object({
    username: z.string().min(3, 'Username must be at least 3 characters'),
    email: z.string().email('Invalid email address'),
    age: z.number().min(18, 'Must be 18 or older').max(120, 'Invalid age'),
    agreedToTerms: z.boolean().refine(val => val === true, {
      message: 'You must agree to the terms'
    })
  });
  type FormData = z.infer<typeof schema>;
  const form = useForm<FormData>({
    resolver: zodResolver(schema),
    defaultValues: {
      username: '',
      email: '',
      age: 18,
      agreedToTerms: false
    }
  });
  const onSubmit = (data: FormData) => {
    console.log('Form submitted:', data);
    onFormSubmit(data);
  };
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        maxWidth: 400
      }}>
          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="username" label="Username" helperText="Choose a unique username" required />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="email" label="Email" type="email" required />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="age" label="Age" type="number" required />

          <Form.Checkbox control={form.control} name="agreedToTerms" label="I agree to the terms and conditions" required />

          <Button type="submit" variant="contained" fullWidth>
            Submit
          </Button>
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```

### Conditional Fields

Conditional fields based on form values.

Use form.watch() to show/hide fields dynamically based on other field values.

```tsx
() => {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      accountType: '',
      companyName: '',
      taxId: ''
    }
  });
  const {
    watch
  } = form;
  const accountType = watch('accountType');
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        maxWidth: 400
      }}>
          <Form.Select control={form.control} name="accountType" label="Account Type" options={[{
          label: 'Personal',
          value: 'personal'
        }, {
          label: 'Business',
          value: 'business'
        }]} />

          {accountType === 'business' && <>
              <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="companyName" label="Company Name" required />

              <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="taxId" label="Tax ID" required />
            </>}
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```

### All Input Types

All available input types in the Simplified API.

Demonstrates every form component: TextField, Select, Autocomplete,
Switch, Checkbox, DatePicker, and DateTimePicker.

```tsx
() => {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      textField: '',
      select: '',
      autocomplete: null,
      switch: false,
      checkbox: false,
      datePicker: null,
      dateTimePicker: null
    }
  });
  const onSubmit = (data: typeof form.formState.defaultValues) => {
    console.log('Form submitted:', data);
    onFormSubmit(data);
  };
  const countries = [{
    label: 'United States',
    value: 'us'
  }, {
    label: 'Canada',
    value: 'ca'
  }, {
    label: 'United Kingdom',
    value: 'uk'
  }, {
    label: 'Germany',
    value: 'de'
  }];
  const tags = ['React', 'Vue', 'Angular', 'Svelte', 'TypeScript', 'JavaScript'];
  return <DayjsLocalizationProvider>
      <Form.Provider {...form}>
        <Form.Root onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
          <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
          maxWidth: 400
        }}>
            <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="textField" label="Text Field" helperText="Standard text input" />

            <Form.Select control={form.control} name="select" label="Select" options={countries} helperText="Dropdown selection" />

            <Form.Autocomplete control={form.control} name="autocomplete" label="Autocomplete" options={tags} helperText="Searchable dropdown" />

            <Form.Switch control={form.control} name="switch" label="Switch" helperText="Toggle switch" />

            <Form.Checkbox control={form.control} name="checkbox" label="Checkbox" helperText="Checkbox input" />

            <Form.DatePicker control={form.control} name="datePicker" label="Date Picker" helperText="Select a date" />

            <Form.DateTimePicker control={form.control} name="dateTimePicker" label="Date Time Picker" helperText="Select date and time" />

            <Button type="submit" variant="contained" fullWidth>
              Submit
            </Button>
          </Stack>
        </Form.Root>
      </Form.Provider>
    </DayjsLocalizationProvider>;
}
```

### Custom Styling

Custom styling with sx prop and className.

All form components accept Material-UI's sx prop for inline styling
and wrapperClassName for custom CSS classes.

```tsx
() => {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      email: '',
      message: ''
    }
  });
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root>
        <Stack spacing={3} sx={{
        maxWidth: 500
      }}>
          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="email" label="Email" wrapperClassName="custom-field" sx={{
          '& .MuiOutlinedInput-root': {
            borderRadius: 2
          }
        }} />

          <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="message" label="Message" multiline rows={4} sx={{
          '& .MuiOutlinedInput-root': {
            backgroundColor: 'action.hover'
          }
        }} />
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```

### Compositional API

Compositional API for complex layouts.

Use Form.Field with Base components when you need full control over:
- Custom layout arrangements
- Inline buttons or icons
- Pixel-perfect positioning
- Complex field combinations

The Base components (Form.TextFieldBase, etc.) don't include automatic
wrappers, labels, or error messages - you build the structure yourself.

```tsx
() => {
  const form = useForm({
    defaultValues: {
      email: '',
      password: ''
    }
  });
  const onSubmit = (data: {
    email: string;
    password: string;
  }) => {
    console.log('Form submitted:', data);
  };
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        maxWidth: 400
      }}>
          <Form.Field control={form.control} name="email" rules={{
          required: 'Email is required'
        }} render={({
          field
        }) => <Form.Item>
                <Form.Label required>Email</Form.Label>
                <Form.Control>
                  <Form.TextFieldBase {...field} type="email" placeholder="Enter your email" />
                </Form.Control>
                <Form.HelperText>We&apos;ll never share your email</Form.HelperText>
                <Form.Message />
              </Form.Item>} />

          <Form.Field control={form.control} name="password" rules={{
          required: 'Password is required',
          minLength: {
            value: 8,
            message: 'Min 8 characters'
          }
        }} render={({
          field
        }) => <Form.Item>
                <Form.Label required>Password</Form.Label>
                <Form.Control>
                  <Form.TextFieldBase {...field} type="password" placeholder="Enter your password" />
                </Form.Control>
                <Form.Message />
              </Form.Item>} />

          <Button type="submit" variant="contained" fullWidth>
            Login
          </Button>
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```

### Real World Example

Real-world example: Event configuration form.

Shows a practical use case with:
- Conditional fields based on form state
- Number inputs with adornments (%, s)
- Select dropdowns with options
- Form.watch() for reactive visibility

```tsx
() => {
  const schema = z.object({
    isMetricEnabled: z.boolean(),
    enableEvents: z.boolean(),
    warning: z.number().min(0),
    warningPriority: z.string(),
    critical: z.number().min(0),
    criticalPriority: z.string(),
    sendToLogbook: z.boolean(),
    sendToEmail: z.boolean(),
    alarmDelay: z.number().min(0),
    inhibitNotification: z.number().min(0)
  });
  type FormData = z.infer<typeof schema>;
  const form = useForm<FormData>({
    resolver: zodResolver(schema),
    defaultValues: {
      isMetricEnabled: true,
      enableEvents: false,
      warning: 75,
      warningPriority: 'low',
      critical: 90,
      criticalPriority: 'high',
      sendToLogbook: false,
      sendToEmail: false,
      alarmDelay: 0,
      inhibitNotification: 60
    }
  });
  const {
    watch
  } = form;
  const onSubmit = (data: FormData) => {
    console.log('Form submitted:', data);
    onFormSubmit(data);
  };
  const priorityOptions = [{
    label: 'None',
    value: 'none'
  }, {
    label: 'Very Low',
    value: 'very-low'
  }, {
    label: 'Low',
    value: 'low'
  }, {
    label: 'Medium',
    value: 'medium'
  }, {
    label: 'High',
    value: 'high'
  }, {
    label: 'Very High',
    value: 'very-high'
  }];
  if (!watch('isMetricEnabled')) {
    return <Form.Provider {...form}>
        <Form.Root>
          <Form.Switch control={form.control} name="isMetricEnabled" label="Enable Metric" />
        </Form.Root>
      </Form.Provider>;
  }
  return <Form.Provider {...form}>
      <Form.Root onSubmit={form.handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        maxWidth: 500
      }}>
          <Form.Switch control={form.control} name="isMetricEnabled" label="Enable Metric" />

          <Form.Switch control={form.control} name="enableEvents" label="Enable Events" />

          {watch('enableEvents') && <>
              <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="warning" label="Warning Threshold" type="number" slotProps={{
            input: {
              startAdornment: <InputAdornment position="start">↑</InputAdornment>,
              endAdornment: <InputAdornment position="end">%</InputAdornment>
            }
          }} />

              <Form.Select control={form.control} name="warningPriority" label="Warning Priority" options={priorityOptions} />

              <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="critical" label="Critical Threshold" type="number" slotProps={{
            input: {
              startAdornment: <InputAdornment position="start">↑</InputAdornment>,
              endAdornment: <InputAdornment position="end">%</InputAdornment>
            }
          }} />

              <Form.Select control={form.control} name="criticalPriority" label="Critical Priority" options={priorityOptions} />

              <Form.Switch control={form.control} name="sendToLogbook" label="Send to Logbook" />

              <Form.Switch control={form.control} name="sendToEmail" label="Send to Email" />

              <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="alarmDelay" label="Alarm Delay" type="number" slotProps={{
            input: {
              endAdornment: <InputAdornment position="end">s</InputAdornment>
            }
          }} />

              <Form.TextField control={form.control} name="inhibitNotification" label="Inhibit Notification" type="number" slotProps={{
            input: {
              endAdornment: <InputAdornment position="end">s</InputAdornment>
            }
          }} />

              <Button type="submit" variant="contained" fullWidth>
                Save Configuration
              </Button>
            </>}
        </Stack>
      </Form.Root>
    </Form.Provider>;
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-inputs-autocomplete.txt">
# Design System/inputs/Autocomplete

The Autocomplete component is a normal text input enhanced with the input autocomplete feature. It provides a set of interactions that are superior to a normal text input. For example, the Autocomplete component can suggest similar or previous searches as the user types, allow the input of multiple values, and even accept an arbitrary value that is not among the suggested options.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"primary"` | No | 'primary' | Restricted Metris color palette. |
| `label` | `string` | No |  | Label text forwarded to the default TextField `renderInput`. Ignored when a custom `renderInput` is provided. |
| `placeholder` | `string` | No |  | Placeholder text forwarded to the default TextField `renderInput`. Ignored when a custom `renderInput` is provided. |
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No |  | System override and additional CSS styles forwarded to the root element. |
| `renderInput` | `((params: AutocompleteRenderInputParams) => ReactNode)` | No |  | Render the input element. When omitted, a default `TextField` with the `label` and `placeholder` props is rendered. |
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<AutocompleteSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<"div", {}, AutocompleteOwnerState<T, D, F, M, "div">>; chip?: SlotProps<ElementType<Partial<ChipProps<"div">>, keyof IntrinsicElements>, {}, AutocompleteOwnerState<...>>; ... 4 more ...; popupIndicator?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |


## Examples


### Country Select

Demonstrates how to use the Autocomplete component with a list of countries.
The countries are displayed with their flags and phone codes.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    id: 'country-select-demo',
    options: countries,
    getOptionLabel: (option: unknown) => (option as CountryType).label,
    label: 'Select a country'
  },
  render: args => <Autocomplete {...args} renderOption={(props, option) => {
    const {
      key,
      ...otherProps
    } = props;
    return <Box key={key} component="li" {...otherProps} sx={{
      '& > img': {
        mr: 2,
        flexShrink: 0
      }
    }}>
            <img loading="lazy" width="20" srcSet={`https://flagcdn.com/w40/${(option as CountryType).code.toLowerCase()}.png 2x`} src={`https://flagcdn.com/w20/${(option as CountryType).code.toLowerCase()}.png`} alt="" />
            {(option as CountryType).label} ({(option as CountryType).code}) +{(option as CountryType).phone}
          </Box>;
  }} />
}
```

```tsx
    ...Playground.args,

  }
```

```tsx
    }, {
      title: 'Spirited Away',
      year: 2001
    }, {
      title: 'Saving Private Ryan',
      year: 1998
    }, {
      title: 'Once Upon a Time in the West',
      year: 1968
    }, {
      title: 'American History X',
      year: 1998
    }, {
      title: 'Interstellar',
      year: 2014
    }, {
      title: 'Casablanca',
      year: 1942
    }, {
      title: 'City Lights',
      year: 1931
    }, {
      title: 'Psycho',
      year: 1960
    }, {
      title: 'The Green Mile',
      year: 1999
    }, {
      title: 'The Intouchables',
      year: 2011
    }, {
      title: 'Modern Times',
      year: 1936
    }, {
      title: 'Raiders of the Lost Ark',
      year: 1981
    }, {
      title: 'Rear Window',
      year: 1954
    }, {
      title: 'The Pianist',
      year: 2002
    }, {
      title: 'The Departed',
      year: 2006
    }, {
      title: 'Terminator 2: Judgment Day',
      year: 1991
    }, {
      title: 'Back to the Future',
      year: 1985
    }, {
      title: 'Whiplash',
      year: 2014
    }, {
      title: 'Gladiator',
      year: 2000
    }, {
      title: 'Memento',
      year: 2000
    }, {
      title: 'The Prestige',
      year: 2006
    }, {
      title: 'The Lion King',
      year: 1994
    }, {
      title: 'Apocalypse Now',
      year: 1979
    }, {
      title: 'Alien',
      year: 1979
    }, {
      title: 'Sunset Boulevard',
      year: 1950
    }];
    const options = topFilms.map(option => {
      const firstLetter = option.title[0].toUpperCase();
      return {
        firstLetter: /[0-9]/.test(firstLetter) ? '0-9' : firstLetter,
        ...option
      };
    });
    return <Autocomplete label="Grouped options" placeholder="Type to search..." options={options.sort((a, b) => -b.firstLetter.localeCompare(a.firstLetter))} groupBy={option => option.firstLetter} getOptionLabel={option => typeof option === 'string' ? option : option.title} renderOption={(props, option) => <li {...props}>
            <Box sx={{
        display: 'flex',
        alignItems: 'center',
        gap: 1
      }}>{option.title}</Box>
          </li>} renderInput={params => <TextField {...params} label="With categories" />} />;
  }
}
```

### Limit Tags

Shows how to implement auto-completion with limited options.
You can use the <code>limitTags</code> prop to limit the number of displayed options when not focused.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Multiple.args,
    options: countries,
    getOptionLabel: (option: unknown) => (option as CountryType).label,
    limitTags: 2
  }
}
```
