### Remote Search

Simulates an async remote search with debounce and loading indicator.
Typing in the input triggers a mocked 300 ms fetch that returns matching product names.
The loading spinner is visible during the fetch delay.

```tsx
{
  render: function RemoteSearchRender() {
    const [inputValue, setInputValue] = useState('');
    const [options, setOptions] = useState<string[]>([]);
    const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);
    const debounceRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
    const fetchOptions = useCallback((query: string) => {
      if (!query) {
        setOptions([]);
        setLoading(false);
        return;
      }
      setLoading(true);
      if (debounceRef.current) clearTimeout(debounceRef.current);
      debounceRef.current = setTimeout(() => {
        const lower = query.toLowerCase();
        setOptions(remoteSearchProducts.filter(p => p.toLowerCase().includes(lower)));
        setLoading(false);
      }, 300);
    }, []);
    useEffect(() => {
      fetchOptions(inputValue);
      return () => {
        if (debounceRef.current) clearTimeout(debounceRef.current);
      };
    }, [inputValue, fetchOptions]);
    return <Box sx={{
      width: 320
    }}>
        <Autocomplete options={options} loading={loading} inputValue={inputValue} onInputChange={(_e, value) => setInputValue(value)} filterOptions={x => x} label="Search materials" placeholder="Type to search…" noOptionsText={inputValue ? 'No matches' : 'Start typing to search'} renderInput={params => <TextField {...params} label="Search materials" InputProps={{
        ...params.InputProps,
        endAdornment: <>
                    {loading && <CircularProgress color="inherit" size={16} />}
                    {params.InputProps.endAdornment}
                  </>
      }} />} />
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Type And Pick

Demonstrates type-to-filter and keyboard selection in the Autocomplete.
The play function types into the input, asserts the listbox appears, then uses
ArrowDown + Enter to select the first matching option and verifies the value is set.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    options: simpleOptions,
    label: 'Pick an option',
    placeholder: 'Type to search...'
  },
  play: async ({
    canvasElement
  }) => {
    const canvas = within(canvasElement);
    const input = await canvas.findByRole('combobox');
    await userEvent.click(input);
    await userEvent.type(input, 'Op');
    const listbox = await within(document.body).findByRole('listbox');
    await expect(listbox).toBeInTheDocument();
    await userEvent.keyboard('{ArrowDown}');
    await userEvent.keyboard('{Enter}');
    await expect(input).toHaveValue('Option 1');
  }
}
```

### With Checkboxes

Multiple selections with checkboxes in the dropdown.
Combines checkboxes with hidden chips for a clean input field.
Selected items are indicated by checkmarks next to each option.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    multiple: true,
    options: simpleOptions,
    disableCloseOnSelect: true,
    label: 'Select options',
    placeholder: 'Type to search...'
  },
  render: function RenderMultipleWithCheckboxes(args) {
    const [selectedOptions, setSelectedOptions] = useState<string[]>([]);
    return <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: 2
    }}>
        <Autocomplete {...args} value={selectedOptions} onChange={(_event, newValue) => setSelectedOptions(newValue as string[])} renderOption={(props, option, {
        selected
      }) => {
        const {
          key,
          ...otherProps
        } = props;
        return <ListItem key={key} {...otherProps}>
                <ListItemIcon sx={{
            minWidth: 40
          }}>
                  <Checkbox edge="start" checked={selected} tabIndex={-1} disableRipple />
                </ListItemIcon>
                <ListItemText primary={option as string} />
              </ListItem>;
      }} />
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Without Chips

Multiple selections without chips displayed in the input field.
Selected items are shown with checkmarks in the dropdown only.
This is useful for controlled selections where you want to display selected items elsewhere.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    multiple: true,
    options: simpleOptions,
    renderValue: () => null,
    label: 'Select multiple (no chips)',
    placeholder: 'Select items...'
  },
  render: function RenderMultipleWithoutChips(args) {
    const [selectedOptions, setSelectedOptions] = useState<string[]>([]);
    return <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: 2
    }}>
        <Autocomplete {...args} value={selectedOptions} onChange={(_event, newValue) => setSelectedOptions(newValue as string[])} />
        <Box sx={{
        mt: 1
      }}>
          <strong>Selected items:</strong>
          {selectedOptions.length === 0 ? <Box sx={{
          color: 'text.secondary',
          mt: 0.5
        }}>None selected</Box> : <ul style={{
          margin: '4px 0',
          paddingLeft: '20px'
        }}>
              {selectedOptions.map(option => <li key={option}>{option}</li>)}
            </ul>}
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/inputs/Button

A customizable button component built on top of Material-UI.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris brand color palette. Narrows MUI's full color union to the Metris-approved values. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | No | false | If `true`, the button shows a circular loading spinner and is non-interactive. The spinner size adapts to the current `size` prop value. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |

# Design System/inputs/Checkbox

A customizable checkbox component built on top of Material-UI.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"default" "error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris brand color palette. Narrows MUI's full color union to the Metris-approved values. |
| `size` | `"small" "medium" "large"` | No | medium | Size of the checkbox. Extends MUI's standard sizes with `large`, which applies the `MuiCheckbox-large` CSS class for the oversized icon. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLButtonElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLButtonElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<CheckboxSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<SwitchBaseProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, CheckboxRootSlotPropsOverrides, CheckboxOwnerState>; input?: SlotProps<...>; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Checked

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    checked: true
  }
}
```

### Checked Disabled

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    checked: true,
    disabled: true
  }
}
```

### Disabled

```tsx

    ...Playground.args,
    disabled: true
  }
}
```

### Indeterminate

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    indeterminate: true
  }
}
```

### Large

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    size: 'large'
  }
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    color: 'primary',
    checked: false,
    disabled: false,
    indeterminate: false,
    size: 'medium'
  }
}
```

```tsx
    ...Playground.args,
    size: 'small'
  }
```

A checkbox within a `FormControl` with a `FormLabel`.

```tsx
        <FormControlLabel control={<StatefulCheckbox {...args} />} label="Option B" />
```

# Design System/inputs/FormControl

The `FormControl` component is a wrapper for form input elements, designed to be compatible with Material UI, with additional color options (`primary`, `error`, `warning`, `success`).

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris brand color palette. Narrows MUI's full color union to the Metris-approved values. Affects the focused-state highlight of child inputs. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Disabled

FormControl with disabled state

```tsx
args => <FormControl {...args} disabled>
    <FormLabel>Disabled State</FormLabel>
    <RadioGroup>
      <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
      <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
    </RadioGroup>
    <FormHelperText>This form control is disabled</FormHelperText>
  </FormControl>
```

### Error State

FormControl with error state

```tsx
args => <FormControl {...args} error>
    <FormLabel>Error State</FormLabel>
    <RadioGroup>
      <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
      <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
    </RadioGroup>
    <FormHelperText>This is an error message</FormHelperText>
  </FormControl>
```

### Full Width

FormControl with fullWidth

```tsx
args => <FormControl {...args} fullWidth>
    <FormLabel>Full Width FormControl</FormLabel>
    <RadioGroup>
      <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
      <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
    </RadioGroup>
    <FormHelperText>This form control takes up the full width</FormHelperText>
  </FormControl>
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    color: 'primary',
    disabled: false,
    error: false,
    fullWidth: false,
    required: false,
    variant: 'standard'
  },
  render: args => <FormControl {...args}>
      <FormLabel>Form Control Label</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>This is a helper text</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>
}
```

### Required

FormControl with required state

```tsx
args => <FormControl {...args} required>
    <FormLabel>Required FormControl</FormLabel>
    <RadioGroup>
      <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
      <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
    </RadioGroup>
    <FormHelperText>This field is required</FormHelperText>
  </FormControl>
```

### Variants

FormControl with different variants

```tsx
args => <>
    <FormControl {...args} variant="standard">
      <FormLabel>Standard Variant</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>This is the standard variant</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>
    <br />
    <FormControl {...args} variant="outlined">
      <FormLabel>Outlined Variant</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>This is the outlined variant</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>
    <br />
    <FormControl {...args} variant="filled">
      <FormLabel>Filled Variant</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>This is the filled variant</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>
  </>
```
</file>

# Design System/inputs/IconButton

A customizable icon button component built on top of Material-UI and customized to have a outlined variant.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `"default" "outlined"` | No | default | Visual style of the button. When `'outlined'`, the button is rendered using a MUI `Button` with `variant="outlined"` to produce a bordered icon button. |
| `color` | `"default" "error" "success" "warning" "inherit" "primary"` | No | default | Restricted Metris brand color palette. Narrows MUI's full color union to the Metris-approved values. |
| `size` | `"small" "medium" "large" "xSmall"` | No | medium | Size of the button. Extends MUI's standard sizes with `xSmall` (24 px), which uses explicit `sx` sizing rather than a MUI size token. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |

# Design System/inputs/PasswordField

Password Fields let users enter and edit passwords. They are typically used in forms where a password is required.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris color palette. |
| `variant` | `"outlined"` | No | outlined | Input variant. Only `'outlined'` is supported. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<TextFieldSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<FormControlProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, TextFieldRootSlotPropsOverrides, BaseTextFieldProps>; ... 4 more ...; select?: SlotProps<...>; } | { ...; } | { ...; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples

component's full API.

```tsx
    disabled: false,
    error: false,
    size: 'medium',
    color: 'primary'
  }
}
```

This story renders the PasswordField component in an error state.
It includes an error message displayed as helper text.

```tsx
    helperText: 'Error message'
  }
}
```

# Design System/inputs/Radio

The Radio component allows users to select a single option from a set. It is typically used in forms where a single choice is required.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"default" "error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris brand color palette. Narrows MUI's full color union to the Metris-approved values. |
| `size` | `"small" "medium" "large"` | No | medium | Size of the radio button. Extends MUI's standard sizes with `large`, which enlarges the icon to 28 px via an `sx` override on the inner SVG. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLButtonElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLButtonElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<RadioSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<SwitchBaseProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, RadioRootSlotPropsOverrides, RadioOwnerState>; input?: SlotProps<...>; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Colors

Radio with different colors

```tsx
args => <FormControl>
    <FormLabel>Colors</FormLabel>
    <RadioGroup>
      <FormControlLabel value="primary" control={<Radio {...args} color="primary" />} label="Primary" />
      <FormControlLabel value="default" control={<Radio {...args} color="default" />} label="Default" />
      <FormControlLabel value="error" control={<Radio {...args} color="error" />} label="Error" />
      <FormControlLabel value="warning" control={<Radio {...args} color="warning" />} label="Warning" />
      <FormControlLabel value="success" control={<Radio {...args} color="success" />} label="Success" />
    </RadioGroup>
  </FormControl>
```

### Controlled Radio Group

Controlled Radio Group

```tsx
args => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('option1');
  const handleChange = (event: React.ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => {
    setValue(event.target.value);
  };
  return <FormControl>
      <FormLabel>Controlled Radio Group</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup value={value} onChange={handleChange}>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio {...args} />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio {...args} />} label="Option 2" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option3" control={<Radio {...args} />} label="Option 3" />
      </RadioGroup>
    </FormControl>;
}
```

### Disabled

```tsx
    <FormLabel>Disabled</FormLabel>
    <RadioGroup>
      <FormControlLabel value="disabled" control={<Radio {...args} disabled />} label="Disabled" />

    </RadioGroup>
  </FormControl>
```

# Design System/inputs/RadioGroup

The Radio Group allows the user to select one option from a set.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Controlled Radio Group

Controlled RadioGroup with helper text

```tsx
args => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('option1');
  const handleChange = (event: React.ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => {
    setValue(event.target.value);
  };
  return <FormControl>
      <FormLabel>Controlled RadioGroup</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup {...args} value={value} onChange={handleChange}>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option3" control={<Radio />} label="Option 3" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>Selected: {value}</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>;
}
```

### Disabled

Disabeld RadioGroup

```tsx

  return <FormControl disabled={true}>
      <FormLabel>Disabled RadioGroup</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup {...args}>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option3" control={<Radio />} label="Option 3" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>this Radio Group is disabled</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>;
}
```

### Error State

RadioGroup with error state and helper text

```tsx
args => {
  const [value, setValue] = useState('');
  const [error, setError] = useState(true);
  const [helperText, setHelperText] = useState('Please select an option');
  const handleChange = (event: React.ChangeEvent<HTMLInputElement>) => {
    setValue(event.target.value);
    setError(false);
    setHelperText('You selected: ' + event.target.value);
  };
  return <FormControl error={error}>
      <FormLabel>Error State</FormLabel>
      <RadioGroup {...args} value={value} onChange={handleChange}>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option3" control={<Radio />} label="Option 3" />
      </RadioGroup>
      <FormHelperText>{helperText}</FormHelperText>
    </FormControl>;
}
```

# Design System/inputs/Rating

A wrapper around MUI Rating component for collecting user feedback through ratings.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `slots` | `Partial<RatingSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<"span", RatingRootSlotPropsOverrides, RatingOwnerState>; label?: SlotProps<"label", RatingLabelSlotPropsOverrides, RatingOwnerState>; icon?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; decimal?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLSpanElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLSpanElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Disabled

Disabled rating component

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: 3,
    disabled: true
  }
}
```

### Read Only

Read-only rating component

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: 4,
    readOnly: true
  }
}
```

### With Hover Feedback

Rating with hover feedback

```tsx
{
  render: function RatingWithHoverFeedback() {
    const [value, setValue] = useState<number | null>(2);
    const [hover, setHover] = useState(-1);
    const labels: {
      [index: string]: string;
    } = {
      1: 'Useless',
      2: 'Poor',
      3: 'Ok',
      4: 'Good',
      5: 'Excellent'
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: 1,
      alignItems: 'flex-start'
    }}>
        <Typography variant="h6">Rating with Hover Feedback</Typography>
        <Rating value={value} onChange={(_, newValue) => {
        setValue(newValue);
      }} onChangeActive={(_, newHover) => {
        setHover(newHover);
      }} />
        <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
        color: 'text.secondary'
      }}>
          {labels[hover !== -1 ? hover : value || 0] || ''}
        </Typography>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Sizes

Rating with different sizes

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    flexDirection: 'column',
    gap: 2
  }}>
      <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      alignItems: 'center',
      gap: 2
    }}>
        <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
        minWidth: 80
      }}>
          Small:
        </Typography>
        <Rating size="small" defaultValue={3} />
      </Box>
      <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      alignItems: 'center',
      gap: 2
    }}>
        <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
        minWidth: 80
      }}>
          Medium:
        </Typography>
        <Rating size="medium" defaultValue={3} />
      </Box>
      <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      alignItems: 'center',
      gap: 2
    }}>
        <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
        minWidth: 80
      }}>
          Large:
        </Typography>
        <Rating size="large" defaultValue={3} />
      </Box>
    </Box>
}
```

# Design System/inputs/SearchField

A special Text Field that is designed to be used for search functionlity.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"primary"` | No | primary | Restricted color — only `'primary'` is supported. |
| `variant` | `"outlined"` | No | outlined | Input variant. Only `'outlined'` is supported. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<TextFieldSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<FormControlProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, TextFieldRootSlotPropsOverrides, BaseTextFieldProps>; ... 4 more ...; select?: SlotProps<...>; } | { ...; } | { ...; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### All In One

```tsx
{
  args: {},
  render: args => <Stack direction="column" spacing={10}>
      {/* Disabled State */}
      <div>
        <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
          Disabled
        </Typography>
        <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
          <SearchField size="medium" placeholder="Medium size" disabled />
          <SearchField size="small" placeholder="Small size" disabled />
        </Stack>
      </div>

      {/* active State */}
      <div>
        <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
          Active
        </Typography>
        <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
          <SearchField size="medium" placeholder="Medium size" {...args} />
          <SearchField size="small" placeholder="Small size" />
        </Stack>
      </div>

      {/* Focused State */}
      <div>
        <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
          Focused
        </Typography>
        <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
          <SearchField size="medium" placeholder="Medium size" focused />
          <SearchField size="small" placeholder="Small size" focused />
        </Stack>
      </div>

      {/* Error State */}
      <div>
        <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
          Error
        </Typography>
        <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
          <SearchField size="medium" error placeholder="Medium size" />
          <SearchField size="small" error placeholder="Small size" />
        </Stack>
      </div>
    </Stack>
}
```

# Design System/inputs/Select

The Select component allows users to select a value from a dropdown menu. It is typically used in forms where a single choice is required.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"primary"` | No | 'primary' | Restricted color — only `'primary'` is supported. |
| `variant` | `"outlined"` | No | 'outlined' | Input variant. Only `'outlined'` is supported. |
| `ref` | `((instance: unknown) => void) | RefObject<unknown> | null` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Auto Width

AutoWidth story for the Select component.
Demonstrates the Select component with the `autoWidth` property enabled,
where the width of the dropdown adjusts to the content inside.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    color: 'primary',
    autoWidth: true
  },
  render: args => <FormControl sx={{
    minWidth: 130
  }} size="small">
      <InputLabel id="auto-width-select-label" color={args.color}>
        Auto Width
      </InputLabel>
      <Select id="demo-auto-width-select" labelId="auto-width-select-label" label="Auto Width" {...args}>
        <MenuItem value={1}>Option</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value={2}>A longer option</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value={3}>A little more longer option</MenuItem>
      </Select>
    </FormControl>
}
```

```tsx
      </InputLabel>
```

```tsx
        <ListItemText primary="Frontend" sx={{
        fontWeight: 'bold',
        padding: '6px 16px',
        color: 'text.secondary',
        backgroundColor: 'var(--background-paper-2)'
      }} />
        <MenuItem value="javascript">JavaScript</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="typescript">TypeScript</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="html">HTML/CSS</MenuItem>

        fontWeight: 'bold',
        padding: '6px 16px',
        color: 'text.secondary',
        backgroundColor: 'var(--background-paper-2)',
        marginTop: 1
      }} />
        <MenuItem value="java">Java</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="python">Python</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="nodejs">Node.js</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="csharp">C#</MenuItem>

        <ListItemText primary="Mobile" sx={{
        fontWeight: 'bold',
        padding: '6px 16px',
        color: 'text.secondary',
        backgroundColor: 'var(--background-paper-2)',
        marginTop: 1
      }} />
        <MenuItem value="swift">Swift</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="kotlin">Kotlin</MenuItem>
        <MenuItem value="reactnative">React Native</MenuItem>
      </Select>
    </FormControl>
}
```

### Native Select

NativeSelect story for the Select component.
Demonstrates the Select component using a native HTML `<select>` element
with the `native` property enabled.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    color: 'primary',
    native: true
  },
  render: args => <FormControl fullWidth size="small">
      <InputLabel id="default-open-select-label" color={args.color}>
        Native Select
      </InputLabel>
      <Select id="demo-default-open-select" labelId="default-open-select-label" label="Native Select" {...args}>
        <option value="option1">Option 1</option>
        <option value="option2">Option 2</option>
        <option value="option3">Option 3</option>
      </Select>
    </FormControl>
}
```

component's full API.

# Design System/inputs/Slider

Sliders allow users to make selections from a range of values.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"primary"` | No | primary | Restricted color — only `'primary'` is supported. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLSpanElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLSpanElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Medium Size Slider

```tsx
      <Slider {...args} size="medium" disabled />
    </SliderWrapper>
    <SliderWrapper label="Vertical Medium Slider (Enabled)">
      <Slider {...args} size="medium" orientation="vertical" sx={{
      height: 200
    }} />
    </SliderWrapper>

      <Slider {...args} size="medium" orientation="vertical" sx={{
      height: 200
    }} disabled />

  </Stack>
```

# Design System/inputs/SplitButton

A split button component that combines a primary action button with a dropdown menu for additional options. Built on top of Material-UI ButtonGroup.

## Examples


### Custom Item Render

A SplitButton with custom item rendering, showing items with avatar placeholders.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    text: 'Select user',
    items: [{
      text: 'John Doe'
    }, {
      text: 'Jane Smith'
    }, {
      text: 'Bob Johnson'
    }],
    itemRender: item => <div style={{
      display: 'flex',
      alignItems: 'center',
      gap: '8px'
    }}>
        <span style={{
        width: 24,
        height: 24,
        borderRadius: '50%',
        backgroundColor: '#ccc',
        display: 'inline-block'
      }} />
        {item.text}
      </div>
  }
}
```

### With Disabled Items

A SplitButton where some menu items are disabled.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    items: [{
      text: 'Create a merge commit'
    }, {
      text: 'Squash and merge',
      disabled: true
    }, {
      text: 'Rebase and merge'
    }]
  }
}
```

### Disabled

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    disabled: true
  }
}
```

### Text

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    variant: 'text'
  }
}
```

### Outlined

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    variant: 'outlined'
  }
}
```

```tsx
    color: 'primary',
    disabled: false,
    text: ''
  }
```

# Design System/inputs/Switch

Switches toggle the state of a single setting on or off.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris brand color palette. Narrows MUI's full color union to the Metris-approved values. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLButtonElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLButtonElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<SwitchSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<"span", SwitchRootSlotPropsOverrides, SwitchOwnerState>; track?: SlotProps<"span", SwitchTrackSlotPropsOverrides, SwitchOwnerState>; thumb?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; switchBase?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; input?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Checked

Checked story of the Switch component

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    checked: true
  }
}
```

### Checked Disabled

CheckedDisabled story of the Switch component

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Checked.args,
    disabled: true
  }
}
```

### Multiple With Labels

Multiple component with labels

```tsx
{
  render: args => <Box component={'section'} sx={{
    display: 'grid',
    gap: '1rem'
  }}>
      <FormControl>
        <FormLabel>Dark Mode</FormLabel>
        <Switch {...args} color="success" checked={true} />
        <FormHelperText>Enable dark theme</FormHelperText>
      </FormControl>
      <FormControl>
        <FormLabel>Email Notifications</FormLabel>
        <Switch {...args} color="warning" checked={false} />
        <FormHelperText>Receive email updates</FormHelperText>
      </FormControl>
      <FormControl>
        <FormLabel>Auto Updates</FormLabel>
        <Switch {...args} color="error" checked={false} />
        <FormHelperText>Enable automatic updates</FormHelperText>
      </FormControl>
    </Box>,
  args: {}
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    color: 'primary',
    checked: false,
    disabled: false,
    size: 'medium'
  }
}
```

### Small

Small size story of the Switch component

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Checked.args,
    size: 'small'
  }
}
```

### States

Story with different states.

```tsx
{
  render: args => <Box component={'search'} sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    gap: '1rem',
    alignItems: 'center'
  }}>
      <Switch {...args} checked={false} />
      <Switch {...args} checked={true} />
    </Box>,

    color: 'error'
  }
}
```

```tsx
    color: 'primary',
    checked: false
  }
}
```

# Design System/inputs/TextField

Text Fields let users enter and edit text.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `"outlined"` | No | outlined | Input variant. Only `'outlined'` is supported. |
| `color` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | Restricted Metris color palette. |
| `slots` | `Partial<TextFieldSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<FormControlProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, TextFieldRootSlotPropsOverrides, BaseTextFieldProps>; ... 4 more ...; select?: SlotProps<...>; } | { ...; } | { ...; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Max Rows

MaxRows Story

```tsx
{

    multiline: true,
    maxRows: 6,
    value: 'TextField with maximum rows'
  }
}
```

```tsx
    multiline: true,
    rows: 4,
    value: 'Multiline TextField'
  }
}
```

```tsx
    multiline: true,
    minRows: 2,
    value: 'TextField with minimum rows'
  }
}
```

```tsx
}
```

```tsx
  return <TextField label="Password" type={showPassword ? 'text' : 'password'} slotProps={{
    input: {
      endAdornment: <InputAdornment position="end">
              <IconButton onClick={() => setShowPassword(!showPassword)}>

    }
  }} />;
}
```

<file path="design-system-inputs-togglebutton.txt">
# Design System/inputs/ToggleButton

A customizable ToggleButton component built on top of Material-UI.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLButtonElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLButtonElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Large

This story showcases the ToggleButton in a large size.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    size: 'large'
  },
  render: args => <ToggleButton {...args}>
      <FormatAlignLeftIcon />
    </ToggleButton>,
  parameters: {
    docs: {
```

# Design System/layout/AppPlatform/AppCard

### Metris AppCard Stories
These Stories showcase the AppCard for the main App Platform screen

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `title` | `string` | Yes |  | Display name of the application shown on the card. |
| `description` | `string` | Yes |  | Short description of the application (used for search filtering). |
| `icon` | `ComponentType` | No |  | Icon component. Pass an MUI icon from `@mui/icons-material/<IconName>`. For an app-logo image, wrap the `<img>` in a small component (e.g. `const Logo = () => <img src="…" />`). |
| `onClick` | `(app: T) => void` | Yes |  | Callback fired when the card is clicked. Receives the full app props as `T`. |
| `onFavorite` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the favorite star is clicked. |
| `isFavorite` | `boolean` | No | false | Whether the application is currently marked as a favourite. |


## Examples


### Playground

```tsx
{
  args: {
    title: 'App Card Title',
    description: 'This is a description of the app card.',
    icon: RocketLaunchOutlined,
    isFavorite: false
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/layout/AppPlatform/Header

### Metris Header Stories
These Stories showcase the Header for the main App Platform screen

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `scrollDistance` | `number` | Yes |  | Current scroll distance from the top of the scrollable container (in px). Controls header height transition. |
| `serverVersion` | `string` | No |  | Optional server version string to display in the banner area. |
| `searchQuery` | `string` | Yes |  | Current value of the search input field. |
| `onSearch` | `(query: string) => void` | Yes |  | Callback fired when the search input value changes. |
| `onHelpClick` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the help icon button is clicked. |


## Examples


### Playground

```tsx
{
  args: {
    scrollDistance: 0,
    serverVersion: 'Metris 2025.R1.282',
    onHelpClick: () => console.log('Help clicked'),
    onSearch: query => console.log('Search:', query),
    searchQuery: ''
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-layout-appplatform.txt">
# Design System/layout/AppPlatform

A responsive app platform header that changes appearance when scrolling. Includes navigation, user actions, and adapts to different screen sizes.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `serverVersion` | `string` | No |  | Optional server version string displayed in the header banner. |
| `apps` | `AppCardProps<T>[]` | Yes |  | List of application cards to display in the platform grid. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | Yes |  | Whether the application list is in a loading state. |
| `onHelpClick` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the help icon button is clicked. |


## Examples


### Loading

```tsx
{
  args: {
    serverVersion: 'Metris 2025.R1.282',
    apps: [],
    loading: true
  }
}
```

### Playground

Default AppPlatform with mock content. Scroll to see the header behavior change.
The header uses sticky positioning and should remain at the top while scrolling.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    serverVersion: 'Metris 2025.R1.282',
    apps: Array.from({
      length: 10
    }, (_, index) => ({
      title: `Very long Application name ${index + 1}`,
      description: `Description for App ${index + 1}`,
      icon: RocketLaunchOutlined,
      onClick: () => console.log(`App ${index + 1} clicked`),
      onFavorite: () => console.log(`App ${index + 1} favorited`),
      isFavorite: index === 0
    })),
    loading: false,
    onHelpClick: () => console.log('Help clicked')
  }
}
```

### Responsive Dashboard Shell

Full application shell that wires together MetrisAppBar (top header), MetrisAppNavigationDrawer
(left nav with 3 items), and AppPlatform (main content area showing app cards).
Demonstrates how these pieces compose into a complete Metris dashboard layout.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    layout: 'fullscreen'
  },
  render: () => {
    const APP_BAR_HEIGHT = 48;
    const navItems = [{
      name: 'Dashboard',
      icon: <DashboardIcon />,
      route: '/dashboard'
    }, {
      name: 'Reports',
      icon: <AssessmentIcon />,
      route: '/reports'
    }, {
      name: 'Settings',
      icon: <SettingsIcon />,
      route: '/settings'
    }];
    const mockApps = Array.from({
      length: 6
    }, (_, i) => ({
      title: `Metris App ${i + 1}`,
      description: `App ${i + 1} description`,
      icon: RocketLaunchOutlined,
      onClick: () => {},
      onFavorite: () => {},
      isFavorite: i < 2
    }));
    return <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      height: '700px',
      width: '100%'
    }}>
        <MetrisAppBar title="Metris Platform" height={APP_BAR_HEIGHT} />

        <Box sx={{
        display: 'flex',
        flex: 1,
        mt: `${APP_BAR_HEIGHT}px`,
        overflow: 'hidden'
      }}>
          <MetrisAppNavigationDrawer appNavItems={navItems} viewMode="collapsed" onItemClicked={onNavItemClicked}>
            <Box sx={{
            flex: 1,
            p: 2,
            overflow: 'auto'
          }}>
              <Paper sx={{
              p: 2,
              mb: 2
            }}>
                <Typography variant="h6">Welcome to Metris</Typography>
                <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
                color: 'text.secondary'
              }}>
                  Select an application below to get started.
                </Typography>
              </Paper>
              <AppPlatform apps={mockApps} loading={false} serverVersion="Metris 2025.R1" />
            </Box>
          </MetrisAppNavigationDrawer>
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-content-box.txt">
# Design System/layout/content/Box

A wrapper component for rendering with Material UI Box properties and styles.

## Examples


### Card Pattern

Card-like component built with Box

This example demonstrates how to create a card pattern using Box components.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 4,
    bgcolor: 'background.paper',
    borderRadius: 2,
    boxShadow: 3,
    maxWidth: 350
  }}>
      <Typography variant="h5" gutterBottom>
        Card Title
      </Typography>
      <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" sx={{
      mb: 2
    }}>
        This is a card-like component built using Box with nested Typography elements. The Box component is highly
        versatile for creating common UI patterns.
      </Typography>
      <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      justifyContent: 'space-between',
      alignItems: 'center',
      mt: 2,
      pt: 2,
      borderTop: 1,
      borderColor: 'divider'
    }}>
        <Typography variant="caption" color="text.secondary">
          Last updated: 2 days ago
        </Typography>
        <Box sx={{
        bgcolor: 'success.main',
        color: 'success.contrastText',
        px: 1,
        py: 0.5,
        borderRadius: 1
      }}>
          <Typography variant="caption">Active</Typography>
        </Box>
      </Box>
    </Box>
}
```

### Flexbox Layout

Box with flexbox layout

This example demonstrates using Box with flexbox properties to create layouts.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    justifyContent: 'space-between',
    p: 4,
    bgcolor: 'background.paper',
    borderRadius: 1,
    boxShadow: 1
  }}>
      <Box sx={{
      p: 1,
      bgcolor: 'primary.light',
      borderRadius: 1
    }}>Item 1</Box>
      <Box sx={{
      p: 1,
      bgcolor: 'primary.light',
      borderRadius: 1
    }}>Item 2</Box>
      <Box sx={{
      p: 1,
      bgcolor: 'primary.light',
      borderRadius: 1
    }}>Item 3</Box>
    </Box>
}
```

### Grid Layout

Box with grid layout

This example demonstrates using Box with CSS Grid to create responsive layouts.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'grid',
    gridTemplateColumns: {
      xs: '1fr',
      sm: '1fr 1fr',
      md: '1fr 1fr 1fr'
    },
    gap: 2,
    p: 4,
    bgcolor: 'background.paper',
    borderRadius: 1,
    boxShadow: 1
  }}>
      {[1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6].map(item => <Box key={item} sx={{
      p: 4,
      bgcolor: 'primary.main',
      color: 'primary.contrastText',
      borderRadius: 1,
      textAlign: 'center'
    }}>
          Grid Item {item}
        </Box>)}
    </Box>
}
```

### Playground

Interactive playground for the Box component.
Use the controls panel to adjust the `sx` prop and observe layout behaviour.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    sx: {
      display: 'flex',
      justifyContent: 'center',
      alignItems: 'center',
      p: 4,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper',
      borderRadius: 1,
      boxShadow: 1
    },
    children: 'Box content'
  }
}
```

### Responsive Box

Box with responsive styling

This example demonstrates how Box styling adapts to different viewport sizes.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: {
      xs: 1,
      sm: 2,
      md: 4
    },
    bgcolor: {
      xs: 'primary.light',
      md: 'primary.main'
    },
    color: {
      xs: 'text.primary',
      md: 'primary.contrastText'
    },
    fontSize: {
      xs: '0.875rem',
      sm: '1rem',
      md: '1.25rem'
    },
    borderRadius: {
      xs: 1,
      md: 2
    },
    textAlign: 'center',
    boxShadow: {
      xs: 1,
      md: 3
    }
  }}>
      This box adapts to different screen sizes
    </Box>
}
```

### Semantic Elements

Box as a semantic element

This example demonstrates how Box can be rendered as different HTML elements.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    display: 'flex',
    flexDirection: 'column',
    gap: 2
  }}>
      <Box component="section" sx={{
      p: 4,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper',
      borderRadius: 1
    }}>
        <Typography variant="subtitle1">This Box renders as a &lt;section&gt;</Typography>
      </Box>

      <Box component="article" sx={{
      p: 4,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper',
      borderRadius: 1
    }}>
        <Typography variant="subtitle1">This Box renders as an &lt;article&gt;</Typography>
      </Box>

      <Box component="nav" sx={{
      p: 4,
      bgcolor: 'background.paper',
      borderRadius: 1
    }}>
        <Typography variant="subtitle1">This Box renders as a &lt;nav&gt;</Typography>
      </Box>

      <Box component="button" sx={{
      p: 4,
      bgcolor: 'primary.main',
      color: 'primary.contrastText',
      border: 'none',
      borderRadius: 1,
      cursor: 'pointer',
      '&:hover': {
        bgcolor: 'primary.dark'
      }
    }}>
        This Box renders as a &lt;button&gt;
      </Box>
    </Box>
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-navigation-app-navigation-list.txt">
# Design System/layout/navigation/App Navigation List

The App Navigation List component displays a list of navigation items for a specific app.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `isOpen` | `boolean` | Yes |  | Whether the drawer is currently in expanded (open) state. When `false` the list collapses to icon-only mode and item labels are hidden. |
| `items` | `NavigationApp[]` | Yes |  | Ordered array of navigation applications to render as list items. |
| `onItemClicked` | `((route: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks a navigation item. Receives the `route` string of the clicked item. |


## Examples


### Collapsed

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: false,
    items: navigationItems
  }
}
```

### Expanded

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    items: navigationItems
  }
}
```

### Expanded With Click Handler

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Expanded.args,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    items: navigationItems,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```

### With Click Handler

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Collapsed.args,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/layout/navigation/MainNavigationList

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `items` | `NavigationApp[]` | Yes |  | Ordered array of platform applications to display as navigation items. |
| `focusedApp` | `string` | No |  | Name of the currently focused/active application. When provided, the matching item is rendered in its selected state. |
| `onItemClicked` | `(app: NavigationApp) => void` | Yes |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks a navigation item. Receives the full `NavigationApp` object. |


## Examples


### Many Items

```tsx
{
  args: {
    items: [...navigationItems, {
      name: 'Reports',
      route: '/reports',
      icon: <DescriptionIcon />
    }, {
      name: 'Calendar',
      route: '/calendar',
      icon: <CalendarTodayIcon />
    }, {
      name: 'Messages',
      route: '/messages',
      icon: <ChatIcon />
    }, {
      name: 'Help',
      route: '/help',
      icon: <HelpIcon />
    }, {
      name: 'Feedback',
      route: '/feedback',
      icon: <FeedbackIcon />
    }, {
      name: 'Support',
      route: '/support',
      icon: <SupportAgentIcon />
    }],
    onItemClicked: app => console.log('Item clicked:', app.name)
  }
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    items: navigationItems,
    focusedApp: navigationItems[0].name,
    onItemClicked: app => console.log('Item clicked:', app.name)
  }
}
```

### With Focused Item

```tsx
{
  args: {
    items: navigationItems,
    focusedApp: navigationItems[1].name,
    // Analytics is focused
    onItemClicked: app => console.log('Item clicked:', app.name)
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-layout-navigation-metris-app-navigation.txt">
# Design System/layout/navigation/Metris App Navigation

The Metris App Navigation Drawer component. This component is used to display the navigation for specific apps.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `appNavItems` | `NavigationApp[]` | Yes |  | Ordered array of navigation applications to display in the drawer. Each item represents a destination within the current application. |
| `viewMode` | `"expanded" "collapsed" "floating"` | No | 'collapsed' | Controls the display state of the drawer. Overrides the global `useNavigationDrawer` store value when provided. |
| `drawerWidth` | `number` | No | 80 | Width of the navigation drawer in pixels. Overrides the global `useNavigationDrawer` store value when provided. |
| `onItemClicked` | `((route: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks a navigation item. Receives the `route` string of the clicked item. |
| `contentSx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No |  | Additional `sx` styles forwarded to the `MainContent` wrapper surrounding `children`. Use this to customise the content area without touching the drawer itself. |


## Examples


### Expanded

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    viewMode: 'expanded'
  }
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    appNavItems,
    children: <div style={{
      padding: '20px',
      width: '100%'
    }}>Main Content Area</div>,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  },
  render: function Render(args, _) {
    const [viewMode, setViewMode] = useState<DrawerViewState>(args.viewMode ?? 'collapsed');
    useEffect(() => {
      setViewMode(prev => args.viewMode ?? prev);
    }, [args.viewMode]);
    return <MetrisAppNavigationDrawer {...args} viewMode={viewMode} />;
  }
}
```

### With Click Handler

Shows how to handle navigation item clicks with a custom callback function.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Shows how to handle navigation item clicks with a custom callback function.'
      }
    }
  },
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-navigation-metris-nested-app-navigation.txt">
# Design System/layout/navigation/Metris Nested App Navigation

Navigation drawer for app shells whose menu has up to two levels (group → leaf). Items are passed as `appNavSections: NavigationSection[]` — section titles are accepted in the API but not rendered visually yet. Single-expand accordion behavior; group headers never navigate.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `appNavSections` | `NavigationSection[]` | Yes |  | Tree of navigation items grouped into sections. Each section may have an optional `title` (currently not rendered) and a list of items. Each item may declare `children` (one level only) — items with children act as expandable group headers and never navigate, even if a route is provided. |
| `viewMode` | `"expanded" "collapsed" "floating"` | No | 'collapsed' | Controls the display state of the drawer. Overrides the global `useNavigationDrawer` store value when provided. |
| `drawerWidth` | `number` | No | 80 | Width of the navigation drawer in pixels. Overrides the global `useNavigationDrawer` store value when provided. |
| `onItemClicked` | `((route: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback function triggered when a leaf navigation item is clicked. Receives the route string as a parameter. Group headers do not invoke this. |
| `contentSx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No |  | Custom sx styles to apply to the MainContent component. |


## Examples


### With Click Handler

Shows how to handle leaf navigation item clicks with a custom callback.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Shows how to handle leaf navigation item clicks with a custom callback.'
      }
    }
  },
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```

### Expanded

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    viewMode: 'expanded'
  }
}
```

### Sectioned

Two named sections passed via `appNavSections`. Section titles are present
in the data but not visually rendered yet (header rendering deferred to UI/UX).

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    appNavSections: titledSections,
    viewMode: 'expanded'
  }
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    appNavSections: flatSections,
    children: <div style={{
      padding: '20px',
      width: '100%'
    }}>Main Content Area</div>,
    onItemClicked: fn()
  },
  render: function Render(args, _) {
    const [viewMode, setViewMode] = useState<DrawerViewState>(args.viewMode ?? 'collapsed');
    useEffect(() => {
      setViewMode(prev => args.viewMode ?? prev);
    }, [args.viewMode]);
    return <MetrisNestedAppNavigationDrawer {...args} viewMode={viewMode} />;
  }
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-navigation-metrisplatformnavigationdrawer-metrisplatformnavigationdrawer.txt">
# Design System/layout/navigation/MetrisPlatformNavigationDrawer/MetrisPlatformNavigationDrawer

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `drawerWidth` | `number` | Yes |  | Width of the drawer in pixels. Used to size the fixed rail and its header. |
| `viewMode` | `"expanded" "collapsed" "floating"` | Yes |  | Current display state of the drawer: `'collapsed'` (icon rail), `'expanded'` (icon + label rail), or `'floating'` (drawer hidden, replaced by a compact floating header). |
| `activeApps` | `NavigationApp[]` | Yes |  | Ordered list of platform applications to display in the navigation rail. |
| `focusedApp` | `string` | No |  | Name of the currently focused application. When set, the header switches to `DefaultStateHeader` and the matching rail item is highlighted. |
| `loggedInUser` | `{ name: string; email: string; imageUrl?: string | undefined; }` | Yes |  | Authenticated user data forwarded to the `UserAccount` bottom-navigation item (avatar, name, email, initials). |
| `toggleExpand` | `() => void` | Yes |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks the expand/collapse toggle button. |
| `toggleFloatingMode` | `() => void` | Yes |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks the floating-mode toggle button. |
| `onItemClicked` | `(app: NavigationApp) => void` | Yes |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks a rail navigation item. Receives the full `NavigationApp` object. |
| `onAllAppsClicked` | `() => void` | Yes |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks the "All Apps" button. |
| `onThemeToggle` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback invoked when the user activates the theme toggle in the bottom navigation menu. |
| `onLogout` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback invoked when the user selects "Log out" from the user menu. |
| `onOpenAccountPage` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback invoked when the user selects "My Account" from the user menu. |


## Examples


### With Many Apps

```tsx
{
  args: {
    drawerWidth: 80,
    viewMode: 'collapsed',
    activeApps: [...navigationItems, ...Array(8).fill(0).map((_, i) => ({
      name: `App ${i + 1}`,
      route: `/app-${i + 1}`,
      icon: <HomeIcon />,
      children: []
    }))],
    loggedInUser: user
  }
}
```

### Expanded

```tsx
{
  args: {
    drawerWidth: 80,
    viewMode: 'expanded',
    activeApps: navigationItems,
    focusedApp: navigationItems[0].name,
    loggedInUser: user
  }
}
```

### Floating

```tsx
{
  args: {
    drawerWidth: 80,
    viewMode: 'floating',
    activeApps: navigationItems,
    loggedInUser: user
  },
  parameters: {
    backgrounds: {
      default: 'light'
    }
  }
}
```

### Collapsed

```tsx
{
  args: {
    drawerWidth: 80,
    viewMode: 'collapsed',
    activeApps: navigationItems,
    loggedInUser: user
  }
}
```

```tsx
      }
    }
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-layout-navigation-nested-app-navigation-list.txt">
# Design System/layout/navigation/Nested App Navigation List

Navigation list with up to two levels of nesting. Items are grouped into NavigationSection objects; section titles are accepted in the API but not rendered visually yet (deferred until UI/UX provides a header spec). Top-level items can either navigate (leaf) or expand a sub-list (group). Only one group can be expanded at a time (accordion). Group headers never navigate, even if a route is provided.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `isOpen` | `boolean` | Yes |  | Whether the drawer is currently in expanded (open) state. When `false` the list collapses to icon-only mode, labels are hidden, and group toggles are not rendered. |
| `sections` | `NavigationSection[]` | Yes |  | Ordered array of navigation sections. Each section groups related `NestedNavigationApp` items; section titles are accepted in the API but not currently rendered. |
| `onItemClicked` | `((route: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback invoked when the user clicks a leaf navigation item. Receives the `route` string of the clicked item. Group headers never invoke this. |


## Examples


### With Click Handler

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    sections: [{
      items: navigationItems
    }],
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```

### Expanded

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    sections: [{
      items: navigationItems
    }]
  }
}
```

### With Disabled Items

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    sections: [{
      items: [...navigationItems, {
        name: 'Restricted',
        icon: <BlockIcon />,
        route: '/restricted',
        disabled: true
      }]
    }]
  }
}
```

### Collapsed

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: false,
    sections: [{
      items: navigationItems
    }]
  }
}
```

### With Sections

Two named sections. Section titles are passed in the data but **not rendered**
visually — header rendering is deferred until the UI/UX team provides a spec.
The accordion (single-expand) behaviour applies across the whole list, not
per section.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    sections: [{
      title: 'Production',
      items: [{
        name: 'Lines Group',
        icon: <FactoryIcon />,
        children: [{
          name: 'Overview',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/production/overview'
        }, {
          name: 'Lines',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/production/lines'
        }]
      }, {
        name: 'Schedule',
        icon: <EventIcon />,
        route: '/production/schedule'
      }]
    }, {
      title: 'Reports',
      items: [{
        name: 'Sales',
        icon: <AssessmentIcon />,
        children: [{
          name: 'Daily',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/reports/daily'
        }, {
          name: 'Weekly',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/reports/weekly'
        }]
      }, {
        name: 'Users',
        icon: <PersonIcon />,
        route: '/users'
      }]
    }]
  }
}
```

### With Full Path Active Key

Demonstrates full-path `activeKey` matching. Leaves whose `activeKey` starts
with `/` are matched against the full URL pathname using `startsWith`, so a
parent route highlights while the user is on one of its child routes. Set
`window.history.pushState({}, '', '/production/lines/12')` in the browser
console to see "Lines" stay highlighted on a deep route.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    sections: [{
      items: [{
        name: 'Production',
        icon: <FactoryIcon />,
        children: [{
          name: 'Overview',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/production/overview',
          activeKey: '/production/overview'
        }, {
          name: 'Lines',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/production/lines',
          activeKey: '/production/lines'
        }, {
          name: 'Schedule',
          icon: <CircleIcon />,
          route: '/production/schedule',
          activeKey: '/production/schedule'
        }]
      }, {
        name: 'Users',
        icon: <PersonIcon />,
        route: '/users',
        activeKey: '/users'
      }]
    }]
  }
}
```

### Playground

Playground — every prop wired up to Controls. Use this to explore the
component's full API.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    isOpen: true,
    sections: [{
      items: navigationItems
    }],
    onItemClicked: fn()
  }
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-pageheading.txt">
# Design System/layout/PageHeading

Storybook stories for the PageHeading component.
This file contains stories that demonstrate how to use the PageHeading component with different props and configurations.
It includes examples for basic usage, complex headers with multiple actions, and different use cases,
showcasing how to integrate the PageHeading with various child components and action elements.
The stories are designed to provide a clear understanding of how to use the PageHeading in different contexts,
allowing developers to see the component in action and understand its capabilities.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `icon` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Optional icon displayed to the left of the title, typically representing the application or module. |
| `title` | `ReactNode` | No |  | The title text or element displayed as the page heading. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Optional action elements displayed on the right (e.g. buttons, toggles). |
| `onClose` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the close icon button is clicked. |
| `actionSlots` | `ReactNode[]` | No |  | Optional array of elements rendered in a secondary action row below the title. |
| `tabPosition` | `"bottom" "top"` | No | top | Position of the tabs relative to the title row. |
| `iconAriaLabel` | `string` | No | page-icon | Accessible label for the icon element. |
| `showTooltip` | `boolean` | No | false | Whether to show an MUI Tooltip wrapping the title on hover. |
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No |  | Custom styles applied to the root container. |
| `titleSx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Custom styles applied to the title `Typography` element. |
| `scrollButtons` | `boolean | "auto"` | No | auto | Scroll button behaviour forwarded to the `Tabs` component when tabs are present. |


## Examples


### With Top Tabs

```tsx
{
  args: {
    title: 'Settings Dashboard',
    icon: <SettingsOutlinedIcon />,
    actions: <Button startIcon={<RefreshOutlinedIcon />} onClick={() => {}} sx={{
      height: '36px',
      px: 2,
      py: 1
    }}>
        Reload
      </Button>,
    onClose: () => {}
  },
  render: args => <Box sx={{
    minHeight: '500px',
    p: 2
  }}>
      <TabsProvider>
        <PageHeading {...args} />
        <TabContent />
      </TabsProvider>
    </Box>
}
```

### Basic Header

Story demonstrating a basic PageHeading with minimal props.
This shows the simplest use case with just a title and icon.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    title: 'Simple Page Title',
    icon: trendIcon
  }
}
```

### With Bottom Tabs

```tsx
{
  args: {
    title: 'System Configuration Panel',
    icon: <SettingsOutlinedIcon />,
    actions: <Button startIcon={<RefreshOutlinedIcon />} onClick={() => {}} sx={{
      height: '36px',
      px: 2,
      py: 1
    }}>
        Refresh
      </Button>,
    onClose: () => {},
    tabPosition: 'bottom'
  },
  render: args => <Box sx={{
    minHeight: '500px',
    p: 2
  }}>
      <TabsProvider>
        <PageHeading {...args} />
        <TabContent />
      </TabsProvider>
    </Box>
}
```

```tsx

      py: 1
    }}>
        Refresh
      </Button>,

    p: 2
  }}>

      id: 'tab1',
      label: 'Tab 1',
      content: <GeneralTabContent title="Fixed Tab 1" />
    }, {
      id: 'tab2',
      label: 'Tab 2',
      content: <GeneralTabContent title="Fixed Tab 2" />
    }, {
      id: 'tab3',
      label: 'Tab 3',
      content: <GeneralTabContent title="Fixed Tab 3" />
    }]}>
        <PageHeading {...args} />
        <TabContent />
      </TabsProvider>
    </Box>
}
```

```tsx
      py: 1
    }}>
        Configure
      </Button>,

  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-layout-panels-migration-from-layout-templates.txt">
# Design System/layout/Panels/Migration from layout-templates

## Examples


### Migration From Five Panels Template

```tsx
{
  // FivePanelsTemplate layout (from FivePanelsTemplate.tsx):
  //   3 horizontal columns:
  //     left   = panel 1 (25%)
  //     middle = panel 2 on top, with bottom row split into panel 3 | panel 4
  //     right  = panel 5 (~33%)
  render: () => <>
      <StoryFrame title="Migration — Five Panels" description="Replaces `FivePanelsTemplate` with three horizontal columns where the middle column stacks panel 2 above a row of panels 3 and 4." height={400}>
        <Panels.Group>
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="25%" surface>
            {cell('1')}
          </Panels.Item>
          <Panels.Separator />
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="42%">
            <Panels.Group orientation="vertical">
              <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                {cell('2')}
              </Panels.Item>
              <Panels.Separator />
              <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%">
                <Panels.Group>
                  <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                    {cell('3')}
                  </Panels.Item>
                  <Panels.Separator />
                  <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                    {cell('4')}
                  </Panels.Item>
                </Panels.Group>
              </Panels.Item>
            </Panels.Group>
          </Panels.Item>
          <Panels.Separator />
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="33%" surface>
            {cell('5')}
          </Panels.Item>
        </Panels.Group>
      </StoryFrame>
      {codeBlock(`// Before
<FivePanelsTemplate resizable>
  <FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>1</FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>
  <FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>2</FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>
  <FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>3</FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>
  <FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>4</FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>
  <FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>5</FivePanelsTemplate.Panel>
</FivePanelsTemplate>

// After — FivePanelsTemplate is laid out as 3 horizontal columns where the
// middle column stacks panel 2 above a row of panel 3 | panel 4:
//
//   ┌───────┬──────────────────────────┬─────────┐
//   │       │          panel 2         │         │
//   │ panel ├─────────────┬────────────│  panel  │
//   │   1   │   panel 3   │   panel 4  │    5    │
//   └───────┴─────────────┴────────────┴─────────┘
<Panels.Group>
  <Panels.Item defaultSize="25%" surface>1</Panels.Item>
  <Panels.Separator />
  <Panels.Item defaultSize="42%">
    <Panels.Group orientation="vertical">
      <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>2</Panels.Item>
      <Panels.Separator />
      <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%">
        <Panels.Group>
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>3</Panels.Item>
          <Panels.Separator />
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>4</Panels.Item>
        </Panels.Group>
      </Panels.Item>
    </Panels.Group>
  </Panels.Item>
  <Panels.Separator />
  <Panels.Item defaultSize="33%" surface>5</Panels.Item>
</Panels.Group>`)}
    </>
}
```

### Migration From Five Panels Template Both Collapsible

Maps `FivePanelsTemplate.BothCollapsibleDemo` onto the largest migration recipe in this file: the canonical IDE-style layout. Left "Files" and right "Properties" become `Panels.SidebarPanel`s with controlled collapse; the center column nests a vertical group ("Center Panel" on top, a side-by-side "Center Left Subpanel" / "Center Right Subpanel" row below). When both sidebars collapse, the center column expands to fill the viewport.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Maps `FivePanelsTemplate.BothCollapsibleDemo` onto the largest migration recipe in this file: the canonical IDE-style layout. Left "Files" and right "Properties" become `Panels.SidebarPanel`s with controlled collapse; the center column nests a vertical group ("Center Panel" on top, a side-by-side "Center Left Subpanel" / "Center Right Subpanel" row below). When both sidebars collapse, the center column expands to fill the viewport.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: () => <MigrationFromFivePanelsTemplateBothCollapsibleRender />
}
```

### Migration From Four Panels Template Both Collapsible

Maps `FourPanelsTemplate.CollapsibleDemo` onto a four-in-a-row layout that matches this file’s existing `MigrationFromFourPanelsTemplate` shape. Leftmost ("Files") and rightmost ("Properties") become `Panels.SidebarPanel`s with controlled collapse; the two middle panels stay as regular `Panels.Item`s. The original v1 story used the `TwoTopTwoBottom` 2x2 variant with three collapsible quadrants — the recipe here is intentionally the linear-row equivalent so it pairs with the non-collapsible four-panel recipe above.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {

The original v1 story used the `TwoTopTwoBottom` 2x2 variant with three collapsible quadrants — the recipe here is intentionally the linear-row equivalent so it pairs with the non-collapsible four-panel recipe above.'
      }
    }

  render: () => <MigrationFromFourPanelsTemplateBothCollapsibleRender />
}
```

### Migration From Two Panels Template

```tsx
{
  render: () => <>
      <StoryFrame title="Migration — Two Panels" description="Replaces a basic `TwoPanelsTemplate` layout with the equivalent `Panels.Group` + two `Panels.Item` + one `Panels.Separator`." height={300}>
        <Panels.Group>
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
            {cell('panel 1')}
          </Panels.Item>
          <Panels.Separator />
          <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
            {cell('panel 2')}
          </Panels.Item>
        </Panels.Group>
      </StoryFrame>
      {codeBlock(`// Before (1.x)
<TwoPanelsTemplate resizable>
  <TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={50}>…</TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel>
  <TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={50}>…</TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel>
</TwoPanelsTemplate>

// After (2.0)
<Panels.Group>
  <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>…</Panels.Item>
  <Panels.Separator />
  <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>…</Panels.Item>
</Panels.Group>`)}
    </>
}
```

### Migration From Two Panels Template Collapsible Sidebar

Maps `TwoPanelsTemplate.ControlledRightCollapsibleDemo`. A two-panel layout where the right side hosts a sidebar that collapses via a chevron in the card header. Dragging the separator below 5% also fires `onToggleCollapse`.

### Side By Side Two Panels Before After

Renders the deprecated `TwoPanelsTemplate` (1.x) and its `Panels.Group` replacement (2.0) side by side at the same height, so the surface chrome, container padding, and separator gutter can be eyeballed for parity. The new layout reproduces the templates `16 px` container padding via an outer `Box sx={{ p: 2 }}`, the `4 px` rounded paper surfaces via `surface`, and the `16 px` resize-handle gutter via the default `dots` separator variant.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Renders the deprecated `TwoPanelsTemplate` (1.x) and its `Panels.Group` replacement (2.0) side by side at the same height, so the surface chrome, container padding, and separator gutter can be eyeballed for parity. The new layout reproduces the templates `16 px` container padding via an outer `Box sx={{ p: 2 }}`, the `4 px` rounded paper surfaces via `surface`, and the `16 px` resize-handle gutter via the default `dots` separator variant.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: () => <>
      <StoryFrame title="Side-by-side — Two Panels" description="Same content, same fixed height, same outer chrome — compare padding, panel surfaces, and the resize-handle gutter between 1.x and 2.0." height={340}>
        <SideBySide beforeLabel="Before — TwoPanelsTemplate (1.x, deprecated)" afterLabel="After — Panels.Group (2.0)" height={300} beforeContent={<TwoPanelsTemplate resizable persistent={false}>
              <TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={50}>{cell('panel 1')}</TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel>
              <TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={50}>{cell('panel 2')}</TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel>
            </TwoPanelsTemplate>} afterContent={<Box sx={{
        height: '100%',
        p: 2
      }}>
              <Panels.Group>
                <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                  {cell('panel 1')}
                </Panels.Item>
                <Panels.Separator />
                <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                  {cell('panel 2')}
                </Panels.Item>
              </Panels.Group>
            </Box>} />
      </StoryFrame>
      {codeBlock(`// Before (1.x) — Container padding, panel chrome, and the resize-handle
// gutter are all baked into the template.
<TwoPanelsTemplate resizable>
  <TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={50}>panel 1</TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel>
  <TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={50}>panel 2</TwoPanelsTemplate.Panel>
</TwoPanelsTemplate>

// After (2.0) — outer Box reproduces the 16 px container padding;
// "surface" gives each panel the 4 px rounded paper chrome; the default
// "dots" Separator provides the 16 px gutter for the resize seam.
<Box sx={{ height: '100%', p: 2 }}>
  <Panels.Group>
    <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>panel 1</Panels.Item>
    <Panels.Separator />
    <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>panel 2</Panels.Item>
  </Panels.Group>
</Box>`)}
    </>
}
```
