<file path="design-system-layout-panels.txt">
# Design System/layout/Panels


Compound component wrapping `react-resizable-panels` v4 with Metris-friendly defaults.

```tsx
import { Panels } from '@andritzot/metris-web-ui/layout/panels';

<Panels.Group orientation={{ xs: 'vertical', md: 'horizontal' }} autoSaveId="my-layout">
  <Panels.Item defaultSize="30%" minSize="20%" surface>…</Panels.Item>
  <Panels.Separator />
  <Panels.Item collapsible collapsedSize="56px">…</Panels.Item>
</Panels.Group>
```

## Components

- **`Panels.Group`** — wraps v4 `Group`. Accepts a string or breakpoint-map `orientation`, an optional `autoSaveId` shortcut for `localStorage` persistence, and every native v4 prop.
- **`Panels.Item`** — wraps v4 `Panel`. Adds an opt-in `surface` prop for Metris border + paper background chrome.
- **`Panels.Separator`** — wraps v4 `Separator`. Auto-orients its dots icon from the group orientation. Variants: `dots` (default), `spacer` (16 px gutter, `--spacing-4` / `1rem`), `invisible`.
- **`Panels.SidebarPanel`** — composes `Panels.Item` + `ContentSidebar`. Supports both controlled (`isCollapsed` + `onToggleCollapse`) and uncontrolled drag-derived collapse. Default `collapsedSize` is `"56px"` so the chevron rail stays visible.

## Hooks

- `Panels.useGroupRef()` — imperative `Group` ref. Exposes `.getLayout()` and `.setLayout({ [panelId]: percentage })`.
- `Panels.usePanelRef()` — imperative `Panel` ref. Exposes `.collapse()`, `.expand()`, `.resize(value)`, `.isCollapsed()`, `.getSize()`.
- `Panels.useDefaultLayout({ id, storage })` — direct access to the layout-persistence hook (used internally by the `Panels.Group autoSaveId` shortcut). Returns `{ defaultLayout, onLayoutChanged }`.

## Size unit semantics

v4 parses bare numeric size props as **pixels**, not percentages. Always pass explicit unit strings (`"50%"`, `"200px"`, `"20rem"`) so the values mean what they read. The imperative `panelRef.current?.resize(value)` follows the same rule — pass `"40%"` if you mean 40 percent.

## Migrating from `layout/templates`

The fixed-count templates (`TwoPanelsTemplate`, `ThreePanelsTemplate`, `FourPanelsTemplate`, `FivePanelsTemplate`) are deprecated since **1.12** and will be **removed in 2.0**. The migration recipes live at *Layout / Panels / Migration from layout-templates*.

Key behavior differences worth knowing during migration:

- **Surface chrome is opt-in.** Old templates always applied a border + paper background to every panel. New `Panels.Item` is unstyled by default. Pass `surface` to get the same look.
- **ContentSidebar auto-detection is removed.** Old templates silently wrapped any panel whose children contained `ContentSidebar.Card` / `ContentSidebar.Footer` in a `ContentSidebar`. The new component family makes this explicit via `Panels.SidebarPanel`. `Panels.Item` emits a development-only console warning if it detects sidebar children directly inside it.
- **Vertical scrolling is per-panel, not per-group.** v4's `Group` sets its own overflow. To allow vertical scrolling inside a panel, wrap your content in a Box with `overflow: 'auto'` and `height: '100%'`.

## Server rendering & Server Components

For SSR layouts where `localStorage` isn't available (or you want to persist per-user across devices), use `Panels.useDefaultLayout({ id, storage: yourAdapter })` directly and pass `defaultLayout` / `onLayoutChanged` to `Panels.Group`. The `autoSaveId` shortcut uses `localStorage`, which causes a hydration mismatch when the server's first paint disagrees with the persisted client layout — prefer an explicit cookie- or server-state-based `storage` adapter for SSR consumers.

See the upstream docs at the [react-resizable-panels examples](https://react-resizable-panels.vercel.app/examples/persistent-layout) for cookie- and server-state-based adapters.


## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `orientation` | `PanelsResponsiveOrientation` | No | horizontal | Layout orientation. Accepts a string or a breakpoint map. @example orientation="horizontal" orientation={{ xs: 'vertical', md: 'horizontal' }} |
| `autoSaveId` | `string` | No |  | Convenience shortcut for persistent layouts. When set, internally calls `useDefaultLayout({ id: autoSaveId, storage: localStorage })` and forwards the resulting `defaultLayout` / `onLayoutChanged` to the underlying `Group`.  Consumer-provided `defaultLayout` / `onLayoutChange` / `onLayoutChanged` override this shortcut. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | No |  | Disable resize functionality. |
| `defaultLayout` | `Layout` | No |  | Default layout for the Group.  ℹ️ This value allows layouts to be remembered between page reloads.  ⚠️ Slight layout shift may occur when server-rendering panels with percentage-based default sizes. Refer to the documentation for suggestions on how to minimize the impact of this. |
| `disableCursor` | `boolean` | No |  | This library sets custom mouse cursor styles to indicate drag state. Use this prop to disable that behavior for Panels and Separators in this group. |
| `elementRef` | `Ref<HTMLDivElement | null>` | No |  | Ref attached to the root `HTMLDivElement`. |
| `groupRef` | `Ref<GroupImperativeHandle | null>` | No |  | Exposes the following imperative API: - `getLayout(): Layout` - `setLayout(layout: Layout): void`  ℹ️ The `useGroupRef` and `useGroupCallbackRef` hooks are exported for convenience use in TypeScript projects. |
| `onLayoutChange` | `((layout: Layout) => void)` | No |  | Called when the Group's layout is changing.  ⚠️ For layout changes caused by pointer events, this method is called each time the pointer is moved. For most cases, it is recommended to use the `onLayoutChanged` callback instead. |
| `onLayoutChanged` | `((layout: Layout) => void)` | No |  | Called after the Group's layout has  been changed.  ℹ️ For layout changes caused by pointer events, this method is not called until the pointer has been released. This method is recommended when saving layouts to some storage api. |
| `resizeTargetMinimumSize` | `{ coarse: number; fine: number; }` | No |  | Minimum size of the resizable hit target area (either `Separator` or `Panel` edge) This threshold ensures are large enough to avoid mis-clicks.  - Coarse inputs (typically a finger on a touchscreen) have reduced accuracy; to ensure accessibility and ease of use, hit targets should be larger to prevent mis-clicks. - Fine inputs (typically a mouse) can be smaller  ℹ️ [Apple interface guidelines](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/accessibility) suggest `20pt` (`27px`) on desktops and `28pt` (`37px`) for touch devices In practice this seems to be much larger than many of their own applications use though. |


## Examples


### Basics

```tsx
{
  render: () => <StoryFrame title="Basics" description="Two equal-width panels separated by a draggable seam. The foundation of every other story.">
      <Panels.Group>
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
          {cell('left')}
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator />
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
          {cell('right')}
        </Panels.Item>
      </Panels.Group>
    </StoryFrame>
}
```

### Collapsible Panels

Drag the separator past the threshold and the right `Panels.SidebarPanel` snaps to its `collapsedSize` (56 px). The wrapped `ContentSidebarCard` keeps its chevron visible in the collapsed rail, so the panel can always be expanded back.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Drag the separator past the threshold and the right `Panels.SidebarPanel` snaps to its `collapsedSize` (56 px). The wrapped `ContentSidebarCard` keeps its chevron visible in the collapsed rail, so the panel can always be expanded back.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: () => <CollapsiblePanelsRender />
}
```

### Fixed Size Panels

A center panel pinned to 30% via matching `defaultSize`/`minSize`/`maxSize`. The flanking panels redistribute the remaining 70% as the user drags the separators between them.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'A center panel pinned to 30% via matching `defaultSize`/`minSize`/`maxSize`. The flanking panels redistribute the remaining 70% as the user drags the separators between them.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: () => <StoryFrame title="Fixed-Size Panels" description="A center panel pinned to 30% via matching `defaultSize`/`minSize`/`maxSize`. The flanking panels share the remaining 70%.">
      <Panels.Group>
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="35%" surface>
          {cell('flexible')}
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator />
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="30%" minSize="30%" maxSize="30%" surface>
          {cell('fixed 30%')}
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator />
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="35%" surface>
          {cell('flexible')}
        </Panels.Item>
      </Panels.Group>
    </StoryFrame>
}
```

### Nested Groups

Three-column outer layout where the middle column splits into a top region plus a bottom row of two sub-panels. **Drag near the corner where the middle horizontal separator meets the bottom vertical separator** to resize both axes simultaneously — v4 collects hit regions from every group containing the pointer and drives them together. All separators use `variant="invisible"` so their bounding rects do not block v4's multi-group hit detection (per v4's `Ot` filter, an overlapping click-target rect rejects neighboring groups). Mirrors the upstream `react-resizable-panels` nested-groups example.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Three-column outer layout where the middle column splits into a top region plus a bottom row of two sub-panels. **Drag near the corner where the middle horizontal separator meets the bottom vertical separator** to resize both axes simultaneously — v4 collects hit regions from every group containing the pointer and drives them together. All separators use `variant="invisible"` so their bounding rects do not block v4\'s multi-group hit detection (per v4\'s `Ot` filter, an overlapping click-target rect rejects neighboring groups). Mirrors the upstream `react-resizable-panels` nested-groups example.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: () => <StoryFrame title="Nested Groups" description="Outer three-column layout with a vertically nested middle group. Drag near the corner where horizontal and vertical separators meet to resize both axes at once.">
      <Panels.Group>
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="25%" surface>
          {cell('left')}
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator variant="invisible" />
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%">
          <Panels.Group orientation="vertical">
            <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
              {cell('top')}
            </Panels.Item>
            <Panels.Separator variant="invisible" />
            <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%">
              <Panels.Group>
                <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                  {cell('bottom-left')}
                </Panels.Item>
                <Panels.Separator variant="invisible" />
                <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
                  {cell('bottom-right')}
                </Panels.Item>
              </Panels.Group>
            </Panels.Item>
          </Panels.Group>
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator variant="invisible" />
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="25%" surface>
          {cell('right')}
        </Panels.Item>
      </Panels.Group>
    </StoryFrame>
}
```

### Overflow

```tsx

  render: () => <StoryFrame title="Overflow" description="Long content inside a panel scrolls when its container sets `overflow: 'auto'` and a height. Resize the seam to see content reflow.">

          overflow: 'auto',
          height: '100%',

            length: 60
          }).map((_, i) => <Box key={i} sx={{
            py: 1,
            borderBottom: '1px solid',
            borderColor: 'divider'
          }}>
                Line {i + 1}
              </Box>)}
          </Box>
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator />

    </StoryFrame>
}
```

### Separator Disable Drag

Pass `disableDrag` to turn the separator into a static, non-interactive divider: it disables drag-to-resize and double-click-to-reset, defaults the variant to `spacer`, and drops the `not-allowed` cursor. Explicit props still win — set `variant`, `disabled`, or a custom `style.cursor` to override any of the bundled defaults.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Pass `disableDrag` to turn the separator into a static, non-interactive divider: it disables drag-to-resize and double-click-to-reset, defaults the variant to `spacer`, and drops the `not-allowed` cursor. Explicit props still win — set `variant`, `disabled`, or a custom `style.cursor` to override any of the bundled defaults.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: () => <StoryFrame title="Separator — Disable Drag" description="`disableDrag` renders a fixed divider: no drag, no double-click reset, `spacer` variant, normal cursor. The panels keep their `defaultSize` and cannot be resized." height={200}>
      <Panels.Group>
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
          {cell('fixed left')}
        </Panels.Item>
        <Panels.Separator disableDrag />
        <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
          {cell('fixed right')}
        </Panels.Item>
      </Panels.Group>
    </StoryFrame>
}
```

### Separator Variants

```tsx
{
  render: () => <StoryFrame title="Separator Variants" description="`Panels.Separator` ships with `dots`, `spacer`, and `invisible` variants — pick the visual weight that suits your layout." height={560}>
      <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
      height: '100%'
    }}>

        height: 160
      }}>
          <Panels.Group>
            <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
              {cell('spacer variant →')}
            </Panels.Item>
            <Panels.Separator variant="spacer" />
            <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
              {cell('16px gutter')}
            </Panels.Item>
          </Panels.Group>
        </Box>
        <Box sx={{
        height: 160
      }}>
          <Panels.Group>
            <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
              {cell('invisible variant →')}
            </Panels.Item>
            <Panels.Separator variant="invisible" />
            <Panels.Item defaultSize="50%" surface>
              {cell('still draggable on the seam')}
            </Panels.Item>
          </Panels.Group>
        </Box>
      </Stack>
    </StoryFrame>
}
```

### Sidebar Panel

<file path="design-system-layout-stack.txt">
# Design System/layout/Stack

`Stack` is a container component for arranging elements vertically or horizontally.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `border` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<number | (string & {}) | "inset" | "hidden" | "inherit" | "none" | "-moz-initial" | "initial" | "revert" | "revert-layer" | "unset" | "medium" | "thick" | "thin" | "dashed" | ... 198 more ...> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<...>)` | No |  |  |
| `boxShadow` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<number | BoxShadow> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<number | BoxShadow>)` | No |  |  |
| `fontWeight` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<string | (string & {}) | (number & {})> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<string | (string & {}) | (number & {})>)` | No |  |  |
| `zIndex` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<string | (string & {}) | (number & {})> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<string | (string & {}) | (number & {})>)` | No |  |  |
| `alignContent` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | AlignContent> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | AlignContent>)` | No |  |  |
| `alignItems` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | AlignItems> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | AlignItems>)` | No |  |  |
| `alignSelf` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | AlignSelf> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | AlignSelf>)` | No |  |  |
| `bottom` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<Bottom<string | number> | readonly NonNullable<Bottom<string | number>>[]> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<...>)` | No |  |  |
| `boxSizing` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<BoxSizing | readonly NonNullable<BoxSizing>[]> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<...>)` | No |  |  |
| `color` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | Color> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<readonly string[] | Color>)` | No |  |  |
| `columnGap` | `ResponsiveStyleValue<ColumnGap<string | number> | readonly NonNullable<ColumnGap<string | number>>[]> | ((theme: Theme) => ResponsiveStyleValue<...>)` | No |  |  |

## Examples


### Alignment Options

Stack with different alignment options for both horizontal and vertical arrangements.
This demonstrates how to control the alignment of items within a Stack.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Stack spacing={4}>
        <Typography variant="h6">Horizontal Stack with Different Alignments</Typography>

        <Stack spacing={2}>
          <Typography variant="subtitle2" justifyContent="center">
            Start
          </Typography>
          <Paper sx={{
          p: 2,
          bgcolor: 'background.default'
        }}>
            <Stack direction="row" spacing={2} justifyContent="flex-start" sx={{
            bgcolor: 'background.paper',
            p: 1
          }}>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
            </Stack>
          </Paper>

          <Typography variant="subtitle2" justifyContent="center">
            Center
          </Typography>
          <Paper sx={{
          p: 2,
          bgcolor: 'background.default'
        }}>
            <Stack direction="row" spacing={2} justifyContent="center" sx={{
            bgcolor: 'background.paper',
            p: 1
          }}>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
            </Stack>
          </Paper>

          <Typography variant="subtitle2" justifyContent="flex-end">
            End
          </Typography>
          <Paper sx={{
          p: 2,
          bgcolor: 'background.default'
        }}>
            <Stack direction="row" spacing={2} justifyContent="flex-end" sx={{
            bgcolor: 'background.paper',
            p: 1
          }}>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
            </Stack>
          </Paper>

          <Typography variant="subtitle2" justifyContent="space-between">
            Space Between
          </Typography>
          <Paper sx={{
          p: 2,
          bgcolor: 'background.default'
        }}>
            <Stack direction="row" justifyContent="space-between" sx={{
            bgcolor: 'background.paper',
            p: 1
          }}>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
              <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                Instance Slot
              </Button>
            </Stack>
          </Paper>
        </Stack>

        <Typography variant="h6">Vertical Stack with alignItems</Typography>

        <Stack direction="row" spacing={4} sx={{
        overflow: 'auto'
      }}>
          <Stack spacing={2}>
            <Typography variant="subtitle2" alignItems="flex-start">
              Start
            </Typography>
            <Paper sx={{
            p: 2,
            bgcolor: 'background.default',
            width: 200
          }}>
              <Stack spacing={2} alignItems="flex-start" sx={{
              bgcolor: 'background.paper',
              p: 1
            }}>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Short
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Medium length
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Very long button text
                </Button>
              </Stack>
            </Paper>
          </Stack>

          <Stack spacing={2}>
            <Typography variant="subtitle2" alignItems="center">
              Center
            </Typography>
            <Paper sx={{
            p: 2,
            bgcolor: 'background.default',
            width: 200
          }}>
              <Stack spacing={2} alignItems="center" sx={{
              bgcolor: 'background.paper',
              p: 1
            }}>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Short
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Medium length
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Very long button text
                </Button>
              </Stack>
            </Paper>
          </Stack>

          <Stack spacing={2}>
            <Typography variant="subtitle2" alignItems="flex-end">
              End
            </Typography>
            <Paper sx={{
            p: 2,
            bgcolor: 'background.default',
            width: 200
          }}>
              <Stack spacing={2} alignItems="flex-end" sx={{
              bgcolor: 'background.paper',
              p: 1
            }}>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Short
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Medium length
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small">
                  Very long button text
                </Button>
              </Stack>
            </Paper>
          </Stack>

          <Stack spacing={2}>
            <Typography variant="subtitle2" alignItems="stretch">
              Stretch
            </Typography>
            <Paper sx={{
            p: 2,
            bgcolor: 'background.default',
            width: 200
          }}>
              <Stack spacing={2} alignItems="stretch" sx={{
              bgcolor: 'background.paper',
              p: 1
            }}>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small" fullWidth>
                  Short
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small" fullWidth>
                  Medium length
                </Button>
                <Button variant="contained" size="small" fullWidth>
                  Very long button text
                </Button>
              </Stack>
            </Paper>
          </Stack>
        </Stack>
      </Stack>;
  }
}
```

### Card Layout

A card layout with text and actions using Stack with different spacing values.
This shows how to use Stack for card-like components with proper content arrangement.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Card sx={{
      maxWidth: 400
    }}>
        <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
        p: 2
      }}>
          <Typography variant="h6">Notification Settings</Typography>

          <Stack spacing={1}>
            <FormControlLabel control={<Checkbox defaultChecked />} label="Email notifications" />
            <FormControlLabel control={<Checkbox defaultChecked />} label="Push notifications" />
            <FormControlLabel control={<Checkbox />} label="SMS notifications" />
          </Stack>

          <Divider />

          <Stack spacing={1}>
            <Typography variant="subtitle2">Notification Frequency</Typography>
            <RadioGroup defaultValue="daily">
              <Stack spacing={0.5}>
                <FormControlLabel value="realtime" control={<Radio />} label="Real-time" />
                <FormControlLabel value="daily" control={<Radio />} label="Daily digest" />
                <FormControlLabel value="weekly" control={<Radio />} label="Weekly summary" />
              </Stack>
            </RadioGroup>
          </Stack>

          <Button variant="contained">Save Preferences</Button>
        </Stack>
      </Card>;
  }
}
```

### Customize

Customize the spacing and direction of the items using the controls below.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Template />;
  }
}
```

### Direction

Use the `direction` prop to position items horizontally in a row. By default, Stack arranges items vertically in a column.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <RadioGroup>
        <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
          <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
          <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
          <FormControlLabel value="option3" control={<Radio />} label="Option 3" />
        </Stack>
      </RadioGroup>;
  }
}
```

### Form Layout

A form layout using Stack to arrange form fields vertically with consistent spacing.
This demonstrates how Stack simplifies form layouts with proper spacing.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Paper sx={{
      p: 3,
      maxWidth: 500
    }}>
        <Stack spacing={3}>
          <Typography variant="h6">Registration Form</Typography>

          <Stack spacing={2}>
            <TextField label="Full Name" fullWidth />
            <TextField label="Email" type="email" fullWidth />

            <Stack direction="row" spacing={2}>
              <TextField label="Password" type="password" fullWidth />
              <TextField label="Confirm Password" type="password" fullWidth />
            </Stack>

            <FormControlLabel control={<Checkbox />} label="I agree to the terms and conditions" />
          </Stack>

          <Stack direction="row" spacing={2} justifyContent="flex-end">
            <Button variant="outlined">Cancel</Button>
            <Button variant="contained">Submit</Button>
          </Stack>
        </Stack>
      </Paper>;
  }
}
```

### Nested Stacks

Nested stacks for complex layouts with different spacing and alignment needs.
This demonstrates how to build more sophisticated layouts by composing multiple Stack components.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Paper sx={{
      p: 3
    }}>
        <Stack spacing={3}>
          <Typography variant="h6">Project Dashboard</Typography>

          <Stack direction="row" spacing={2} sx={{
          p: 2,
          bgcolor: 'background.default',
          borderRadius: 1
        }}>
            <Stack spacing={1} sx={{
            flex: 3
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1">Project Overview</Typography>
              <Typography variant="body2">
                Lorem ipsum dolor sit amet, consectetur adipiscing elit. Sed do eiusmod tempor incididunt ut labore et
                dolore magna aliqua.
              </Typography>

              <Stack direction="row" spacing={1} sx={{
              mt: 1
            }}>
                <Button size="small" variant="outlined">
                  View Details
                </Button>
                <Button size="small" variant="contained">
                  Edit Project
                </Button>
              </Stack>
            </Stack>

            <Divider orientation="vertical" flexItem />

            <Stack spacing={2} sx={{
            flex: 2
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1">Project Status</Typography>

              <Stack spacing={1}>
                <Alert severity="success">Backend API deployed successfully</Alert>
                <Alert severity="warning">Frontend build in progress</Alert>
                <Alert severity="info">3 pull requests pending review</Alert>
              </Stack>
            </Stack>
          </Stack>

          <Stack direction={{
          xs: 'column',
          md: 'row'
        }} spacing={2} divider={<Divider orientation="vertical" flexItem />}>
            <Stack spacing={1} sx={{
            flex: 1
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1">Team Members</Typography>
              <Stack spacing={0.5}>
                {['John Doe', 'Jane Smith', 'Robert Johnson'].map(name => <Box key={name} sx={{
                p: 1,
                bgcolor: 'background.default',
                borderRadius: 1
              }}>
                    {name}
                  </Box>)}
              </Stack>
            </Stack>

            <Stack spacing={1} sx={{
            flex: 1
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1">Recent Activities</Typography>
              <Stack spacing={0.5}>
                {['Updated project documentation', 'Fixed critical bug in payment module', 'Added new feature for premium users'].map(activity => <Box key={activity} sx={{
                p: 1,
                bgcolor: 'background.default',
                borderRadius: 1
              }}>
                    {activity}
                  </Box>)}
              </Stack>
            </Stack>

            <Stack spacing={1} sx={{
            flex: 1
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1">Upcoming Deadlines</Typography>
              <Stack spacing={0.5}>
                {['User testing - Oct 15', 'Version 2.0 release - Nov 1', 'Client presentation - Nov 10'].map(deadline => <Box key={deadline} sx={{
                p: 1,
                bgcolor: 'background.default',
                borderRadius: 1
              }}>
                      {deadline}
                    </Box>)}
              </Stack>
            </Stack>
          </Stack>
        </Stack>
      </Paper>;
  }
}
```

### Playground

Interactive playground for the Stack component.
Use the controls to adjust direction, spacing, and alignment.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    direction: 'column',
    spacing: 1
  },
  render: args => <RadioGroup>
      <Stack {...args}>
        <FormControlLabel value="option1" control={<Radio />} label="Option 1" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option2" control={<Radio />} label="Option 2" />
        <FormControlLabel value="option3" control={<Radio />} label="Option 3" />
      </Stack>
    </RadioGroup>
}
```

### Responsive Layout

A responsive layout that changes direction based on screen size.
On small screens, elements stack vertically, while on larger screens they arrange horizontally.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ResponsiveLayoutStory />
}
```

# Design System/layout/template/FivePanelsTemplate

⚠️ **Deprecated since 1.12 — removal planned for 2.0.** Use [`Panels`](?path=/docs/layout-panels-docs--docs) instead. See [migration recipes](?path=/docs/layout-panels-migration-from-layout-templates--docs).

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `resizable` | `boolean` | No | false | Whether to show a drag handle between panels to allow resizing. |
| `persistent` | `boolean` | No | true | Whether panel sizes are persisted across sessions via `autoSaveId`. |
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Additional MUI `sx` styles applied to the outer container. Defaults to an empty object. |
| `panelStyle` | `CSSProperties` | No | {} | Inline CSS styles forwarded to each panel wrapper element. Defaults to an empty object. |
| `direction` | `"horizontal" | "vertical" | { xs?: "horizontal" | "vertical"; sm?: "horizontal" | "vertical"; md?: "horizontal" | "vertical" | undefined; lg?: "horizontal" | "vertical" | undefined; xl?: "horizontal" | ... 1 more ... | undefined; } | undefined` | No |  | Direction of the panel layout. Accepts a fixed value or a responsive breakpoint map. @example // Fixed direction direction="horizontal" @example // Responsive direction direction={{ xs: 'vertical', md: 'horizontal' }} |

```tsx
}

}
```

# Design System/layout/template/FourPanelsTemplate

⚠️ **Deprecated since 1.12 — removal planned for 2.0.** Use [`Panels`](?path=/docs/layout-panels-docs--docs) instead. See [migration recipes](?path=/docs/layout-panels-migration-from-layout-templates--docs).

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `"TwoTopTwoBottom"` | No | TwoTopTwoBottom | Layout variant that determines how the four panels are arranged. Currently only `'TwoTopTwoBottom'` is supported (two panels on top, two on the bottom). |
| `resizable` | `boolean` | No | false | Whether to show a drag handle between panels to allow resizing. |
| `persistent` | `boolean` | No | true | Whether panel sizes are persisted across sessions via `autoSaveId`. |
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Additional MUI `sx` styles applied to the outer container. Defaults to an empty object. |
| `panelStyle` | `CSSProperties` | No | {} | Inline CSS styles forwarded to each panel wrapper element. Defaults to an empty object. |
| `direction` | `"horizontal" | "vertical" | { xs?: "horizontal" | "vertical"; sm?: "horizontal" | "vertical"; md?: "horizontal" | "vertical" | undefined; lg?: "horizontal" | "vertical" | undefined; xl?: "horizontal" | ... 1 more ... | undefined; } | undefined` | No |  | Direction of the panel layout. Accepts a fixed value or a responsive breakpoint map. @example // Fixed direction direction="horizontal" @example // Responsive direction direction={{ xs: 'vertical', md: 'horizontal' }} |


## Examples


### TwoTopTwoBottom / Custom Sized

```tsx
{
  name: 'TwoTopTwoBottom / Custom Sized',
  args: {
    resizable: true,
    persistent: false,
    children: <>
        {/* Top Row - 70/30 split */}
        <FourPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={70} minSize={20} maxSize={80}>
          <PanelContent title="Wide Top Left" />
        </FourPanelsTemplate.Panel>
        <FourPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={30} minSize={20} maxSize={80}>
          <PanelContent title="Narrow Top Right" />
        </FourPanelsTemplate.Panel>
        {/* Bottom Row - 30/70 split */}
        <FourPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={30} minSize={20} maxSize={80}>
          <PanelContent title="Narrow Bottom Left" />
        </FourPanelsTemplate.Panel>
        <FourPanelsTemplate.Panel defaultSize={70} minSize={20} maxSize={80}>
          <PanelContent title="Wide Bottom Right" />
        </FourPanelsTemplate.Panel>
      </>
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-layout-template-overview.txt">
> ⚠️ **Deprecated since 1.12 — removal planned for 2.0.** Use [`Panels`](?path=/docs/layout-panels-docs--docs) instead. See [migration recipes](?path=/docs/layout-panels-migration-from-layout-templates--docs).

# Panel Templates

The `templates/` family provides four resizable layout shells for dashboard-style screens. They share a common `PanelsTemplateProps` API:

-   `resizable` (default `false`) — whether the divider between panels can be dragged.
-   `persistent` (default `true`) — whether panel sizes persist across sessions via localStorage.
-   `direction` — `"horizontal"` (default) or `"vertical"`, also accepts a responsive breakpoint map.
-   `sx` — MUI `sx` styles applied to the outer container.
-   `panelStyle` — inline CSS forwarded to each panel wrapper element.

All templates expose a `.Panel` subcomponent with `defaultSize`, `minSize`, and `maxSize` (percentages).

## Picking a template

| You need | Use | |---|---| | Two side-by-side or top/bottom regions | **TwoPanelsTemplate** | | Three equal-width columns | **ThreePanelsTemplate** with `variant="Columns"` (default) | | Two panels on top, one spanning the bottom | **ThreePanelsTemplate** with `variant="TwoTopOneBottom"` | | 2×2 grid (two top, two bottom) | **FourPanelsTemplate** with `variant="TwoTopTwoBottom"` (default) | | Sidebar + 3-cell main area + 1 right sidebar | **FivePanelsTemplate** |

## When NOT to use these

For simple two-column shells without resize, prefer `ContentSidebar` (under `layout/`). The templates are heavier — they ship `react-resizable-panels` and localStorage persistence. Don't use them for static layouts.

## Examples

See per-template stories:

-   [TwoPanelsTemplate – Playground](?path=/story/design-system-layout-template-twopanelstemplate--playground)
-   [ThreePanelsTemplate – Playground](?path=/story/design-system-layout-template-threepanelstemplate--playground)
-   [FourPanelsTemplate – Playground](?path=/story/design-system-layout-template-fourpanelstemplate--playground)
-   [FivePanelsTemplate – Playground](?path=/story/design-system-layout-template-fivepanelstemplate--playground)
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-template-threepanelstemplate.txt">
# Design System/layout/template/ThreePanelsTemplate

⚠️ **Deprecated since 1.12 — removal planned for 2.0.** Use [`Panels`](?path=/docs/layout-panels-docs--docs) instead. See [migration recipes](?path=/docs/layout-panels-migration-from-layout-templates--docs).

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `variant` | `"Columns" "TwoTopOneBottom"` | No | Columns | Layout variant that determines how the three panels are arranged. - `'Columns'` – three equal-width columns side by side. - `'TwoTopOneBottom'` – two panels on top and one spanning the bottom. |
| `resizable` | `boolean` | No | false | Whether to show a drag handle between panels to allow resizing. |
| `persistent` | `boolean` | No | true | Whether panel sizes are persisted across sessions via `autoSaveId`. |
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Additional MUI `sx` styles applied to the outer container. Defaults to an empty object. |
| `panelStyle` | `CSSProperties` | No | {} | Inline CSS styles forwarded to each panel wrapper element. Defaults to an empty object. |
| `direction` | `"horizontal" | "vertical" | { xs?: "horizontal" | "vertical"; sm?: "horizontal" | "vertical"; md?: "horizontal" | "vertical" | undefined; lg?: "horizontal" | "vertical" | undefined; xl?: "horizontal" | ... 1 more ... | undefined; } | undefined` | No |  | Direction of the panel layout. Accepts a fixed value or a responsive breakpoint map. @example // Fixed direction direction="horizontal" @example // Responsive direction direction={{ xs: 'vertical', md: 'horizontal' }} |


## Examples


### Columns / Custom Sized

```tsx
{
  name: 'Columns / Custom Sized',
  args: {
    resizable: true,
    persistent: false,
    children: <>
        <ThreePanelsTemplate.Panel minSize={15} defaultSize={15} maxSize={50}>
          <PanelContent title="Left Panel" />
        </ThreePanelsTemplate.Panel>
        <ThreePanelsTemplate.Panel minSize={20} defaultSize={70} maxSize={50}>
          <PanelContent title="Wide Center Panel" />
        </ThreePanelsTemplate.Panel>
        <ThreePanelsTemplate.Panel minSize={15} defaultSize={15} maxSize={50}>
          <PanelContent title="Right Panel" />
        </ThreePanelsTemplate.Panel>
      </>
  }
}
```

```tsx
    docs: {

      }
    }
  }
```

<file path="design-system-layout-toolbar-customizedsearchtoolbar.txt">
# Design System/layout/toolbar/CustomizedSearchToolbar

A customizable toolbar component with search, dropdown, filter, and action buttons. It is designed to be flexible and responsive, adapting to different screen sizes and use cases.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `size` | `"small" "medium"` | No | medium | the size of the toolbar defines the size of: - search field - dropdawn component - filter button |
| `showSearch` | `boolean` | No | false | if `true` the search field will be availible in toolbar |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | No | Name, age, ... | Placeholder text for the search field. |
| `onSearchChange` | `((query: string) => void)` | No |  | callback fired when the search value in serch field changes |
| `showPropertySelect` | `boolean` | No | false | If `true` the property selector dropdown wil |
| `propertyOptions` | `PropertyOption[]` | No | [] | Dropdown menu options (required) |
| `propertySelectLabel` | `string` | No | Attribute | Label for the property select dropdown. |
| `onPropertySelectChange` | `((value: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback fires when property dropdown selection changes |
| `showFilter` | `boolean` | No | false | I f `true` displays the filter button. |
| `filterOptions` | `FilterOption[]` | No | [] | Filter options displayed inside the filter menu |
| `onFilterSelect` | `((filter: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback fires when a filter option is selected |
| `actionButtons` | `ActionButton[]` | No | [] | Array of action buttons to be displayed on the right side |


## Examples


### Playground

```tsx
{
  args: {
    showSearch: true,
    searchPlaceholder: 'Name, age, ...',
    onSearchChange: value => console.log('Search:', value),
    showPropertySelect: true,
    propertyOptions: propertyOptions,
    onPropertySelectChange: value => console.log('Dropdown:', value),
    showFilter: true,
    filterOptions: filterOptions,
    onFilterSelect: value => console.log('Filter:', value),
    actionButtons: actionButtons
  }
}
```

### Small Version

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args,
    size: 'small'
  }
}
```

### With Data Grid

```tsx
{
  args: {
    ...Playground.args
  },
  render: args => <div>
      <CustomizedSearchToolbar {...args}></CustomizedSearchToolbar>
      <DataGrid rows={rows} columns={columns} />
    </div>
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-layout-toolbar-simplesearchtoolbar.txt">
# Design System/layout/Toolbar/SimpleSearchToolbar


A versatile search toolbar component that combines search functionality with action buttons and optional filtering.

## Features

- **Search Input**: Text field with search icon and customizable placeholder
- **Action Buttons**: Flexible array of React nodes for toolbar actions
- **Filter Dropdown**: Automatic dropdown generation when filter options are provided
- **Responsive Design**: Search field grows to fill available space
- **Customizable Styling**: Multiple sx props for fine-tuned styling control
- **Accessibility**: Proper ARIA labels and keyboard navigation support

## Use Cases

- Data table toolbars
- List view controls
- Search interfaces with actions
- Content management toolbars
- Dashboard filters and actions
        

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `placeholder` | `string` | No | Search... | Placeholder text for the search input field. |
| `actions` | `ReactElement<any, string | JSXElementConstructor<any>>[]` | No | [] | Array of React nodes to render as action buttons in the toolbar. |
| `onSearchChange` | `((value: string) => void)` | No | () => {} | Callback fired when the search input value changes. |
| `filterOptions` | `string[]` | No | [] | Array of filter options displayed in an auto-generated dropdown. When provided, the dropdown is prepended to `actions`. |
| `filterValue` | `string` | No | undefined | Current controlled value of the filter dropdown. |
| `onFilterChange` | `((value: string) => void)` | No | () => {} | Callback fired when a filter option is selected. |
| `size` | `"small" "medium"` | No | small | Size of the search input field. |
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Custom styles applied to the root container. |
| `fieldSx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Custom styles applied to the search input field. |
| `actionsSx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No | {} | Custom styles applied to the actions container. |
| `variant` | `SimpleSearchToolbarVariant | SimpleSearchToolbarVariant[]` | No |  | Predefined style variants that can be applied to the toolbar. |


## Examples


### Test

Complete example showing all features: search input, filter dropdown, primary actions, and icon buttons with tooltips.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    placeholder: 'Search templates, documents, or files...',
    onSearchChange: fn(),
    filterOptions: ['All Types', 'Templates', 'Documents', 'Images', 'Archives'],
    onFilterChange: fn(),
    actions: [<Button key="create" variant="contained" startIcon={<AddIcon />} onClick={() => fn()}>
        Create New
      </Button>, <Button key="filter" variant="outlined" startIcon={<FilterListIcon />} onClick={() => fn()}>
        Advanced Filter
      </Button>, <Tooltip key="settings" title="View Settings">
        <IconButton onClick={fn()}>
          <SettingsIcon />
        </IconButton>
      </Tooltip>, <Tooltip key="refresh" title="Refresh Data">
        <IconButton onClick={fn()}>
          <RefreshIcon />
        </IconButton>
      </Tooltip>, <Tooltip key="download" title="Export All">
        <IconButton onClick={fn()}>
          <DownloadIcon />
        </IconButton>
      </Tooltip>]
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Complete example showing all features: search input, filter dropdown, primary actions, and icon buttons with tooltips.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### Responsive Design

Responsive design example showing how actions can adapt to different screen sizes using MUI breakpoints.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    placeholder: 'Search on mobile...',
    onSearchChange: fn(),
    filterOptions: ['All', 'Active', 'Inactive'],
    onFilterChange: fn(),
    actions: [<IconButton key="add" color="primary" onClick={fn()} sx={{
      display: {
        xs: 'inline-flex',
        sm: 'none'
      },
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: 'primary.main'
    }}>
        <AddIcon />
      </IconButton>, <Button key="add-desktop" variant="contained" startIcon={<AddIcon />} onClick={fn()} sx={{
      display: {
        xs: 'none',
        sm: 'inline-flex'
      }
    }}>
        Add Item
      </Button>, <Tooltip key="refresh" title="Refresh">
        <IconButton onClick={fn()}>
          <RefreshIcon />
        </IconButton>
      </Tooltip>]
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Responsive design example showing how actions can adapt to different screen sizes using MUI breakpoints.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

# Design System/metris/FilteredMenuList

A searchable, grouped menu list with footer and loading state. Supports multi- and single-select, custom rendering, and accessibility.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `options` | `T[]` | Yes |  | Array of options to display in the grouped list. |
| `value` | `T[]` | No |  | Currently selected options (controlled). When omitted the component manages selection internally. |
| `onChange` | `((selected: T[]) => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the selection changes. |
| `onSearchChange` | `((searchTerm: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback fired on every search input change (after debounce). |
| `searchTerm` | `string` | No | '' | Current search term value (controlled). |
| `debounceTimeout` | `number` | No | 300 | Milliseconds to debounce the `onSearchChange` callback. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | No |  | When `true`, shows a loading overlay and disables the search input. |
| `label` | `string` | No |  | Label for the search text field. |
| `placeholder` | `string` | No |  | Placeholder text for the search text field. |
| `footerText` | `string` | No |  | Descriptive text rendered below the search field. |
| `groupBy` | `((option: T) => string)` | No |  | Returns the group key for an option. Defaults to `option.group`. |
| `getOptionLabel` | `((option: T) => string)` | No |  | Returns the display label for an option. Defaults to `option.name`. |
| `getOptionKey` | `((option: T) => string | number)` | No |  | Returns the unique key for an option. Defaults to `option.id`. |
| `noResultsText` | `string` | No |  | Text shown when the filtered list is empty and not loading. |
| `loadingComponent` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Custom node rendered instead of the default `CircularProgress` while loading. |
| `multiple` | `boolean` | No | true | When `true`, allows selecting multiple items simultaneously. |
| `sx` | `SxProps` | No |  | MUI `sx` styles applied to the root container. |
| `menuListProps` | `Readonly<MenuListProps>` | No |  | Additional props forwarded to the underlying `MenuList`. |
| `menuItemProps` | `Partial<Readonly<MenuItemProps>>` | No |  | Additional props forwarded to each selectable `MenuItem`. |


## Examples


### Loading

FilterTagList showing loading state.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2,
    maxWidth: 400
  }}>
      <FilteredMenuList options={[]} loading={true} label="Search Tags" placeholder="Search by name or description..." noResultsText="No results found" footerText="Loading..." />
    </Box>,
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilterTagList showing loading state.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### No Options

FilterTagList with empty options array.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2,
    maxWidth: 400
  }}>
      <FilteredMenuList options={[]} label="Search Tags" placeholder="Search by name or description..." noResultsText="No results found" footerText="No items found" />
    </Box>,
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilterTagList with empty options array.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### With Custom Footer

FilterTagList with custom footer text.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ControlledFilteredMenuList footerText="Custom footer message here" multiple />,
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilterTagList with custom footer text.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

```tsx
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
```

<file path="design-system-metris-filteredmultiselect.txt">
# Design System/metris/FilteredMultiSelect

A flexible autocomplete component for single or multi-select tag selection with search functionality.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `multiple` | `boolean` | No | true as Multiple | Enable multiple selection mode. |
| `onChange` | `((selectedItems: Multiple extends true ? T[] : T | null) => void)` | No |  | Callback when selected items change. |
| `placeholder` | `string` | No |  | Placeholder text for the input. |
| `debounceTimeout` | `number` | No | 300 | Debounce timeout in milliseconds for the search input. |
| `onSearchChange` | `((searchTerm: string) => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the search input value changes (debounced). |
| `searchTerm` | `string` | No |  | Controlled search term value. |
| `label` | `string` | No |  | Label for the input field. |
| `totalCount` | `number` | No |  | Total count of available items, used to build the default footer text. |
| `footerText` | `string` | No |  | Custom footer text. When omitted, displays "Showing X tags from Y possible search results". |
| `maxOptionsToRender` | `number` | No | 100 | Maximum number of options rendered at once for performance. |
| `showCheckboxes` | `boolean` | No | false | Show checkboxes to visualise selection state. Only applies when `multiple` is `true`. |
| `error` | `boolean` | No |  | Whether the input is in an error state. |
| `helperText` | `string` | No |  | Helper text displayed below the input. |
| `required` | `boolean` | No |  | Whether the input is required. |
| `ref` | `Ref<unknown>` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<AutocompleteSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<"div", {}, AutocompleteOwnerState<T, Multiple, false, true, "div">>; chip?: SlotProps<ElementType<Partial<ChipProps<"div">>, keyof IntrinsicElements>, {}, AutocompleteOwnerState<...>>; ... 4 more ...; popupIndicator?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |


## Examples


### Loading

FilteredMultiSelect showing loading state with custom loading text.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2,
    maxWidth: 600
  }}>
      <FilteredMultiSelect value={[]} options={[]} loading={true} loadingText="Searching tags..." label="Search Tags" placeholder="Search by name or description..." noOptionsText="No results found" totalCount={1000} />
    </Box>,
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilteredMultiSelect showing loading state with custom loading text.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

```tsx
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilteredMultiSelect with custom footer text instead of the default count message.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### With Large Dataset

FilteredMultiSelect with 1000 options, implementing controlled search functionality with performance optimization.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    label: 'Large Dataset (1000 items)',
    placeholder: 'Search through 1000 items...',
    totalCount: 5000,
    footerText: 'Performance optimized: showing max 100 options',
    maxOptionsToRender: 100
  },
  render: function Render(args) {
    // Generate a large mock dataset
    const largeDataset = Array.from({
      length: 1000
    }, (_, i) => ({
      id: i + 1,
      name: `Tag ${i + 1}`,
      description: `Description for tag ${i + 1}`
    }));
    const [selectedTags, setSelectedTags] = useState<BaseItem[]>([]);
    const [searchTerm, setSearchTerm] = useState(args.searchTerm ?? '');
    const [filteredOptions, setFilteredOptions] = useState(largeDataset);
    const [loading, setLoading] = useState(args.loading || false);
    const handleSearchChange = (search: string) => {
      setSearchTerm(search);
      setLoading(true);

      // Simulate API delay
      setTimeout(() => {
        const filtered = largeDataset.filter(tag => tag.name.toLowerCase().includes(search.toLowerCase()) || tag.description?.toLowerCase().includes(search.toLowerCase()));
        setFilteredOptions(filtered);
        setLoading(false);
      }, 200);
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      p: 2,
      maxWidth: 600
    }}>
        <FilteredMultiSelect {...args} value={selectedTags} onChange={tags => setSelectedTags(tags as BaseItem[])} options={filteredOptions} onSearchChange={handleSearchChange} searchTerm={searchTerm} loading={loading} />
      </Box>;
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilteredMultiSelect with 1000 options, implementing controlled search functionality with performance optimization.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### Single Selection

FilteredMultiSelect in single selection mode - only one tag can be selected at a time.

```tsx
{
  render: function Render() {
    const [selectedTag, setSelectedTag] = useState<BaseItem | null>(null);
    const [searchTerm, setSearchTerm] = useState('');
    const [filteredOptions, setFilteredOptions] = useState(mockTags);
    const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);
    const totalCount = 1000;
    const handleSearchChange = (search: string) => {
      setSearchTerm(search);
      setLoading(true);
      setTimeout(() => {
        const filtered = mockTags.filter(tag => tag.name.toLowerCase().includes(search.toLowerCase()) || tag.description?.toLowerCase().includes(search.toLowerCase()));
        setFilteredOptions(filtered);
        setLoading(false);
      }, 300);
    };
    const handleChange = (tag: BaseItem | null) => {
      setSelectedTag(tag);
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      p: 2,
      maxWidth: 600
    }}>
        <FilteredMultiSelect<BaseItem, false> multiple={false} value={selectedTag} onChange={handleChange} options={filteredOptions} onSearchChange={handleSearchChange} searchTerm={searchTerm} loading={loading} totalCount={totalCount} label="Select Tag" placeholder="Search for a tag..." noOptionsText="No results found" />
      </Box>;
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilteredMultiSelect in single selection mode - only one tag can be selected at a time.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

```tsx
    return <Box sx={{

        minWidth: 500,
        '& .MuiOutlinedInput-root': {
          borderRadius: 2
        },
        '& .MuiChip-root': {
          borderRadius: 1,
          backgroundColor: 'primary.light',
          color: 'primary.contrastText'
        }
      }} />
      </Box>;
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'FilteredMultiSelect with custom styling applied via sx prop.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-metris-itempickerdialog.txt">
# Design System/metris/ItemPickerDialog

A paginated picker dialog. Composes Dialog + DataGrid + SearchField. See the README for the full prop reference.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `open` | `boolean` | Yes |  | Controls open/closed state. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | Yes |  | Fired on cancel, ESC, backdrop click, or after a successful add. |
| `rows` | `TRecord[]` | Yes |  | Rows fed to the underlying DataGrid. |
| `columns` | `GridColDef<TRecord>[]` | Yes |  | Column definitions for the underlying DataGrid. |
| `getRowId` | `((row: TRecord) => string | number)` | No |  | Required only when rows lack a top-level `id` field. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | No | false | Drives the DataGrid loading overlay and the primary button loading state. |
| `selectionMode` | `"multiple" "single"` | Yes |  | Row selection mode. Drives the `onAdd` payload type. |
| `onAdd` | `(selected: ItemPickerSelectedValue<TRecord, TMode>) => void | Promise<void>` | Yes |  | Called when the user confirms the selection. Returning a Promise keeps the primary button in its loading state until it resolves. |
| `onSearch` | `((value: string) => void)` | No |  | Renders the built-in SearchField. Omit to hide the built-in search. |
| `searchSlot` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Replaces the built-in search row entirely. Takes precedence over `onSearch`. |
| `searchMessage` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Optional message line under the search row. |
| `title` | `string` | No |  | Dialog title. Falls back to `t('item-picker.add-items')`. |
| `addLabel` | `string` | No |  | Primary button label. Falls back to `t('item-picker.add')`. |
| `cancelLabel` | `string` | No |  | Secondary button label. Falls back to `t('cancel')`. |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | No |  | Built-in search placeholder. Falls back to `t('search-placeholder')`. |
| `slots` | `Partial<GridPremiumSlotsComponent>` | No |  | Forwarded to the embedded DataGrid. |
| `slotProps` | `GridPremiumSlotProps` | No |  | Forwarded to the embedded DataGrid. |
| `gridSx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No |  | Forwarded to the embedded DataGrid as `sx`. |
| `closeOnBackdropClick` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, clicking the dialog backdrop fires `onClose` (resets selection and closes). Defaults to `false` |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `size` | `"xs" "sm" "md" "lg"` | No | md | Predefined width + height preset applied when MUI's `maxWidth` is not set and `fullWidth` is false. Each size pins the paper to a width range and applies a default height range of `40vh`–`60vh`.  | Size | min-width | max-width | | ---- | --------- | --------- | | `xs` | —         | 400 px    | | `sm` | 600 px    | 600 px    | | `md` | 600 px    | 960 px    | | `lg` | 960 px    | 1280 px   |  Consumers can override individual bounds by passing `slotProps={{ paper: { sx: { ... } } }}` — overrides merge per-property because the size defaults are emitted as an `sx` array.  If MUI's `maxWidth` is provided (any value other than `undefined`/`false`) it takes precedence and the size preset is skipped entirely. |
| `draggable` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, the dialog can be dragged around the screen via its title bar. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |
| `allowBackdropClick` | `boolean` | No | false | When `true`, clicking outside the dialog does not close it, enabling multiple dialogs to coexist on screen simultaneously. |


## Examples


### Loading

```tsx
{
  render: args => <PickerDemo args={args} />,
  args: {
    ...baseArgs,
    loading: true
  }
}
```

### Backend Search

Simulates server-side search: typing in the search field debounces and shows the picker in a loading state until the "backend" returns filtered results.

```tsx
{
  render: args => <BackendSearchRender {...args} />,
  args: {
    ...baseArgs,
    selectionMode: 'multiple'
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Simulates server-side search: typing in the search field debounces and shows the picker in a loading state until the "backend" returns filtered results.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

### Load Async

Simulates fetching backend data before opening the picker. The Open button enters a loading state for ~1.5s, then the dialog appears with the data ready.

```tsx
{
  render: args => <PickerDemo args={args} openDelayMs={1500} buttonLabel="Load and open" />,
  args: {
    ...baseArgs,
    selectionMode: 'single'
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Simulates fetching backend data before opening the picker. The Open button enters a loading state for ~1.5s, then the dialog appears with the data ready.'
      }
    }
  }
}
```

```tsx
    selectionMode: 'multiple'
  }
}
```

```tsx
    open,

    rows,
    onAdd
  }) => <WithBuiltInSearchPicker open={open} onClose={onClose} rows={rows} onAdd={onAdd} args={args} />} />,
```

# Design System/mui-x/CrudDataGrid

CrudDataGrid wraps the MUI-X DataGrid Premium with inline/dialog CRUD, declarative row actions, and react-hook-form + zod validation.

## Examples


### Inline Create

```tsx
{
  render: () => <InlineCreateDemo />
}
```

### Polymorphic Edit

Each row type gets its own fields component. The same control + form values
shape is reused; only the renderEditDialog branches.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <PolymorphicEditDemo />
}
```

### With Custom Toolbar

```tsx
{
  render: () => <CustomToolbarDemo />
}
```

### Delete Confirm

```tsx
{
  render: () => <DeleteConfirmDemo />
}
```

### Loading

Loading state — `showToolbar` is required for the LinearProgress in v8.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    rows: [],
    columns: readOnlyColumns,
    loading: true,
    showToolbar: true
  }
}
```

### With Row Action

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ArchiveDemo />
}
```

### Dialog Edit With Extra Fields

```tsx
{
  render: () => <DialogEditWithExtraFieldsDemo />
}
```

### Dialog Create

```tsx
{
  render: () => <DialogCreateDemo />
}
```

### Inline Edit

```tsx
{
  render: () => <InlineEditDemo />
}
```

### Actions Promoted Custom

Promotes the custom "Clone" action inline alongside Edit and pushes Delete + Archive into the kebab.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <PromotedCustomDemo />
}
```

### Async Edit Hydration

```tsx
{
  render: () => <AsyncEditHydrationDemo />
}
```

### Server Error

`onUpdate` rejects — the dialog stays open with the typed values, the root
error appears in the banner (via `formState.errors.root`), and `onError`
fires (see Storybook Actions panel).

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ServerErrorDemo />
}
```

### Nested Editable List

The parent's `onUpdate(id, values)` receives the full record including
`values.contacts: Contact[]` — no formRef, no sideband store. Use this
pattern for any "parent-with-list-of-children" form (notifier addressees,
area technical-data, grade details, optimizer items).

```tsx
{
  render: () => <NestedListDemo />
}
```

### Default

```tsx
{
  args: {
    rows: seed,
    columns: readOnlyColumns
  }
}
```

### Dialog Edit

```tsx
{
  render: () => <DialogEditDemo />
}
```

### Empty

```tsx
{
  args: {
    rows: [],
    columns: readOnlyColumns,
    onUpdate: fn(async () => undefined),
    onDelete: fn(async () => undefined),
    schema: userSchema
  }
}
```

### Validation

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ValidationDemo />
}
```

<file path="design-system-mui-x-datagrid-toolbar-compound-api.txt">
# DataGrid Compound Toolbar API

`DataGrid` exposes its toolbar as a compound pattern. Pass a custom toolbar to `slots.toolbar` and compose it from these subcomponents:

## Subcomponents

| Subcomponent | Purpose | |---|---| | `DataGrid.Toolbar` | Root toolbar container. Wraps filter and action slots. | | `DataGrid.Toolbar.QuickFilter` | Search input that filters across all visible columns. | | `DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions` | Right-aligned container for action buttons. | | `DataGrid.Toolbar.ColumnsButton` | Column visibility toggle. | | `DataGrid.Toolbar.FilterButton` | Advanced filter panel toggle. | | `DataGrid.Toolbar.DensitySelector` | Comfortable / Compact / Standard row density. | | `DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton` | Export to CSV / Excel / print. |

## Common compositions

### Full toolbar (Metris default)

```tsx
import { Divider } from '@mui/material';
<DataGrid.Toolbar>
  <DataGrid.Toolbar.QuickFilter />
  <DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.ColumnsButton />
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.FilterButton />
    <Divider orientation="vertical" />
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton />
  </DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
</DataGrid.Toolbar>
```

### Minimal — just search + export

```tsx
<DataGrid.Toolbar>
  <DataGrid.Toolbar.QuickFilter />
  <DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton />
  </DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
</DataGrid.Toolbar>
```

### Actions-only — no search

```tsx
import { Divider } from '@mui/material';
<DataGrid.Toolbar>
  <DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.ColumnsButton />
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.FilterButton />
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.DensitySelector />
    <Divider orientation="vertical" />
    <DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton />
  </DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
</DataGrid.Toolbar>
```

## Wiring into DataGrid

Pass the composed toolbar via `slots` and set `showToolbar` to `true`:

```tsx
<DataGrid
  rows={rows}
  columns={columns}
  showToolbar
  slots={{ toolbar: MyToolbar }}
/>
```

`showToolbar` must be `true` for the slot to render — even if `slots.toolbar` is provided.

## See also

-   [Default DataGrid Playground](?path=/story/design-system-mui-x-datagrid--playground)
-   [CustomToolbar example](?path=/story/design-system-mui-x-datagrid--custom-toolbar)
-   [MinimalToolbar example](?path=/story/design-system-mui-x-datagrid--minimal-toolbar)
</file>

# Design System/mui-x/DataGrid

The Data Grid presents information in a structured format of rows and columns. The data is displayed in a user-friendly interface for efficient editing, reviewing, and analysis.

## Examples


### Actions Only Toolbar

DataGrid with actions-only toolbar
This story shows a toolbar with only action buttons, no search

```tsx
args => {
  const ActionsOnlyToolbarComponent = () => <DataGrid.Toolbar>
      <DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.ColumnsButton />
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.FilterButton />
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.DensitySelector />
        <Divider orientation="vertical" />
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton />
      </DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
    </DataGrid.Toolbar>;
  return <CardShell title="Actions-only toolbar">
      <DataGrid {...args} rows={rows} columns={columns} slots={{
      toolbar: ActionsOnlyToolbarComponent
    }} showToolbar />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

### With Pagination

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    data,
    loading
  } = useDemoData({
    dataSet: 'Employee',
    rowLength: 500,
    maxColumns: 8
  });
  return <CardShell title="Employees">
      <DataGrid {...args} columns={data.columns as GridColDef[]} rows={data.rows} loading={loading} showToolbar />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

### No Toolbar

DataGrid with no toolbar
This story shows how to completely disable the toolbar

```tsx
args => {
  return <CardShell title="No toolbar">
      <DataGrid {...args} rows={rows} columns={columns} slots={{
      toolbar: undefined
    }} />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

### Minimal Toolbar

DataGrid with minimal toolbar
This story shows a toolbar with only search and export functionality

```tsx
args => {
  const MinimalToolbarComponent = () => <DataGrid.Toolbar>
      <DataGrid.Toolbar.QuickFilter />
      <DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton />
      </DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
    </DataGrid.Toolbar>;
  return <CardShell title="Minimal toolbar">
      <DataGrid {...args} rows={rows} columns={columns} slots={{
      toolbar: MinimalToolbarComponent
    }} showToolbar />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

### Dynamic Data

```tsx
args => {
  const {
    dataSet,
    rowLength,
    maxColumns,
    ...gridProps
  } = args;
  const {
    data,
    loading
  } = useDemoData({
    dataSet,
    rowLength,
    maxColumns
  });
  const {
    columns,
    rows
  } = data;
  return <CardShell title="Commodity demo">
      <DataGrid {...gridProps} columns={columns as GridColDef[]} rows={rows} loading={loading} showToolbar />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

### With Row Actions

Demonstrates a realistic CRUD table where each row has Edit and Delete action buttons.
Clicking an action calls the corresponding handler (visible in the Actions panel).
Deleted rows are removed from the local state immediately.

```tsx
args => {
  type Employee = {
    id: number;
    name: string;
    role: string;
    department: string;
  };
  const initialEmployees: Employee[] = [{
    id: 1,
    name: 'Alice Müller',
    role: 'Engineer',
    department: 'R&D'
  }, {
    id: 2,
    name: 'Bob Schneider',
    role: 'Analyst',
    department: 'Finance'
  }, {
    id: 3,
    name: 'Carol Weber',
    role: 'Designer',
    department: 'UX'
  }, {
    id: 4,
    name: 'David Fischer',
    role: 'Manager',
    department: 'Operations'
  }, {
    id: 5,
    name: 'Eva Bauer',
    role: 'Developer',
    department: 'R&D'
  }];
  const [employees, setEmployees] = useState<Employee[]>(initialEmployees);
  const actionColumns: GridColDef[] = [{
    field: 'id',
    headerName: 'ID',
    width: 70
  }, {
    field: 'name',
    headerName: 'Name',
    flex: 1
  }, {
    field: 'role',
    headerName: 'Role',
    flex: 1
  }, {
    field: 'department',
    headerName: 'Department',
    flex: 1
  }, {
    field: 'actions',
    headerName: 'Actions',
    width: 120,
    sortable: false,
    renderCell: params => <Stack direction="row" spacing={0.5} alignItems="center" sx={{
      height: '100%'
    }}>
          <Tooltip title="Edit">
            <IconButton size="small" onClick={() => onEditRow(params.row)} aria-label={`Edit ${params.row.name}`}>
              <EditIcon fontSize="small" />
            </IconButton>
          </Tooltip>
          <Tooltip title="Delete">
            <IconButton size="small" color="error" onClick={() => {
          onDeleteRow(params.row);
          setEmployees(prev => prev.filter(e => e.id !== params.row.id));
        }} aria-label={`Delete ${params.row.name}`}>
              <DeleteIcon fontSize="small" />
            </IconButton>
          </Tooltip>
        </Stack>
  }];
  return <CardShell title="Row actions">
      <DataGrid {...args} rows={employees} columns={actionColumns} />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

### Custom Toolbar

DataGrid with custom toolbar using compound pattern
This story demonstrates how to create a custom toolbar with specific components

```tsx
args => {
  const CustomToolbarComponent = () => <DataGrid.Toolbar>
      <DataGrid.Toolbar.QuickFilter />
      <DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.ColumnsButton />
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.FilterButton />
        <Divider orientation="vertical" />
        <DataGrid.Toolbar.ExportButton />
      </DataGrid.Toolbar.Actions>
    </DataGrid.Toolbar>;
  return <CardShell title="Custom toolbar">
      <DataGrid {...args} rows={rows} columns={columns} showToolbar slots={{
      toolbar: CustomToolbarComponent
    }} />
    </CardShell>;
}
```

<file path="design-system-mui-x-date-time-dayhourpicker.txt">
# Design System/mui-x/Date Time/DayHourPicker

The Day Hour Picker component lets the user select a day and hour. Optional max limits can be set via maxDay and maxHour props.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `value` | `{ day: number; hour: number; }` | No |  | The currently selected day and hour value. |
| `onChange` | `((value: { day: number; hour: number; }) => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when the selected value changes. |
| `label` | `string` | No |  | Label displayed on the trigger text field. |
| `sx` | `object` | No |  | MUI `sx` prop forwarded to the trigger text field. |
| `maxDay` | `number` | No |  | Maximum allowed day value. When omitted, any non-negative integer is accepted. |
| `maxHour` | `number` | No |  | Maximum allowed hour value. When omitted, any non-negative integer is accepted. |
| `formatDisplayValue` | `((day: number, hour: number) => string)` | No | (day: number, hour: number): string => {
  const formatTime = (hour: number): string => {
    return hour < 10 ? `0${hour}` : `${hour}`;
  };
  return `${day} : ${formatTime(hour)}`;
} | Custom formatter for the display value shown in the trigger field. |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | No | false | If true, the picker and trigger field are disabled. |


## Examples


### Without Max Limits

DayHourPicker without max limits - allows any positive number.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: {
      day: 100,
      hour: 50
    },
    label: 'Unlimited Range'
  }
}
```

### Playground

Default DayHourPicker with standard configuration and typical max limits.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: {
      day: 1,
      hour: 0
    },
    label: 'Time Resolution',
    maxDay: 30,
    maxHour: 23
  }
}
```

### With Custom Format

DayHourPicker with custom display format.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: {
      day: 10,
      hour: 14
    },
    label: 'Custom Format',
    formatDisplayValue: (day: number, hour: number) => `Day ${day} at ${hour}:00`
  }
}
```

### With Custom Max Day

DayHourPicker with custom maximum day.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: {
      day: 3,
      hour: 6
    },
    label: 'Week Schedule',
    maxDay: 7
  }
}
```

### With Custom Max Hour

DayHourPicker with custom maximum hour (12 hours).

```tsx
{
  args: {
    value: {
      day: 5,
      hour: 8
    },
    label: 'Half Day Schedule',
    maxHour: 12
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/MUI X/Tree View/NestedListTreeview

The `NestedListTreeview` component is a MUI X RichTreeView component which UI-style is like a nested List component

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `items` | `T[]` | Yes |  | Array of tree items to render. Replaces MUI's `items` prop with a strongly-typed variant. |
| `onItemSelectionToggle` | `((event: SyntheticEvent<Element, Event> | null, item: T, isSelected: boolean) => void)` | No |  | Callback fired when an item's selection state changes. Receives the full tree item object instead of just the item id. |
| `toolbar` | `ReactNode` | No |  | Optional toolbar element rendered above the tree. |
| `searchTerm` | `string` | No |  | When provided, tree items are filtered client-side to those matching this string. |
| `filterType` | `"lenient" "strict"` | No | lenient | Controls how items are filtered when `searchTerm` is set.  - `'lenient'` — if a parent matches, all its children are included. - `'strict'` — every item and child is independently matched. |


## Examples


### Search With Fiter Type

Search toolbar with filter type

```tsx
{
  render: args => <SearchToolbarWithFilterType {...args} />,
  args: {
    items: treeviewData
  }
}
```

### With Search Toolbar

Search toolbar with default filter type

```tsx
{
  render: args => <SearchToolbarWithOutFilterType {...args} />,
  args: {
    items: treeviewData
  }
}
```

### Simple Two Line Example

```tsx
{

    items: [{
      id: '1',
      label: 'TreeItemRoot',
      description: 'this is the only root tree item in this Treeview'
    }]
  }
}
```

### Search With Action Button

Search toolbar with an action button

```tsx
{
  render: args => <SearchToolbarWithActionButton {...args} />,
  args: {
    items: treeviewData
  }
}
```

### More Complex Example With Context Menu Actions

```tsx
{
  args: {
    items: [{
      id: '1',
      label: 'TreeItemRoot',
      description: 'this is the only root tree item in this Treeview',
      itemActions: [{
        icon: <AddIcon />,
        label: 'add some stuff to this Item',
        clickAction: function (item: ActionItemType, contextItem: unknown) {
          const alert = `action : ${JSON.stringify(item)} was triggered on item: ${JSON.stringify(contextItem)}`;
          console.log(alert);
        }
      }, {
        icon: <RemoveIcon />,
        label: 'remove some stuff to this Item',
        clickAction: function (item: ActionItemType, contextItem: unknown) {
          const alert = `action : ${JSON.stringify(item)} was triggered on item: ${JSON.stringify(contextItem)}`;
          console.log(alert);
        }
      }],
      children: [{
        id: '2',
        label: 'First Leaf of the tree',
        itemActions: [{
          icon: <SendIcon />,
          label: 'send funny messages to this leaf',
          clickAction: function (item: ActionItemType, contextItem: unknown) {
            const alert = `action : ${JSON.stringify(item)} was triggered on item: ${JSON.stringify(contextItem)}`;
            console.log(alert);
          }
        }]
      }]
    }]
  }
}
```
