# Design System/MUI X/Tree View/ProjectTreeview/CustomToolbar

This example demonstrates how to use the `customToolbarItems` prop to add custom 
        buttons to the expand/collapse toolbar in the ProjectTreeview component.

## Examples


### Custom Toolbar Example


This example demonstrates how to add custom toolbar items to the ProjectTreeview's expand/collapse toolbar.
        
The custom toolbar includes:
- Delete button: Allows deleting selected items
- Add button: Adds new items to the tree
- Edit button: Edits the selected item
- More Options button: Shows additional options

These buttons are added using the `customToolbarItems` prop, which accepts a dictionary where each key is mapped to a button definition. The toolbar appears alongside the standard expand/collapse buttons when `showExpandToolbar` is set to `true`.

With the new API, you can easily specify icons, labels, and click handlers for each toolbar button:

```tsx
const customToolbarItems = {
  Delete: {
    icon: DeleteOutlinedIcon,  // Icon component or string path
    label: 'Delete Selected',  // Accessible label
    onClick: () => handleDelete()
  },
  // More buttons...
};
```
        

```tsx
{
  render: () => <TreeviewWithCustomToolbar />,
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: `
This example demonstrates how to add custom toolbar items to the ProjectTreeview's expand/collapse toolbar.
        
The custom toolbar includes:
- Delete button: Allows deleting selected items
- Add button: Adds new items to the tree
- Edit button: Edits the selected item
- More Options button: Shows additional options

These buttons are added using the \`customToolbarItems\` prop, which accepts a dictionary where each key is mapped to a button definition. The toolbar appears alongside the standard expand/collapse buttons when \`showExpandToolbar\` is set to \`true\`.

With the new API, you can easily specify icons, labels, and click handlers for each toolbar button:

\`\`\`tsx
const customToolbarItems = {
  Delete: {
    icon: DeleteOutlinedIcon,  // Icon component or string path
    label: 'Delete Selected',  // Accessible label
    onClick: () => handleDelete()
  },
  // More buttons...
};
\`\`\`
        `
      }
    }
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-mui-x-tree-view-projecttreeview.txt">
# Design System/MUI X/Tree View/ProjectTreeview

The `ProjectTreeview` component is a generic, highly customizable tree view built on MUI X's RichTreeView.

## Features

- 🧩 Custom icons via `projectItemTypes`
- 🎯 Drag-and-drop integration with `@dnd-kit`
- 🚫 Interaction control via `disabledTypes`
- ➕ Alternative expand/collapse icons via `useAlternativeIcon`
- 💾 Visual change indicators using `hasChanges: boolean`
- 🔍 Works seamlessly with search/filtering and toolbars
- 🛠️ Customizable expand/collapse toolbar with `customToolbarItems`

Designed for dynamic UIs that need visual hierarchy, custom behavior per node type, and full flexibility.

## Examples


### With Search And Dn D

Showcases search filtering and drag-and-drop together on a shared item list.
Type in the search bar to narrow items, then drag Unassigned nodes to reorder them.
The last drop target is highlighted via a status label below the tree.

```tsx
{
  render: function WithSearchAndDnDRender(args) {
    const [activeItem, setActiveItem] = useState<ProjectTreeviewItem | null>(null);
    const [searchTerm, setSearchTerm] = useState('');
    const [lastDrop, setLastDrop] = useState<string | null>(null);
    const toolbar = <CustomizedSearchToolbar size="small" showSearch searchPlaceholder="Search items…" onSearchChange={value => setSearchTerm(value)} />;
    return <Box sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      gap: 1
    }}>
        <DndContext onDragStart={event => {
        setActiveItem(event.active.data.current as ProjectTreeviewItem);
      }} onDragEnd={event => {
        if (event.over) {
          const overItem = event.over.data.current as ProjectTreeviewItem | undefined;
          setLastDrop(overItem?.label ?? event.over.id.toString());
        }
        setActiveItem(null);
      }}>
          <ProjectTreeview {...args} toolbar={toolbar} searchTerm={searchTerm} />
          <DragOverlay>
            {activeItem ? <Box sx={{
            p: 1,
            bgcolor: 'background.paper',
            border: 1,
            borderColor: 'primary.main',
            fontWeight: 600,
            width: 'max-content',
            borderRadius: 1,
            boxShadow: 3
          }}>
                {activeItem.label}
              </Box> : null}
          </DragOverlay>
        </DndContext>
        {lastDrop && <Box sx={{
        typography: 'caption',
        color: 'text.secondary'
      }}>
            Last dropped onto: <strong>{lastDrop}</strong>
          </Box>}
      </Box>;
  },
  args: {
    items: complexExample,
    showExpandToolbar: true,
    ...storieProps,
    useDndTypes: ['Unassigned', 'Component']
  }
}
```



### Virtualized (Large Tree)

Trees above the internal node-count threshold render as a flat, virtualized list via `@tanstack/react-virtual`. Expand a few folders and scroll — only the visible rows exist in the DOM. Selection, keyboard navigation (arrows / Home / End / Enter), context menus and drag-and-drop all work as in the non-virtualized path.

```tsx
{
  render: args => <VirtualizedTreeHarness {...args} />,
  args: {
    items: largeTreeExample,
    showExpandToolbar: true,
    ...storieProps,
    useDndTypes: ['File']
  }
}
```

# Design System/navigation/menu/ContextMenu

The `ContextMenu` component is a MUI component to display a context menu when the user right clicks the desired screen element

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `items` | `ContextMenuItem<T>[]` | No | [] | Array of menu items to display in the context menu |
| `item` | `T | null` | No | null | Optional data item to pass to menu item actions when clicked |
| `onOpen` | `((event: MouseEvent<Element, MouseEvent>) => void)` | No |  | Callback function called when the context menu is opened |
| `onClose` | `(() => void)` | No |  | Callback function called when the context menu is closed |
| `menuProps` | `Partial<MenuProps>` | No | {} | Additional props to pass to the underlying MUI Menu component |


## Examples


### Playground

```tsx
{
  args: {
    items: items
  },
  render: args => <ContextMenu {...args}>
      <Box sx={{
      width: '20vw',
      height: '20vh',
      bgcolor: 'action.disabledBackground'
    }} />
    </ContextMenu>
}
```

<file path="design-system-navigation-mobilestepper.txt">
# Design System/navigation/MobileStepper

This component implements a compact stepper suitable for a mobile device. It displays progress through numbered steps with navigation controls.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |
| `slots` | `Partial<MobileStepperSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<PaperProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, MobileStepperRootSlotPropsOverrides, MobileStepperOwnerState>; progress?: SlotProps<...>; dots?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; dot?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |


## Examples


### Bottom Position

Use the bottom position when you want the stepper to appear at the bottom of a container.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Use the bottom position when you want the stepper to appear at the bottom of a container.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: function Render() {
    const [activeStep, setActiveStep] = useState(0);
    const maxSteps = 3;
    const handleNext = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => Math.min(prevActiveStep + 1, maxSteps - 1));
    };
    const handleBack = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => Math.max(prevActiveStep - 1, 0));
    };

    // Example product specs
    const productSpecs = [{
      title: 'Technical Specifications',
      content: 'Processor: Intel Core i7, RAM: 16GB, Storage: 512GB SSD'
    }, {
      title: 'Physical Dimensions',
      content: 'Width: 12.8", Depth: 8.9", Height: 0.6", Weight: 2.8 lbs'
    }, {
      title: 'Connectivity',
      content: 'WiFi 6, Bluetooth 5.0, USB-C x2, HDMI, 3.5mm Audio Jack'
    }];
    return <Paper elevation={0} sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      height: 500,
      borderRadius: 2,
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: 'divider',
      position: 'relative'
    }}>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 3,
        flexGrow: 1,
        overflow: 'auto'
      }}>
          <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
            {productSpecs[activeStep].title}
          </Typography>
          <Typography variant="body2">{productSpecs[activeStep].content}</Typography>
        </Box>
        <MobileStepper variant="dots" steps={maxSteps} position="bottom" activeStep={activeStep} sx={{
        flexShrink: 0
      }} nextButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleNext} disabled={activeStep === maxSteps - 1} endIcon={<KeyboardArrowRight />}>
              Next
            </Button>} backButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleBack} disabled={activeStep === 0} startIcon={<KeyboardArrowLeft />}>
              Back
            </Button>} />
      </Paper>;
  }
}
```

### Progress Variant

Use the progress variant to show relative completion of a process.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Use the progress variant to show relative completion of a process.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: function Render() {
    const [activeStep, setActiveStep] = useState(0);
    const maxSteps = 4;
    const handleNext = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => Math.min(prevActiveStep + 1, maxSteps - 1));
    };
    const handleBack = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => Math.max(prevActiveStep - 1, 0));
    };

    // Example form steps
    const formSteps = [{
      title: 'Personal Information',
      fields: ['Name', 'Email', 'Phone']
    }, {
      title: 'Shipping Address',
      fields: ['Address', 'City', 'Postal Code']
    }, {
      title: 'Payment Details',
      fields: ['Card Number', 'Expiry', 'CVV']
    }, {
      title: 'Review Order',
      fields: ['Items', 'Total', 'Terms & Conditions']
    }];
    return <Paper elevation={0} sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      height: 500,
      borderRadius: 2,
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: 'divider'
    }}>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 3,
        flexGrow: 1
      }}>
          <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
            {formSteps[activeStep].title}
          </Typography>
          <Box sx={{
          mt: 2
        }}>
            {formSteps[activeStep].fields.map((field, index) => <Typography key={index} variant="body2" sx={{
            mb: 1
          }}>
                • {field}
              </Typography>)}
          </Box>
        </Box>
        <MobileStepper variant="progress" steps={maxSteps} position="static" activeStep={activeStep} sx={{
        flexShrink: 0
      }} nextButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleNext} disabled={activeStep === maxSteps - 1} endIcon={<KeyboardArrowRight />}>
              {activeStep === maxSteps - 1 ? 'Submit' : 'Next'}
            </Button>} backButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleBack} disabled={activeStep === 0} startIcon={<KeyboardArrowLeft />}>
              Back
            </Button>} />
      </Paper>;
  }
}
```

### Playground

```tsx
{
  args: {
    steps: 5,
    activeStep: 2,
    variant: 'dots',
    position: 'static'
  },
  render: function Render(args) {
    const {
      steps,
      activeStep: initialStep,
      ...rest
    } = args;
    return <Box sx={{
      maxWidth: 400,
      width: '100%',
      mx: 'auto'
    }}>
        <MobileStepper {...rest} steps={steps ?? 5} activeStep={initialStep ?? 0} nextButton={<Button size="small" endIcon={<KeyboardArrowRight />}>
              Next
            </Button>} backButton={<Button size="small" startIcon={<KeyboardArrowLeft />}>
              Back
            </Button>} />
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Text Variant

Use the text variant when you need to clearly show the current position and total number of steps.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Use the text variant when you need to clearly show the current position and total number of steps.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: function Render() {
    const [activeStep, setActiveStep] = useState(0);
    const maxSteps = 5;
    const handleNext = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => (prevActiveStep + 1) % maxSteps);
    };
    const handleBack = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => (prevActiveStep - 1 + maxSteps) % maxSteps);
    };

    // Example image gallery (using colors instead of actual images for simplicity)
    const imageColors = ['primary.main', 'error.main', 'success.main', 'warning.main', 'secondary.main'];
    return <Paper elevation={0} sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      height: 500,
      borderRadius: 2,
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: 'divider'
    }}>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 2,
        flexGrow: 1,
        display: 'flex',
        alignItems: 'center',
        justifyContent: 'center',
        bgcolor: imageColors[activeStep]
      }}>
          <Typography variant="h6" sx={{
          color: 'common.white'
        }}>
            Photo {activeStep + 1}
          </Typography>
        </Box>
        <MobileStepper variant="text" steps={maxSteps} position="static" activeStep={activeStep} sx={{
        flexShrink: 0
      }} nextButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleNext} endIcon={<KeyboardArrowRight />}>
              Next
            </Button>} backButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleBack} startIcon={<KeyboardArrowLeft />}>
              Previous
            </Button>} />
      </Paper>;
  }
}
```

### Dots Variant

Use the dot variant for showing distinct steps where each step is equally important.

```tsx
{
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Use the dot variant for showing distinct steps where each step is equally important.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: function Render() {
    const [activeStep, setActiveStep] = useState(0);
    const maxSteps = 5;
    const handleNext = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => Math.min(prevActiveStep + 1, maxSteps - 1));
    };
    const handleBack = () => {
      setActiveStep(prevActiveStep => Math.max(prevActiveStep - 1, 0));
    };

    // Example content for each step
    const stepContent = [{
      title: 'Welcome to our app!',
      body: 'Swipe through to learn about our main features.'
    }, {
      title: 'Easy Navigation',
      body: 'Our intuitive interface makes finding what you need simple.'
    }, {
      title: 'Powerful Search',
      body: 'Find exactly what you need with our advanced search capabilities.'
    }, {
      title: 'Personalization',
      body: 'Customize your experience to match your preferences.'
    }, {
      title: 'Get Started',
      body: "You're all set! Tap 'Finish' to begin using the app."
    }];
    return <Paper elevation={0} sx={{
      display: 'flex',
      flexDirection: 'column',
      height: 500,
      borderRadius: 2,
      border: '1px solid',
      borderColor: 'divider'
    }}>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 3,
        flexGrow: 1,
        display: 'flex',
        flexDirection: 'column',
        justifyContent: 'center'
      }}>
          <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom align="center">
            {stepContent[activeStep].title}
          </Typography>
          <Typography variant="body2" align="center">
            {stepContent[activeStep].body}
          </Typography>
        </Box>
        <MobileStepper variant="dots" steps={maxSteps} position="static" activeStep={activeStep} sx={{
        flexShrink: 0
      }} nextButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleNext} disabled={activeStep === maxSteps - 1} endIcon={<KeyboardArrowRight />}>
              {activeStep === maxSteps - 1 ? 'Finish' : 'Next'}
            </Button>} backButton={<Button size="small" onClick={handleBack} disabled={activeStep === 0} startIcon={<KeyboardArrowLeft />}>
              Back
            </Button>} />
      </Paper>;
  }
}
```
</file>

# Design System/navigation/SideNavigation

The SideNavigation component uses a composition pattern with compound components for flexible bottom navigation customization. Use SideNavigation.BottomNavigation and its child components to build your navigation.

## Examples


### Expanded

Default expanded navigation with full bottom navigation

```tsx
{
  args: {
    routes: sampleItems,
    appSegment: 'metris-platform',
    viewMode: 'expanded',
    toggleExpand: () => console.log('Toggle expand'),
    currentApp: mockCurrentApp,
    children: <SideNavigation.BottomNavigation>
        <SideNavigation.BottomNavigation.Notification hasNotification onClick={() => console.log('Notification clicked')} />
        <SideNavigation.BottomNavigation.Settings onClick={() => console.log('Settings clicked')} />
        <SideNavigation.BottomNavigation.Information onClick={() => console.log('Information clicked')} />
        <SideNavigation.BottomNavigation.UserAccount user={mockUser} onThemeToggle={() => console.log('Theme toggle')} onLogout={() => console.log('Logout')} onOpenAccountPage={() => console.log('Open account page')} />
      </SideNavigation.BottomNavigation>
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-navigation-stepper.txt">
# Design System/navigation/Stepper

Steppers convey progress through numbered steps. It provides a wizard-like workflow.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `color` | `"error" "success" "warning" "primary"` | No | primary | The color of the Stepper. - 'primary' | 'error' | 'warning' | 'success' |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Error Step

Stepper with error state on a step.
The label and optional label get error color and icon.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    activeStep: 1
  },
  render: args => <Stepper {...args}>
      <Step>First Step</Step>
      <Step color="error">Error Message</Step>
      <Step>Final Step</Step>
    </Stepper>
}
```

### Onboarding Flow

A 3-step onboarding flow where each step contains a TextField.
The Next button stays disabled until the current field has a non-empty value.
The final step renders a summary of the collected data.

```tsx
{
  render: function OnboardingFlowRender() {
    const [activeStep, setActiveStep] = useState(0);
    const [account, setAccount] = useState('');
    const [profile, setProfile] = useState('');
    const stepLabels = ['Account', 'Profile', 'Confirmation'];
    const currentValue = activeStep === 0 ? account : activeStep === 1 ? profile : 'done';
    const isNextDisabled = activeStep < 2 && currentValue.trim() === '';
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%',
      maxWidth: 560
    }}>
        <Stepper activeStep={activeStep}>
          {stepLabels.map(label => <Step key={label}>{label}</Step>)}
        </Stepper>

        <Box sx={{
        mt: 3,
        minHeight: 120
      }}>
          {activeStep === 0 && <TextField label="Email address" placeholder="you@example.com" value={account} onChange={e => setAccount(e.target.value)} fullWidth />}
          {activeStep === 1 && <TextField label="Display name" placeholder="Your name" value={profile} onChange={e => setProfile(e.target.value)} fullWidth />}
          {activeStep === 2 && <Box sx={{
          display: 'flex',
          flexDirection: 'column',
          gap: 1
        }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1">Setup complete — here is your summary:</Typography>
              <Typography variant="body2">
                <strong>Email:</strong> {account}
              </Typography>
              <Typography variant="body2">
                <strong>Display name:</strong> {profile}
              </Typography>
            </Box>}
        </Box>

        <Box sx={{
        mt: 2,
        display: 'flex',
        gap: 1
      }}>
          <Button disabled={activeStep === 0} onClick={() => setActiveStep(s => s - 1)}>
            Back
          </Button>
          {activeStep < 2 ? <Button disabled={isNextDisabled} onClick={() => setActiveStep(s => s + 1)}>
              Next
            </Button> : <Button onClick={() => {
          setActiveStep(0);
          setAccount('');
          setProfile('');
        }}>
              Start over
            </Button>}
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Success Step

Stepper with success state on a step.
The label and optional label get success color and icon.

```tsx

  args: {

  render: args => <Stepper {...args}>
```

```tsx
    activeStep: 1
```


### Walk Through Steps

Demonstrates walking through a linear stepper step by step.
The play function clicks Next three times and asserts the active step label advances each click.

```tsx
{
  render: function WalkThroughStepsRender() {
    const steps = ['Step One', 'Step Two', 'Step Three', 'Step Four'];
    const [activeStep, setActiveStep] = useState(0);
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%'
    }}>
        <Stepper activeStep={activeStep}>
          {steps.map(label => <Step key={label}>{label}</Step>)}
        </Stepper>
        <Box sx={{
        mt: 2,
        display: 'flex',
        gap: 1
      }}>
          <Button disabled={activeStep === 0} onClick={() => setActiveStep(s => s - 1)}>
            Back
          </Button>
          <Button disabled={activeStep === steps.length - 1} onClick={() => setActiveStep(s => s + 1)}>
            Next
          </Button>
        </Box>
        <Typography sx={{
        mt: 1
      }}>Active step: {activeStep}</Typography>
      </Box>;
  },
  play: async ({
    canvasElement
  }) => {
    const canvas = within(canvasElement);
    const nextBtn = await canvas.findByRole('button', {
      name: /next/i
    });
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Active step: 0')).toBeInTheDocument();
    await userEvent.click(nextBtn);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Active step: 1')).toBeInTheDocument();
    await userEvent.click(nextBtn);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Active step: 2')).toBeInTheDocument();
    await userEvent.click(nextBtn);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Active step: 3')).toBeInTheDocument();
  }
}
```

### Alternative Label Stepper

```tsx
{
  args: {
    alternativeLabel: true,
    activeStep: 0
  },
  render: args => <Stepper {...args}>
      <Step>Step 1</Step>
      <Step>Step 2</Step>
      <Step>Step 3</Step>
    </Stepper>
}
```

<file path="design-system-navigation-tabs.txt">
# Design System/navigation/Tabs

Tabs make it easy to explore and switch between different views.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `sx` | `SxProps<Theme>` | No |  | The system prop that allows defining system overrides as well as additional CSS styles. |
| `size` | `"small" "medium"` | No | medium | The size of the tabs component. - 'small': height 32px - 'medium': height 44px |
| `type` | `"primary" "secondary"` | No | primary | The type of the tabs component styling. - 'primary': Selected tab shows background color change, no indicator - 'secondary': Selected tab shows indicator, no background color change |
| `slots` | `(Partial<TabsSlots> & { StartScrollButtonIcon?: ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>; EndScrollButtonIcon?: ElementType<...>; }) | undefined` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<"div", TabsRootSlotPropsOverrides, TabsOwnerState>; scroller?: SlotProps<"div", TabsScrollerSlotPropsOverrides, TabsOwnerState>; ... 5 more ...; endScrollButtonIcon?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### With Lazy Loaded Panels

Three tabs whose panel content mounts only the first time each tab is activated.
The "Loaded at" timestamp inside each panel stays fixed on revisits, demonstrating
that the panel was not re-rendered — only the first activation triggers a mount.

```tsx
{
  render: function WithLazyLoadedPanelsRender() {
    const tabDefs = useMemo(() => [{
      value: 1,
      label: 'Overview'
    }, {
      value: 2,
      label: 'Analytics'
    }, {
      value: 3,
      label: 'Settings'
    }], []);
    const [activeTab, setActiveTab] = useState(1);
    const [activated, setActivated] = useState<Set<number>>(new Set([1]));
    const [loadedAt] = useState<Map<number, string>>(() => new Map([[1, new Date().toLocaleTimeString()]]));
    const handleChange = (_event: React.SyntheticEvent, newValue: number) => {
      setActiveTab(newValue);
      setActivated(prev => {
        if (prev.has(newValue)) return prev;
        loadedAt.set(newValue, new Date().toLocaleTimeString());
        return new Set([...prev, newValue]);
      });
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%'
    }}>
        <Tabs value={activeTab} onChange={handleChange} size="medium" type="primary">
          {tabDefs.map(tab => <Tab key={tab.value} label={tab.label} value={tab.value} />)}
        </Tabs>

        <Box sx={{
        border: '1px solid',
        borderColor: 'divider',
        p: 2,
        mt: 1,
        minHeight: 100
      }}>
          {tabDefs.map(tab => activated.has(tab.value) ? <Box key={tab.value} role="tabpanel" hidden={activeTab !== tab.value}>
                <Typography variant="subtitle2">{tab.label} panel</Typography>
                <Typography variant="body2" sx={{
            color: 'text.secondary'
          }}>
                  Loaded at: {loadedAt.get(tab.value)}
                </Typography>
              </Box> : null)}
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Tab Switch

Demonstrates switching between tabs and asserting that the corresponding panel content appears.
The play function clicks each tab in sequence and verifies the visible content changes.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    size: 'medium',
    type: 'primary',
    value: 1
  },
  render: function TabSwitchRender(args) {
    const [value, setValue] = useState(args.value ?? 1);
    const handleChange = (_event: React.SyntheticEvent, newValue: number) => {
      setValue(newValue);
    };
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%'
    }}>
        <Tabs {...args} value={value} onChange={handleChange}>
          <Tab label="Overview" value={1} />
          <Tab label="Details" value={2} />
          <Tab label="Settings" value={3} />
        </Tabs>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 2,
        border: '1px solid',
        borderColor: 'divider',
        mt: 2
      }}>
          {value === 1 && <Typography>Overview panel content</Typography>}
          {value === 2 && <Typography>Details panel content</Typography>}
          {value === 3 && <Typography>Settings panel content</Typography>}
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  },
  play: async ({
    canvasElement
  }) => {
    const canvas = within(canvasElement);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Overview panel content')).toBeInTheDocument();
    const detailsTab = await canvas.findByRole('tab', {
      name: /details/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(detailsTab);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Details panel content')).toBeInTheDocument();
    const settingsTab = await canvas.findByRole('tab', {
      name: /settings/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(settingsTab);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Settings panel content')).toBeInTheDocument();
    const overviewTab = await canvas.findByRole('tab', {
      name: /overview/i
    });
    await userEvent.click(overviewTab);
    await expect(canvas.getByText('Overview panel content')).toBeInTheDocument();
  }
}
```

### Spacing Showcase

Verifies consistent 8px spacing between start icon, label, and close icon. Long labels truncate while preserving spacing.

```tsx
{
  args: {
    size: 'medium',
    type: 'primary',
    value: 1
  },
  parameters: {
    docs: {
      description: {
        story: 'Verifies consistent 8px spacing between start icon, label, and close icon. Long labels truncate while preserving spacing.'
      }
    }
  },
  render: function SpacingShowcaseRender(args) {
    const [value, setValue] = useState(1);
    const handleChange = (_e: React.SyntheticEvent, v: number) => setValue(v);
    return <Box sx={{
      width: '100%'
    }}>
        <Tabs {...args} value={value} onChange={handleChange}>
          <Tab value={1} label="Short" icon={<RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon fontSize="small" />} showCloseIcon />
          <Tab value={2} label="Medium Label" icon={<RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon fontSize="small" />} showCloseIcon />
          <Tab value={3} label="A Very Long Tab Label That Should Truncate Gracefully Without Breaking Layout" icon={<RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon fontSize="small" />} showCloseIcon />
          <Tab value={4} label="End Icon Variant" iconPosition="end" icon={<RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon fontSize="small" />} showCloseIcon />
        </Tabs>
        <Box sx={{
        mt: 4,
        p: 4,
        border: '1px solid',
        borderColor: 'divider',
        borderRadius: 1
      }}>
          <Typography variant="body2">Active tab value: {value}</Typography>
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

### Closable Tabs

### Vertical Medium Primary

```tsx
{
  args: {
    size: 'medium',
    type: 'primary',
    value: 1
  },
  render: args => <VerticalTabsDemo {...args} />
}
```

### Use Tabs Horizontal

This story showcases the simplified useTabs hook with TabsComponent - the simplest way to use dynamic tabs

```tsx
{
  args: {
    size: 'medium',
    type: 'primary'
  },
  render: args => <TabsProvider initialTabs={[{
    id: 'tab1',
    label: 'Welcome',
    removable: true,
    content: <Typography variant="body1">Welcome to the dynamic tabs demo!</Typography>
  }, {
    id: 'tab2',
    label: 'Features',
    removable: true,
    content: <Typography variant="body1">This demo shows dynamic tab management.</Typography>
  }]}>
      <UseTabsHorizontalDemo {...args} />
    </TabsProvider>
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-surfaces-accordion.txt">
# Design System/surfaces/Accordion

The `Accordion ` component lets users show and hide sections of related content on a page.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `slots` | `Partial<AccordionSlots>` | No | {} | The components used for each slot inside. |
| `slotProps` | `{ root?: SlotProps<ElementType<PaperProps, keyof IntrinsicElements>, AccordionRootSlotPropsOverrides, AccordionOwnerState>; heading?: SlotProps<...>; transition?: SlotComponentProps<...> | undefined; region?: SlotProps<...> | undefined; } | undefined` | No | {} | The props used for each slot inside. |
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Disabled

The `disabled` prop on the Accordion component to disable interaction and focus.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Accordion disabled>
        <AccordionSummary>
          <Checkbox checked={false} />
          <Typography variant="body1" sx={{
          mt: 4,
          mb: 4
        }}>
            Accordion 2
          </Typography>
          <IconButton sx={{
          marginLeft: 'auto'
        }}>
            <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
          </IconButton>
        </AccordionSummary>
        <AccordionDetails>Details</AccordionDetails>
      </Accordion>;
  }
}
```

### Accordion Group

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Stack spacing={1}>
        <Accordion>
          <AccordionSummary>
            <Checkbox checked={false} />
            <Typography variant="body1" sx={{
            mt: 4,
            mb: 4
          }}>
              Accordion 1
            </Typography>
            <IconButton sx={{
            marginLeft: 'auto'
          }}>
              <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
            </IconButton>
          </AccordionSummary>
          <AccordionDetails>Details</AccordionDetails>
        </Accordion>

        <Accordion>
          <AccordionSummary>
            <Checkbox checked={false} />
            <Typography variant="body1" sx={{
            mt: 4,
            mb: 4
          }}>
              Accordion 2
            </Typography>
            <IconButton sx={{
            marginLeft: 'auto'
          }}>
              <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
            </IconButton>
          </AccordionSummary>
          <AccordionDetails>Details</AccordionDetails>
        </Accordion>
      </Stack>;
  }
}
```

### FAQ

A practical FAQ section implementation using Accordion components.
This example demonstrates how accordions can be used to create an
interactive FAQ section with custom styling and badges.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    const faqItems = [{
      question: 'How do I reset my password?',
      answer: 'You can reset your password by clicking on the "Forgot Password" link on the login page. You will receive an email with instructions to create a new password.',
      isNew: true
    }, {
      question: 'Can I change my subscription plan?',
      answer: 'Yes, you can change your subscription plan at any time. Go to Account Settings > Subscription and select "Change Plan" to see available options.',
      isNew: false
    }, {
      question: 'How do I export my data?',
      answer: 'To export your data, navigate to the Reports section and click on the Export button. You can choose between CSV, Excel, or PDF formats depending on your needs.',
      isNew: false
    }, {
      question: 'Is there a mobile app available?',
      answer: 'Yes, we offer mobile apps for both iOS and Android devices. You can download them from the App Store or Google Play Store by searching for "Metris".',
      isNew: true
    }, {
      question: 'How can I contact customer support?',
      answer: 'Our support team is available 24/7. You can reach us through the Help Center, by emailing support@andritz.com, or by using the live chat feature in the bottom right corner of any page when logged in.',
      isNew: false
    }];
    return <Stack spacing={1} sx={{
      maxWidth: 700,
      mx: 'auto'
    }}>
        <Typography variant="h4" sx={{
        mb: 2
      }}>
          Frequently Asked Questions
        </Typography>

        {faqItems.map((item, index) => <Accordion key={index} sx={{
        border: '1px solid',
        borderColor: 'divider',
        '&:first-of-type': {
          borderTopLeftRadius: 8,
          borderTopRightRadius: 8
        },
        '&:last-of-type': {
          borderBottomLeftRadius: 8,
          borderBottomRightRadius: 8
        }
      }}>
            <AccordionSummary sx={{
          backgroundColor: 'background.paper',
          '&:hover': {
            backgroundColor: 'action.hover'
          }
        }}>
              <Stack direction="row" alignItems="center" spacing={1} sx={{
            width: '100%'
          }}>
                <Typography variant="subtitle1" sx={{
              fontWeight: 500
            }}>
                  {item.question}
                </Typography>
                {item.isNew && <Box sx={{
              ml: 1,
              px: 1,
              py: 0.5,
              bgcolor: 'primary.light',
              borderRadius: 1,
              fontSize: theme => theme.typography.caption.fontSize,
              fontWeight: 'bold',
              color: 'primary.dark'
            }}>
                    NEW
                  </Box>}
              </Stack>
              <IconButton sx={{
            marginLeft: 'auto'
          }}>
                <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
              </IconButton>
            </AccordionSummary>
            <AccordionDetails>
              <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" sx={{
            pl: 1
          }}>
                {item.answer}
              </Typography>
              {item.isNew && <Box sx={{
            mt: 2,
            pt: 2,
            borderTop: '1px solid',
            borderColor: 'divider'
          }}>
                  <Typography variant="caption" color="primary">
                    This answer was updated recently. Was this helpful?
                  </Typography>
                  <Stack direction="row" spacing={1} sx={{
              mt: 1
            }}>
                    <IconButton size="small" color="primary" aria-label="Yes, this was helpful">
                      <Typography variant="caption" sx={{
                  display: 'flex',
                  alignItems: 'center'
                }}>
                        👍 Yes
                      </Typography>
                    </IconButton>
                    <IconButton size="small" color="primary" aria-label="No, this wasn't helpful">
                      <Typography variant="caption" sx={{
                  display: 'flex',
                  alignItems: 'center'
                }}>
                        👎 No
                      </Typography>
                    </IconButton>
                  </Stack>
                </Box>}
            </AccordionDetails>
          </Accordion>)}

        <Box sx={{
        mt: 2,
        textAlign: 'center'
      }}>
          <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary">
            Can&apos;t find what you&apos;re looking for?
          </Typography>
          <Button variant="outlined" size="small" startIcon={<Typography>✉️</Typography>} sx={{
          mt: 1
        }}>
            Contact Support
          </Button>
        </Box>
      </Stack>;
  }
}
```

### Default Expanded

```tsx
  args: {

          marginLeft: 'auto'
        }}>
            <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
          </IconButton>
        </AccordionSummary>
        <AccordionDetails>Details</AccordionDetails>
      </Accordion>;
  }
}
```

### Checkout Flow

Checkout Flow Example

This example demonstrates using accordions to create a multi-step checkout process
with payment details, delivery address, and order confirmation.

```tsx
{
  render: () => {
    return <Box sx={{
      maxWidth: 800,
      mx: 'auto',
      mb: 8,
      position: 'relative'
    }}>
        <Typography variant="h4" sx={{
        mb: 3
      }}>
          Complete Your Order
        </Typography>

        <Stack spacing={2}>
          {/* Payment Details Accordion */}
          <Accordion defaultExpanded>
            <AccordionSummary sx={{
            backgroundColor: 'background.paper',
            '&:hover': {
              backgroundColor: 'action.hover'
            }
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1" sx={{
              fontWeight: 500
            }}>
                Payment Details
              </Typography>
              <IconButton sx={{
              marginLeft: 'auto'
            }}>
                <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
              </IconButton>
            </AccordionSummary>
            <AccordionDetails>
              <Stack spacing={3}>
                <Box>
                  <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
                    Card Number
                  </Typography>
                  <TextField fullWidth placeholder="1234 5678 9012 3456" slotProps={{
                  htmlInput: {
                    maxLength: 19
                  }
                }} />
                </Box>

                <Box>
                  <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
                    Cardholder Name
                  </Typography>
                  <TextField fullWidth placeholder="John Doe" />
                </Box>

                <Stack direction={{
                xs: 'column',
                sm: 'row'
              }} spacing={2}>
                  <Box sx={{
                  width: '100%'
                }}>
                    <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
                      Expiration Date
                    </Typography>
                    <TextField fullWidth placeholder="MM/YY" slotProps={{
                    htmlInput: {
                      maxLength: 5
                    }
                  }} />
                  </Box>
                  <Box sx={{
                  width: '100%'
                }}>
                    <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
                      CVC / CVC2
                    </Typography>
                    <TextField fullWidth placeholder="123" slotProps={{
                    htmlInput: {
                      maxLength: 4
                    }
                  }} />
                  </Box>
                </Stack>
              </Stack>
            </AccordionDetails>
          </Accordion>

          {/* Delivery Address Accordion */}
          <Accordion>
            <AccordionSummary sx={{
            backgroundColor: 'background.paper',
            '&:hover': {
              backgroundColor: 'action.hover'
            }
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1" sx={{
              fontWeight: 500
            }}>
                Delivery Address
              </Typography>
              <IconButton sx={{
              marginLeft: 'auto'
            }}>
                <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
              </IconButton>
            </AccordionSummary>
            <AccordionDetails>
              <Stack spacing={3}>
                <Stack direction={{
                xs: 'column',
                sm: 'row'
              }} spacing={2}>
                  <TextField fullWidth label="First Name" placeholder="John" />
                  <TextField fullWidth label="Last Name" placeholder="Smith" />
                </Stack>

                <TextField fullWidth label="Address Line 1" placeholder="123 Main St" />
                <TextField fullWidth label="Address Line 2" placeholder="Apt 4B" />

                <Stack direction={{
                xs: 'column',
                sm: 'row'
              }} spacing={2}>
                  <TextField fullWidth label="City" placeholder="New York" />
                  <TextField fullWidth label="State/Province" placeholder="NY" />
                  <TextField fullWidth label="Zip/Postal Code" placeholder="10001" />
                </Stack>

                <TextField select fullWidth label="Country" defaultValue="US">
                  <MenuItem value="US">United States</MenuItem>
                  <MenuItem value="CA">Canada</MenuItem>
                  <MenuItem value="UK">United Kingdom</MenuItem>
                  <MenuItem value="AU">Australia</MenuItem>
                </TextField>
              </Stack>
            </AccordionDetails>
          </Accordion>

          {/* Confirmation Accordion */}
          <Accordion>
            <AccordionSummary sx={{
            backgroundColor: 'background.paper',
            '&:hover': {
              backgroundColor: 'action.hover'
            }
          }}>
              <Typography variant="subtitle1" sx={{
              fontWeight: 500
            }}>
                Confirmation
              </Typography>
              <IconButton sx={{
              marginLeft: 'auto'
            }}>
                <RocketLaunchOutlinedIcon />
              </IconButton>
            </AccordionSummary>
            <AccordionDetails>
              <Stack spacing={3}>
                <Typography variant="body1" fontWeight={500}>
                  Order Summary
                </Typography>

                <Box sx={{
                backgroundColor: 'action.hover',
                p: 2,
                borderRadius: 1
              }}>
                  <Stack spacing={2}>
                    <Stack direction="row" justifyContent="space-between">
                      <Typography variant="body2">Product 1</Typography>
                      <Typography variant="body2">$49.99</Typography>
                    </Stack>
                    <Stack direction="row" justifyContent="space-between">
                      <Typography variant="body2">Product 2</Typography>
                      <Typography variant="body2">$29.99</Typography>
                    </Stack>
                    <Stack direction="row" justifyContent="space-between">
                      <Typography variant="body2">Shipping</Typography>
                      <Typography variant="body2">$5.99</Typography>
                    </Stack>
                    <Stack direction="row" justifyContent="space-between">
                      <Typography variant="body2">Tax</Typography>
                      <Typography variant="body2">$8.60</Typography>
                    </Stack>
                    <Box sx={{
                    borderTop: '1px solid',
                    borderColor: 'divider',
                    pt: 1
                  }}>
                      <Stack direction="row" justifyContent="space-between">
                        <Typography variant="subtitle2">Total</Typography>
                        <Typography variant="subtitle2">$94.57</Typography>
                      </Stack>
                    </Box>
                  </Stack>
                </Box>

                <Box>
                  <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
                    Additional Notes
                  </Typography>
                  <TextField fullWidth multiline rows={3} placeholder="Add any special instructions or delivery notes here" />
                </Box>

                <Box sx={{
                display: 'flex',
                alignItems: 'center'
              }}>
                  <Checkbox defaultChecked />
                  <Typography variant="body2">
                    I agree to the{' '}
                    <Link href="#" underline="hover">
                      Terms of Service
                    </Link>{' '}
                    and{' '}
                    <Link href="#" underline="hover">
                      Privacy Policy
                    </Link>
                  </Typography>
                </Box>
              </Stack>
            </AccordionDetails>
          </Accordion>
        </Stack>

        {/* Action Buttons */}
        <Box sx={{
        display: 'flex',
        justifyContent: 'flex-end',
        mt: 4,
        gap: 2
      }}>
          <Button variant="outlined">Cancel</Button>
          <Button variant="contained" color="primary">
            Confirm Order
          </Button>
        </Box>
      </Box>;
  }
}
```

<file path="design-system-surfaces-card.txt">
# Design System/surfaces/Card

The Card component is a flexible container for displaying content. It can include headers, media, content, and actions.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Raised

```tsx
args => <Card {...args} raised>
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="h6">Raised Card</Typography>
      <Typography variant="body1">This card has a raised shadow effect.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
  </Card>
```

### Card Elements Bundled

```tsx
args => <Card {...args}>
    <CardHeader title="Complex Card" subheader="This card includes multiple sections" />
    <CardMedia component="img" style={{
    height: '140px'
  }} image="https://mui.com/static/images/cards/contemplative-reptile.jpg" />
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="body1">This is a complex card with a header, media, content, and actions.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
    <CardActions>
      <Button size="small">Share</Button>
      <Button size="small">Learn More</Button>
    </CardActions>
  </Card>
```

### With Media

```tsx
args => <Card {...args}>
    <CardMedia component="img" style={{
    height: '140px'
  }} image="https://mui.com/static/images/cards/contemplative-reptile.jpg" />
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="h6">Card Title</Typography>
      <Typography variant="body1">This card includes an image as media.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
  </Card>
```

### Square

```tsx
args => <Card {...args} square>
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="h6">Square Card</Typography>
      <Typography variant="body1">This card has square corners.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
  </Card>
```

### With Header

```tsx
args => <Card {...args}>
    <CardHeader title="Card Title" subheader="Card Subheader" />
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="body1">This card includes a header with a title and subheader.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
  </Card>
```

### Outlined

```tsx
args => <Card {...args} variant="outlined">
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="h6">Outlined Card</Typography>
      <Typography variant="body1">This card has an outlined variant.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
  </Card>
```

### Playground

```tsx
{
  args: {
    raised: false,
    elevation: 1,
    square: false,
    variant: 'elevation'
  },
  render: args => <Card {...args}>
      <CardHeader title="Card Title" subheader="Card Subheader" />
      <CardContent>
        <Typography variant="h6">Card Title</Typography>
        <Typography variant="body1">This is a basic card with some content inside it.</Typography>
      </CardContent>
      <CardActions>
        <Button size="small">Share</Button>
        <Button size="small">Learn More</Button>
      </CardActions>
    </Card>
}
```

### With Actions

```tsx
args => <Card {...args}>
    <CardContent>
      <Typography variant="h6">Card Title</Typography>
      <Typography variant="body1">This card includes actions at the bottom.</Typography>
    </CardContent>
    <CardActions>
      <Button size="small">Share</Button>
      <Button size="small">Learn More</Button>
    </CardActions>
  </Card>
```
</file>

# Design System/surfaces/MetrisAppBar

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `title` | `string` | Yes |  | The application title displayed in the app bar. |
| `height` | `number` | Yes | 40 | Height of the app bar in pixels. Applied to both the AppBar and the inner Toolbar. |


## Examples


### Playground

```tsx
{

    title: 'Metris Platform',
    height: 40
  }
}
```

# Design System/surfaces/Paper

The `Paper` component is a container for displaying content on an elevated surface.

## Props


| Name | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|------|------|----------|---------|-------------|
| `ref` | `((instance: HTMLDivElement | null) => void) | RefObject<HTMLDivElement> | null` | No |  |  |
| `component` | `ElementType<any, keyof IntrinsicElements>` | No |  |  |


## Examples


### Outlined Paper Not Square

```tsx
args => <Paper {...args} sx={{
  p: 2
}}>
    <Typography variant="h6">Ountlined Paper</Typography>
    <Typography variant="body1">This paper is of variant {args.variant} and not square.</Typography>
  </Paper>
```

### Outlined Square

```tsx
args => <Paper {...args} sx={{
  p: 2
}}>
    <Typography variant="h6">Outlined Square Paper</Typography>
    <Typography variant="body1">This paper is of variant {args.variant} and square.</Typography>
  </Paper>
```

# Design System/utils/DragAndDrop

Drag-and-drop sortable list. `SortableContainer` manages drag state and calls `reorder(fromIndex, toIndex)`. `SortableItem` wraps each list item with a drag handle.

## Examples


### Disabled

With `enabled={false}`, items render without drag handles

```tsx
{
  render: () => <SortableListDemo enabled={false} />
}
```

### Playground

Drag items to reorder the list

```tsx
{
  render: () => <SortableListDemo />
}
```
</file>

<file path="design-system-utils-mediaquery.txt">
# Design System/utils/MediaQuery

Responsive utilities. `useBreakpoint()` returns 12 boolean flags. The render components (`MobileScreen`, `TabletScreen`, `DesktopScreen`, `DefaultScreen`) conditionally render children based on viewport.

## Examples


### All Flags

Resize the viewport to see flag values update live

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
        useBreakpoint() flags
      </Typography>
      <AllFlagsDisplay />
    </Box>
}
```

### MobileScreen

```tsx
{
  name: 'MobileScreen',
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
        This box is only visible below 768px (mobile):
      </Typography>
      <MobileScreen>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 2,
        bgcolor: 'primary.light',
        borderRadius: 1
      }}>Mobile content (visible below 768px)</Box>
      </MobileScreen>
    </Box>
}
```

### Container Override

`BreakpointOverrideProvider` makes all `useBreakpoint()` calls inside it respond
to the supplied dimensions instead of the real viewport.

Drag the sliders to simulate a container's width and height — the flags below
update exactly as they would if the viewport were that size. No viewport resize needed.

**When to use:** Any resizable panel, dialog, or embedded widget that should drive
its own responsive behaviour independently of the page viewport.

```tsx
{
  render: () => <ContainerOverrideDemo />
}
```

### Playground

Resize the viewport to see breakpoint flags update live

```tsx
{
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Typography variant="h6" gutterBottom>
        useBreakpoint() flags
      </Typography>
      <AllFlagsDisplay />
    </Box>
}
```

### DefaultScreen

```tsx
{
  name: 'DefaultScreen',
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
        This box is visible on tablet and desktop (≥ 768px):
      </Typography>
      <DefaultScreen>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 2,
        bgcolor: 'info.light',
        borderRadius: 1
      }}>Default (non-mobile) content (visible ≥ 768px)</Box>
      </DefaultScreen>
    </Box>
}
```

### TabletScreen

```tsx
{
  name: 'TabletScreen',
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
        This box is only visible between 768px and 991px (tablet):
      </Typography>
      <TabletScreen>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 2,
        bgcolor: 'secondary.light',
        borderRadius: 1
      }}>Tablet content (visible 768–991px)</Box>
      </TabletScreen>
    </Box>
}
```

### DesktopScreen

```tsx
{
  name: 'DesktopScreen',
  render: () => <Box sx={{
    p: 2
  }}>
      <Typography variant="body2" color="text.secondary" gutterBottom>
        This box is only visible at 992px and above (desktop):
      </Typography>
      <DesktopScreen>
        <Box sx={{
        p: 2,
        bgcolor: 'success.light',
        borderRadius: 1
      }}>Desktop content (visible ≥ 992px)</Box>
      </DesktopScreen>
    </Box>
}
```

# Feedback Colors

## 

Color can support comprehension of charts and other data visualizations. Data visualization is the representation of information in pictorial or graphical format, such as charts, graphs, maps, and diagrams. These visuals aid in the communication of complex data so that insights can be more easily drawn. Because color can affect our perception of information, the appropriate use of color is critical in making a data visualization successful.

Name

Swatches

Error

<file path="foundations-icons.txt">
# Icons

Icons are visual symbols used to represent functions, content, and concepts at a glance. Our design system uses Material Design icons in the **Outlined variant** exclusively for consistency and clarity.

## Design Guidelines

### Icon Variant Standard

**Always use Outlined icons** - This is our design system's standard. Outlined icons provide:

-   Consistent visual weight across the interface
-   Better readability at smaller sizes
-   Modern, clean aesthetic
-   Optimal accessibility contrast

### Icon Sizes

Icons should be used in the following standard sizes:

-   **Small**: 16px - For inline text, compact spaces
-   **Medium**: 24px - Default size for most UI elements
-   **Large**: 32px - For headers, primary actions
-   **Extra Large**: 48px+ - For empty states, illustrations

## Usage Examples

### Common Navigation Icons

HomeOutlined

PersonOutlined

SettingsOutlined

SearchOutlined

### Action Icons

AddOutlined

EditOutlined

DeleteOutlined

ShareOutlined

### Status & Feedback Icons

CheckCircleOutlined

Success

WarningOutlined

Warning

ErrorOutlined

Error

InfoOutlined

Info

## Implementation

### Import Icons

When using icons in your components, import them from the icons-material module:

```typescript
import DarkModeOutlined from '@mui/icons-material/DarkModeOutlined';
import LightModeOutlined from '@mui/icons-material/LightModeOutlined';
```

### Basic Usage

```typescript
import { IconButton } from '@metris/ui/inputs/icon-button';
import SettingsOutlined from '@mui/icons-material/SettingsOutlined';
const SettingsButton = () => (
  <IconButton>
    <SettingsOutlined />
  </IconButton>
);
```

### Sizing Icons

Use the `sx` prop to control icon size:

```typescript
<HomeOutlined sx={{ fontSize: 16 }} />  {/* Small */}
<HomeOutlined sx={{ fontSize: 24 }} />  {/* Medium (default) */}
<HomeOutlined sx={{ fontSize: 32 }} />  {/* Large */}
<HomeOutlined sx={{ fontSize: 48 }} />  {/* Extra Large */}
```

### Icon Colors

Icons inherit text color by default, but can be customized:

```typescript
<ErrorOutlined sx={{ color: 'error.main' }} />
<CheckCircleOutlined sx={{ color: 'success.main' }} />
<WarningOutlined sx={{ color: 'warning.main' }} />
<InfoOutlined sx={{ color: 'info.main' }} />
```

## Best Practices

### Do ✅

-   **Always use Outlined variants** for consistency
-   Use semantic colors (error.main for error icons, success.main for success icons)
-   Maintain consistent sizing within the same context
-   Provide proper accessibility labels when icons convey meaning
-   Use icons to enhance, not replace, clear text labels

### Don't ❌

-   Don't mix icon variants (Filled, TwoTone, etc.) with Outlined
-   Don't use custom colors that don't follow the design system
-   Don't use icons without proper context or labels for screen readers
-   Don't scale icons beyond their intended size ranges

## Accessibility

When using icons, consider accessibility:

```typescript
// For decorative icons
<HomeOutlined aria-hidden="true" />
// For meaningful icons, provide labels
<IconButton aria-label="Delete item">
  <DeleteOutlined />
</IconButton>
// Or use title attribute
<SearchOutlined titleAccess="Search" />
```

## Available Icons

The complete set of Material Design Outlined icons is available through our icons-material module. For a full list of available icons, visit the [Material-UI Icons documentation](https://mui.com/material-ui/material-icons/).

Remember: **Always use the Outlined variant** to maintain design system consistency.
</file>

# Metris Web UI

`@andritzot/metris-web-ui` is the shared React component library for Metris applications, built on top of Material-UI v7. It is the source of truth for every Metris UI element — visual language, interaction patterns, accessibility, and theming all flow through this library.

This Storybook is the canonical reference for every public component: API surface, visual variants, real-world usage, and accessibility notes.

## What the library provides

-   The **Metris design system as an MUI theme** — palette, typography scale, spacing, elevation, and dark mode wired through CSS variables.
-   **Wrapped MUI components** with Metris-specific styling and narrowed prop unions (e.g. restricted `color` palettes that match Andritz brand tokens).
-   **Custom Metris components** that have no MUI equivalent: project tree-view, app platform shell, tag selector, PDF viewer, language selector, layout templates, rich-text editor wrappers, map/canvas/react-flow integrations.
-   **Layout templates and navigation shells** matching the Metris UX guidelines (two/three/four/five-panel templates, app platform layout, navigation drawers).
-   **Locale resources and shared providers** — `MetrisUIProvider` aggregates theme, snackbar context, MUI X locale text, and other shared providers in a single mount.
-   **Tree-shakable subpath exports** — apps import only the components they use; the library publishes one entry per component family.

## Installation

```bash

```

`@mui/icons-material` is the only peer your app needs to add explicitly — all icon usage in this library accepts MUI icon components from there. Other peer dependencies (`@mui/material`, `@emotion/react`, `@emotion/styled`, `@mui/system`, …) are auto-resolved by pnpm.

> **Never import from `@mui/material` directly in app code.** Doing so bypasses the Metris design system: you lose the curated color palette, the prop restrictions, and the visual conventions this library enforces. Always import from `@andritzot/metris-web-ui/*` — if a component you need isn't exposed yet, request it via a PR on this repo rather than reaching into `@mui/material` from app code.

## Setup

Wrap your app root in `MetrisUIProvider`. It supplies theme (light/dark), MUI X locale text, the snackbar provider, and the rest of the shared context:

```tsx

```

Load the design-system stylesheet once at app entry (alongside the provider):

```tsx

```

This file ships the Metris fonts, the CSS variables that drive the theme, and base resets. Without it, MUI components will render with default Material colors and typography.

## Importing components

Always import from the leaf subpath of the component family:

```tsx

```

The library is organized in the same categories MUI itself uses, with two Metris-specific additions (`metris/*` and `mui-x/*` premium widgets):

| Category | What's in it | |---|---| | **inputs** | Button, Checkbox, Radio, Select, TextField, Autocomplete, Slider, Switch, search field, password field, language selector, … | | **data-display** | Avatar, Badge, Chip, Divider, Icon, Image, List, Tooltip, Typography, NestedListTreeview, ProjectTreeview, PDF viewer, … | | **feedback** | Alert, Backdrop, Dialog, ConfirmDialog, Modal, Snackbar (with Metris severity variants), progress, skeleton | | **surfaces** | Accordion, Card, Paper, AppBar | | **navigation** | Breadcrumbs, Drawer, Menu family, MobileStepper, Stepper, Tabs, side-navigation | | **layout** | Box, Stack, Grid, Container, AppLayout, MetrisAppBar, content/sidebar shells, panel templates (two/three/four/five) | | **mui-x** | DataGrid (with Metris compound toolbar), date/time pickers, range pickers, tree-view premium widgets | | **metris** | Components with no MUI counterpart: tag selector, app platform shell, AppCard | | **utils** | ClickAwayListener, drag-and-drop primitives, popper/popover, transition wrappers, media-query hook | | **theme & foundations** | Palette, typography scale, design tokens, CSS variables |

## Recommended ESLint setup

This library publishes an ESLint preset that enforces the no-direct-MUI-imports rule shown above. Spread it into your app's flat config:

```js

```

Once enabled, ESLint will fail on `import { Button } from '@mui/material'` (and the Emotion / `@mui/system` equivalents) and point you to the correct `@andritzot/metris-web-ui/*` subpath.

## Theming

Every color, spacing, and typography token flows through the MUI theme. App code should pull from the theme rather than hard-coding values:

```tsx

```

The library ships both light and dark themes wired through CSS variables — toggling the data attribute on `<html>` switches the entire surface automatically. Use the theme toggle in this Storybook's top toolbar to preview either mode.

## Versioning

The library is released via `semantic-release` on each merge to `main`. Versioning follows SemVer; breaking changes increment the major. Conventional Commit messages drive the version bump and CHANGELOG generation — see the commit log for the rationale of each release.

-   **UI/UX guidelines** — [Confluence](https://andritz.atlassian.net/wiki/x/g4C2T)
